# Rashi on I Kings

רש"י על מלכים א

Link: [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings)

הפירוש הנפוץ והמוכר ביותר לתורה, המבאר את פשטי המקראות בתוספת הרחבות פרשניות; חובר במאה ה-11.

Most widely-read biblical commentary, compiled in the 11th-century, explaining the simple meaning of the text with interpretive elaborations.

Sections: Chapter-Verse-Comment

Location: Tanakh/Rishonim on Tanakh/Rashi/Prophets

Originally from [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org) via [TorahLM.org](https://torahlm.org) — see [licensing](https://torahlm.org/sefaria_license).

Version (He): On Your Way -- new (Segments: 630, License: Public Domain)

Version (En): The Metsudah Tanach series, Lakewood, N.J (Segments: 629)


## Chapter 1


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:1:1)


**וְלֹא יִחַם לוֹ.** אָמְרוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ: כָּל הַמְבַזֶּה בְּגָדִים, אֵינוֹ נֶהֶנָה מֵהֶם לַסוֹף, לְפִי שֶׁקָּרַע אֶת כְּנַף הַמְּעִיל לְשָׁאוּל. וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה: אָמַר רַב שְׁמוּאֵל בַּר נַחֲמָנִי: כְּשֶׁרָאָה דָוִד אֶת הַמַּלְאָךְ עוֹמֵד בִּירוּשָׁלַיִם וְחַרְבּוֹ בְּיָדוֹ, נִצְטַנֵּן דָּמוֹ מִיִּרְאָתוֹ.


**But he was not warmed.** Our Rabbis said, “He who disgraces clothing will ultimately be deprived of their pleasures.”¹ [Dovid’s clothing did not warm him] because he tore off the corner of Shaul’s robe.² The Midrash [Aggadah states], Rabbi Shmuel son of Nachmeni said, that as Dovid saw the angel standing in Yerusholayim with his sword drawn in his hand, his blood became cold from fear.³

footnotes:
¹ Maseches Berachos 62b.
² See I Shmuel 24:5.
³ That terror of that experience remained with Dovid, and from then on he could never again find warmth.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:2:1)


**בְתוּלָה.** בְּתוּלֶיהָ מְחַמְּמִין אֶת בְּשָׂרָהּ.


**Virgin girl.** Her virginity warms her flesh.


###### Rashi on I Kings 1:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:2:2)


**סֹכֶנֶת.** מְחַמֶּמֶת, וְכֵן: וּבוֹקֵעַ עֵצִים יִסָּכֶן בָּם.


**A warmer.** “A warmer,”⁴ and similarly, “and he who chops wood will be warmed [יסכן] by them.”⁵

footnotes:
⁴ Koheles 10:9.
⁵ Alternatively, סכנת means that she should be “near” to him.—Targum



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:4:1)


**לֹא יְדָעָהּ.** שֶׁהַבְּתוּלָה יָפָה לְחַמֵּם מִן הַבְּעוּלָה. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ: מִשּׁוּם לֹא יַרְבֶּה לּוֹ נָשִׁים, וּכְבָר הָיוּ לוֹ שְׁמוֹנָה עָשָׂר.


**Was not intimate with her.** Because a virgin is more suitable for [the purpose] of warming than a non-virgin.⁶ But our Rabbis said [he was not intimate with her] because “He shall not have too many wives,”⁷ and he already had eighteen.⁸

footnotes:
⁶ He therefore preferred that she remain a virgin.
⁷ Devarim 17:17. See Rashi there.
⁸ I.e., because Dovid already had eighteen wives, the maximum permitted for a king, he therefore did not marry her. See Maseches Sanhedrin 22a and Rashi there.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:5:1)


**מִתְנַשֵּׂא.** מִתְפָּאֵר.


**Exalted himself.** He elevated himself.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ The literal meaning is, “lifted himself.”


###### Rashi on I Kings 1:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:5:2)


**וַחֲמִשִּׁים אִישׁ.** נְטוּלֵי טְחוֹל וַחֲקוּקֵי כַּפּוֹת רַגְלַיִם.


**And fifty men.** Whose spleens had been removed, and whose soles of their feet had been carved.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ See Maseches Sanhedrin 21b and Rashi there.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:6:1)


**וְלֹא עֲצָבוֹ.** לֹא הִכְעִיסוֹ, לִמֶּדְךָ שֶׁהַמּוֹנֵעַ תּוֹכָחָה מִבְּנוֹ, מְבִיאוֹ לִידֵי מִיתָה.


**Had never angered him.** He did not anger him.¹¹ This [episode] teaches you that he who refrains from rebuking his child causes his death.¹²

footnotes:
¹¹ Throughout his life, Dovid had never gotten angry at Adoniyahu or punished him when he did something bad. Adoniyahu therefore incorrectly assumed that his father would not reprimand him for proclaiming himself king.
¹² See Mishlei 13:24.


###### Rashi on I Kings 1:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:6:2)


**וְגַם הוּא טוֹב תֹּאַר.** כְּאַבְשָׁלוֹם, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וּכְאַבְשָׁלוֹם לֹא הָיָה אִישׁ יָפֶה, הִיא גָּרְמָה לָהֶם שֶׁנִּתְגָּאוּ.


**And he was also very handsome.** [He was as handsome] as Avsholom,¹³ about whom it is written, “And as Avsholom there was no one as handsome.”¹⁴ This caused them to be proud.

footnotes:
¹³ “He too,” infers that there was another individual who was also very handsome.
¹⁴ II Shmuel 14:25.


###### Rashi on I Kings 1:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:6:3)


**וְאֹתוֹ יָלְדָה.** אִמּוֹ.


**She gave birth to him.** [“She” refers to] his mother.


###### Rashi on I Kings 1:6:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:6:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:6:4)


**אַחֲרֵי אַבְשָׁלוֹם.** כְּלוֹמַר גִּדְּלַתּוֹ אַחַר תַּרְבּוּת שֶׁגִּדְלָה אִמּוֹ שֶׁל אַבְשָׁלוֹם.


**After Avsholom.** I.e., his mother reared him in the same spoiled manner that Avsholom’s mother reared him.¹⁵ Avsholom” because it implies that she bore both Adoniyahu and Avsholom, which is not true, for Avsholom’s mother was Ma’acha, and Adoniyahu’s mother was Chagis.

footnotes:
¹⁵ Rashi does not translate “after



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:7:1)


**עִם יוֹאָב בֶּן צְרוּיָה.** לְפִי שֶׁהָיָה יוֹדֵעַ שֶׁבְּלִבּוֹ שֶׁל דָּוִד עָלָיו, עַל שֶׁהָרַג אֶת אַבְנֵר וַעֲמָשָׂא וְאַבְשָׁלוֹם, וְסוֹפוֹ שֶׁיְּצַוֶּה אֶת בְּנוֹ הַמּוֹלֵךְ תַּחְתָּיו עָלָיו, לְפִיכָךְ הָיָה רוֹצֶה שֶׁיִּמְלֹךְ זֶה עַל יָדוֹ, וְיֶאֱהָבֶנּוּ.


**With Yoav ben Tseruyah.** Because he knew that Dovid bore a grudge against him for having killed Avner, Amasa, and Avsholom; and that he would eventually charge his son, who would reign in his place, to deal with him. He [Yoav] therefore wanted that this one [Adoniyahu] should reign through his assistance so that he would favor him.


###### Rashi on I Kings 1:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:7:2)


**וְעִם אֶבְיָתָר הַכֹּהֵן.** שֶׁנִּסְתַּלֵּק מִן הַכְּהֻנָּה מִשֶּׁבָּרַח דָּוִד מִירוּשָׁלַיִם מִפְּנֵי אַבְשָׁלוֹם, שֶׁשָּׁאַל בָּאוּרִים וְתֻמִּים וְלֹא עָלְתָה לּוֹ, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וַיַּעַל אֶבְיָתָר, וְהָיָה מִבְּנֵי בָּנָיו שֶׁל עֵלִי, וְיָדַע שֶׁלֹּא יְשַׁמֵּשׁ בִּימֵי שְׁלֹמֹה, שֶׁהֲרֵי נֶאֱמַר לְעֵלִי: וַהֲקִימֹתִי לִי כֹּהֵן נֶאֱמָן וְהִתְהַלֵּךְ לִפְנֵי מְשִׁיחִי, וְהָיָה חָפֵץ שֶׁיַּעֲמֹד זֶה עַל יָדוֹ.


**And with Evyosor the *kohein*.** He was dismissed from the priesthood at the time when Dovid escaped from Yerusholayim on account of Avsholom, when he [Evyosor] consulted the *Urim* and *Tumim* and was unsuccessful as it is stated, “And Evyosor went up.”¹⁶ He [Evyosor] was a descendant of Eili. And he knew that he would not serve in the days of Shlomo, for it was stated to Eili, “And I will appoint for Myself a faithful *kohein*... and he will serve before My anointed one...”¹⁷ He [Evyosor] wanted that this one [Adoniyah become king] through his assistance.¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁶ II Shmuel 15:24.
¹⁷ I Shmuel 2:35.
¹⁸ So that Adoniyahu would listen to him when he became king and would restore him to his previous position of Kohein Gadol.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:8:1)


**וְנָתָן הַנָּבִיא.** שֶׁנִּבֵּא לְדָוִד שֶׁשְּׁלֹמֹה יִמְלֹךְ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים: וּשְׁלֹמֹה יִהְיֶה שְׁמוֹ.


**And Noson the prophet.** Who prophesied to Dovid that Shlomo would reign, as it is stated in Divrei Hayomim, “ his name will be Shlomo.”¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁹ I Divrei Hayomim 22:9. The verse reads, “Behold a son will be born to you, he will be a man of rest, and I will give him rest from all his enemies around, his name will be Shlomo.”



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:9:1)


**וּמְרִיא.** שׁוֹר שֶׁל פְּטָם.


**And fattened [oxen].** Oxen which are [fattened by being] fed forcefully.


###### Rashi on I Kings 1:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:9:2)


**אֶבֶן הַזֹּחֶלֶת.** אֶבֶן גְּדוֹלָה, שֶׁהָיוּ הַבַּחוּרִים מְנַסִּין בָּהּ אֶת כֹּחָם לַהֲזִיזָהּ וּלְגָרְרָהּ, לְשׁוֹן מַיִם זוֹחֲלִין: זוֹחֲלֵי עָפָר. וְיוֹנָתָן תִּרְגֵּם: אֶבֶן סְכוּתָא, שֶׁעוֹמְדִין עָלֶיהָ וְצוֹפִין לְמֵרָחוֹק.


**The stone of Zocheles.** A huge stone used by the young men to test their strength by moving or dragging it. An expression similar to “moving [זוחלין]”²⁰ water, or, “those that creep [זוחלי] on the earth.”²¹ [Targum] Yonoson rendered [אבן זוחלת as] אבן סכותא [=a lookout stone], upon which people stand and look far into the distance.

footnotes:
²⁰ See Misnayoy Mikvaos 5:4,5.
²¹ Devarim 32:24.


###### Rashi on I Kings 1:9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:9:3)


**עֵין רֹגֵל.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: עֵין קַצְרָא, הוּא כּוֹבֵס, שֶׁמְּתַקֵּן בִּגְדֵי צֶמֶר בְּרַגְלָיו, עַל יְדֵי בְּעִיטָה.


**Ein-Rogel.** [Targum] Yonoson rendered [עין רוגל as] עין קצרא, which is [a well]²² used by a clothes washer who would press the woolen clothes with his feet [=רגליו], by trampling [on them].

footnotes:
²² It was customary to anoint kings near a well or another source of running water to symbolize continuity and prolonged rule. See Rashi 1:33 below.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:10:1)


**וְאֶת שְׁלֹמֹה אָחִיו לֹא קָרָא.** שֶׁיּוֹדֵעַ הָיָה, שֶׁהִתְנַבֵּא עָלָיו הַנָּבִיא לִמְלֹךְ.


**And his brother Shlomo, he did not invite.** For he knew that the prophet had prophesied about him that he would reign.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:12:1)


**וּמַלְּטִי אֶת נַפְשֵׁךְ.** מִן הַמַּחֲלֹקֶת לְאַחַר מוֹת הַמֶּלֶךְ, שֶׁיִּרְצֶה בְּנֵךְ לִמְלֹךְ, כְּמָה שֶׁהִבְטִיחוֹ הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא.


**And you will save your life.** From the rivalry which will follow the death of the king, for your son will wish to reign, as the Holy One Blessed Is He, had promised him.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:21:1)


**חַטָּאִים.** חֲסֵרִים וּמְנוּעִין מִן הַגְּדֻלָּה, כְּמוֹ: אֶל הַשַּׂעֲרָה וְלֹא יַחֲטִא.


**Sinners.** [They will be] lacking²³ and restrained from any greatness,²⁴ as in, “[Sling a stone] at a hair, and not miss [יחטיא].”²⁵

footnotes:
²³ Literally, חטאים means “offenders.”
²⁴ Alternatively, חטאים means “banished.”—Targum, or will be treated as sinners.
²⁵ Shoftim 20:16.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:24:1)


**אַתָּה אָמַרְתָּ.** בִּתְמִיָּה.


**Did you say.** In the interrogative.²⁶

footnotes:
²⁶ I.e., “Did you say that Adoniyahu will reign after me?” Literally, “אמרת” means, “you said” [in the affirmative.]



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:25:1)


**כִּי יָרַד הַיּוֹם.** שֶׁהָעִיר הָיְתָה גְּבוֹהָה, וְאֶבֶן הַזּוֹחֶלֶת לְמַטָּה בַּגַּיְא.


**For he has gone down this very day.** The city was situated on higher ground and the אבן הזוחלת was below in a valley.



### Verse 33

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:33:1)


**עַל הַפִּרְדָּה אֲשֶׁר לִי.** הוּא סִימָן לוֹ שֶׁיִּמְלֹךְ, וּתְחִלַּת הַגְּדֻלָּה, שֶׁאֵין הֶדְיוֹט רוֹכֵב עַל סוּסוֹ שֶׁל מֶלֶךְ.


**On my personal mule.** This will be a sign that he will reign and the beginning of [his] greatness, because a commoner is forbidden to ride on the king’s horse.


###### Rashi on I Kings 1:33:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:33:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:33:2)


**אֶל גִּחוֹן.** הוּא מַעְיַן הַשִּׁלּוֹחַ, מִכָּאן שֶׁאֵין מוֹשְׁחִין אֶת הַמְּלָכִים אֶלָּא עַל הַמַּעְיָן, סִימָן שֶׁתִּמָּשֵׁךְ מַלְכוּתוֹ.


**To Gichon.** [Gichon] is the well of Shilo’ach. From this [episode is derived the custom] not to anoint the kings except at the well, to symbolize thereby that his kingship shall continue on and on.²⁷

footnotes:
²⁷ As the waters of a well do.



### Verse 34

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:34:1)


**וּמָשַׁח אֹתוֹ.** נוֹתֵן לוֹ שֶׁמֶן בֵּין רִיסֵי עֵינָיו, כְּמִין נֵזֶר.


**And he shall be anointed.** He places the oil between his eyelids in the form of a crown.²⁸

footnotes:
²⁸ See Maseches Horiyos 12a.



### Verse 36

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:36:1)


**אָמֵן.** שֶׁיִּחְיֶה שְׁלֹמֹה.


**Amein.** [Long] live Shlomo.²⁹

footnotes:
²⁹ “Amein” serves as an affirmation to this proclamation.



### Verse 37

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:37:1)


**מִכִּסֵּא אֲדֹנִי הַמֶּלֶךְ.** מִכָּאן שֶׁאֵין אָדָם מִתְקַנֵּא בִּבְנוֹ.


**Than the throne of my lord Dovid Hamelech.** From this [we derive] that a father is not envious of his son.³⁰

footnotes:
³⁰ See Maseches Sanhedrin 105b.



### Verse 38

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:38:1)


**וְהַכְּרֵתִי וְהַפְּלֵתִי.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: וְקַשָּׁתַיָּא וְקַלָּעַיָּא. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ: אוּרִים וְתֻמִּים.


**And the archers and the sling shooters.** [Targum] Yonoson rendered, “the archers and the sling shooters.”³¹ But our Rabbis maintained [that it refers to] the *Urim* and *Tumim*.³²

footnotes:
³¹ Alternatively, כרתי ופלתי refers to the Sanhedrin.—Ralbag
³² The Urim and Tumim are called “כרתי ופלתי”; כרתי because their words are “cut” [כרת], i.e., they render irrevocable decisions and פלתי because they are “wondrous” [פלא] in revealing the future. See Maseches Berachos 4a.



### Verse 39

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:39:1)


**אֶת קֶרֶן הַשֶּׁמֶן.** מִשֶּׁמֶן הַמִּשְׁחָה שֶׁעָשָׂה משֶׁה.


**The horn of oil.** [Tzadok took] of the anointment oil compounded by Moshe.³³

footnotes:
³³ See Shemos 30, 31.


###### Rashi on I Kings 1:39:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:39:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:39:2)


**מִן הָאֹהֶל.** שֶׁהָאָרוֹן נָתוּן בְּעִיר דָּוִד, וּצְלוֹחִית שֶׁל שֶׁמֶן הַמִּשְׁחָה לִפְנֵי הָאָרוֹן (יומא נב ב).


**From the Tent.** Since the Holy Ark was in the City of Dovid [Yerusholayim], and the jug of oil of anointment was before the Ark. as we have learned in [Maseches] Yoma.³⁴

footnotes:
³⁴ 52b.



### Verse 40

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:40:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:40:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:40:1)


**בַּחֲלִלִים.** פלאוט"י בְּלַעַ"ז (חָלִיל).


**The flutes.** *Flute* in O.F.


###### Rashi on I Kings 1:40:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:40:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:40:2)


**וַתִּבָּקַע הָאָרֶץ.** דִּבְּרוּ הַנְּבִיאִים בִּלְשׁוֹן הֲבַאי, כִּלְשׁוֹן בְּנֵי אָדָם.


**And the earth [seemed to] split.** Prophets spoke in terms of exaggeration as [is common] among people.³⁵

footnotes:
³⁵ see Maseches Chulin 90b.



### Verse 43

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:43:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:43:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:43:1)


**אֲבָל אֲדֹנֵינוּ.** כְּלוֹמַר, עַכְשָׁיו אֵין בְּשׂוֹרָתִי טוֹבָה לְךָ.


**But our lord.** I.e., “Now, my report is not of good [tidings] for you.”



### Verse 50

###### Rashi on I Kings 1:50:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:50:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/1:50:1)


**בְּקַרְנוֹת הַמִּזְבֵּחַ.** שֶׁהָיָה בְּגִבְעוֹן, אָמַר: הֲרוּגֵי בֵּית דִּין נִקְבָּרִין בְּקִבְרֵי בֵּית דִּין, אָמוּת כָּאן, וְאֶקָּבֵר בְּקִבְרֵי אֲבוֹתַי (תנחומא מסעי יב).


**Of the horns [of the corners] of the Altar.** Which was in Givon.³⁶ He said, “Those who are executed by Beis Din are buried in the graves of Beis Din. I will die here, and be buried in the graves of my forefathers.” Thus did Rabbi Tanchuma expound.

footnotes:
³⁶ Alternatively, he grabbed on to the corners of the altar that was in Yerushalayim.—Radak. In those days it was permissible to build an altar and bring certain sacrifices outside of the Mishkan.



## Chapter 2


### Verse 5

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:5:1)


**אֶת אֲשֶׁר עָשָׂה לִי.** שֶׁהֶרְאָה הַסֵּפֶר שֶׁשָּׁלַח דָּוִד בְּיַד אוּרִיָּה. (במדרש רבי תנחומא שם)


**[What] was done to me.** By showing them the letter which Dovid had sent through Uryah,¹ [according to] the Midrash Rabbi Tanchuma.

footnotes:
¹ Uryah was one of Dovid’s generals. Dovid wanted Uryah killed so that he could marry Bas Sheva, Uryah’s wife. With Yoav’s assistance, he arranged to have Uryah placed in the front lines of the battle. When Uryah was killed, everyone blamed Yoav. Instead of protecting Dovid, Yoav betrayed him by disclosing that it was Dovid’s plan to place Uryah in the thick of the battle in order that he be killed.


###### Rashi on I Kings 2:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:5:2)


**דְּמֵי מִלְחָמָה בְּשָׁלֹם.** שֶׁהָיָה לָהֶם שָׁלוֹם עִמּוֹ, וְלֹא הָיוּ נִשְׁמָרִים מִמֶּנּוּ.


**[And shed] the blood of war at a time of peace.** Since they were at peace with him, they were not on guard against him.


###### Rashi on I Kings 2:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:5:3)


**בַּחֲגֹרָתוֹ אֲשֶׁר בְּמָתְנָיו.** שֶׁחָגַר חַרְבּוֹ מְצֻמֶּדֶת עַל מָתְנָיו, שֶׁלֹּא כְּדֶרֶךְ הַחוֹגְרִים, בְּצֵאתוֹ לִקְרַאת עֲמָשָׂא, וּפִיהָ לְמַטָּה כְּדֵי שֶׁתִּפֹּל, כְּמָה שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וְהוּא יָצָא וַתִּפֹּל, וּכְשֶׁנָּפְלָה וּנְטָלָהּ, כְּסָבוּר עֲמָשָׂא שֶׁלֹּא נְטָלָהּ אֶלָּא לְהַגְבִּיהָהּ מִן הָאָרֶץ, וְלֹא נִשְׁמַר מֵהַחֶרֶב אֲשֶׁר בְּיַד יוֹאָב.


**On his belt which was on his loins.** He had girded his sword, attached to his loins, not in the customary manner of those girding swords, when he went out to meet Amasa. [He placed the sword’s] opening downward so that it would fall out, as it is stated, “and he went out and it fell out.”² Once it fell, he [Yo’av] retrieved it. Amasa presumed that he retrieved it only to pick it up from the ground, and he did not guard himself from the sword which was in Yo’av’s hand.

footnotes:
² II Shmuel 20:8.


###### Rashi on I Kings 2:5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:5:4)


**וּבְנַעֲלוֹ אֲשֶׁר בְּרַגְלָיו.** אֶת אַבְנֵר הָרַג בְּעָרְמָה, שֶׁשְּׁאָלוֹ גִּדֶּמֶת הֵיאָךְ חוֹלֶצֶת, כְּמָה שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וַיַּטֵּהוּ יוֹאָב אֶל תּוֹךְ הַשַּׁעַר לְדַבֵּר אִתּוֹ 'בַשֶּׁלִי', לְשׁוֹן 'שַׁל' נְעָלֶיךָ.


**And in his shoe that was on his feet.** He killed Avner cunningly by asking him, “How does a woman who has no hands remove the shoes?”³ as it is stated, “And Yo’av caused him to turn aside at the gate to speak with him בשלי.”⁴ [בשלי is] an expression of “remove [של] your shoes.”⁵

footnotes:
³ During חליצה, the widow must remove the shoe of her deceased husband’s brother who does not want to marry her.
⁴ II Shmuel 3:27.
⁵ Shemos 3:5.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:6:1)


**וְלֹא תוֹרֵד שֵׂיבָתוֹ בְּשָׁלֹם שְׁאֹל.** אַל תַּנִּיחֵהוּ לָמוּת מִיתַת עַצְמוֹ וְלִפֹּל בְּגֵיהִנֹּם.


**And do not let his greying head go down to the grave in peace.** Do not let him die a natural death [causing him] to fall into *Gehinnom*.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Dovid had Yoav’s ultimate welfare in mind.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:8:1)


**קְלָלָה נִמְרֶצֶת.** מְפֹרֶשֶׁת, כְּמוֹ: מַה יַּמְרִיצְךָ; (שם ו כה): וּמַה נִּמְרְצוּ, כְּמוֹ מַה נִּמְלְצוּ.


**An explicit curse.** [נמרצת⁷ means] concise, as in, “what will bring clarity [ימריצך] to you,”⁸ [and as in,] “and how clear [נמרצו are right words],”⁹ the same as an interpretation [נמלצו]¹⁰.

footnotes:
⁷ נמרצת is an acronym for the five ways that Shim’i cursed Dovid. He called Dovid an adulterer [=נואף], a Moavi [=מואבי], murderer [=רוצח], oppressor [=צורר], and an abomination [=תועבה]. See Maseches Shabbos105a.
⁸ Iyov 16:3.
⁹ Ibid. 6:25.
¹⁰ נמלצו is the equivalent of נמרצו because the ‘ר’ and the ‘ל’ are interchangeable.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:9:1)


**כִּי אִישׁ חָכָם אָתָּה.** תֵּן לִבְּךָ לִמְצֹא לוֹ עֲוֹן מָוֶת בִּשְׁבִיל דָּבָר אַחֵר, וְזֶהוּ: וְיָדַעְתָּ אֵת אֲשֶׁר תַּעֲשֶׂה לּוֹ.


**For you are a wise man.** Turn your attention to find for him a capital iniquity on account of another matter. This is [what it means by], “You will know how to deal with him.”



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:12:1)


**וַתִּכֹּן מַלְכֻתוֹ מְאֹד.** אַף עַל הָעֶלְיוֹנִים.


**And his kingdom was firmly established.** [He was] also [ruler] over the heavenly creatures.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:19:1)


**לְדַבֶּר לוֹ.** לְדַבֵּר בִּשְׁבִילוֹ, בִּשְׁבִיל אֲדוֹנִיָּהוּ.


**To speak to him.** To speak on his behalf, on behalf of Adoniyahu.¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ Alternatively, on behalf of Shlomo, because she felt that Shlomo’s kingdom would be strengthened by granting Adoniyahu’s request.—Malbim.


###### Rashi on I Kings 2:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:19:2)


**לְאֵם הַמֶּלֶךְ.** אָמְרוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ (בבא בתרא צא ב): לְאִמָּהּ שֶׁל מַלְכוּת, הִיא רוּת.


**For the mother of the king.** Our Rabbis said [this refers] to Rus the matriarch of kingship.¹²

footnotes:
¹² See Maseches Bava Basra 91b and Rashi there. Rus was the great great grandmother of Shlomo who was still alive when Shlomo ascended the throne.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:22:1)


**וְשַׁאֲלִי לוֹ אֶת הַמְּלוּכָה.** מִשֶּׁיִּשְׁתַּמֵּשׁ בְּשַׁרְבִיטוֹ שֶׁל מֶלֶךְ, הוּא תְּחִלַּת הַשְּׂרָרָה.


**Ask that the kingdom be given to him.** From the moment the scepter of the king is used, that is the beginning of authority.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:24:1)


**עָשָׂה לִי בַּיִת.** בֵּית הַמְּלוּכָה, כַּאֲשֶׁר דִּבֵּר בְּיַד נָתָן.


**And Who has made me a [royal] house.** A house of royalty as he had spoken through Noson.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:26:1)


**עֲנָתֹת לֵךְ.** מִשָּׁם הָיָה.


**Go to Anosos.** He was from there.


###### Rashi on I Kings 2:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:26:2)


**כִּי אִישׁ מָוֶת אָתָּה.** שֶׁמָּרַדְתָּ בְּמַלְכוּת אָבִי בִּהְיוֹתְךָ עִם אֲדוֹנִיָּהוּ, לְהַמְלִיכוֹ שֶׁלֹּא מִדַּעְתּוֹ.


**For you are deserving of death.** Because you rebelled against the kingdom of my father when you were with Adoniyahu, to appoint him king without his [i.e., my father’s] knowledge.


###### Rashi on I Kings 2:26:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:26:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:26:3)


**כִּי נָשָׂאתָ אֶת אֲרוֹן.** בִּבְרֹחַ אָבִי מִפְּנֵי אַבְשָׁלוֹם.


**Because you carried the Ark.** When my father was escaping from Avsholom.


###### Rashi on I Kings 2:26:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:26:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:26:4)


**וְכִי הִתְעַנִּיתָ.** עִמּוֹ בְּאוֹתָהּ הַצָּרָה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: כִּי אָמְרוּ הָעָם רָעֵב וְעָיֵף וְצָמֵא בַּמִּדְבָּר.


**And because you suffered.** With him in that very same affliction, as it is stated, “for they said, ‘the people are hungry and weary and thirsty; in the wilderness.’”¹³

footnotes:
¹³ II Shmuel 17:29.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:27:1)


**אֶת אֲשֶׁר דִּבֶּר עַל בֵּית עֵלִי.** וַהֲקִימֹתִי לִי כֹּהֵן נֶאֱמָן וְגוֹ', וְהִבַּטְתָּ צַר מָעוֹן וְגוֹ', וּמִשֶּׁחָרְבָה שִׁילֹה, לֹא הָיָה בַּיִת חָשׁוּב לַעֲבוֹדָה לְהַקָּבָּ"ה, וְעַתָּה כְּשֶׁיִּבָּנֶה בֵּית הַבְּחִירָה, מִלֵּא הַקָּבָּ"ה אֶת דְּבָרוֹ לְגָרֵשׁ אֶת בְּנֵי עֵלִי.


**That He had spoken concerning the house of Eili.** [As it is stated,] “And I will appoint for Myself a faithful *kohein*,”¹⁴ [and] “and you will see a rival in My dwelling place, etc.”¹⁵ Once [the Tabernacle at] Shilo was destroyed, there was no house worthy of service to the Holy One Blessed Is He, but now that the Beis Hamikdosh will be built, the Holy One Blessed Is He, fulfilled His word to banish Eili’s descendants.¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁴ I Shmuel 2:35.
¹⁵ Ibid. 2:32.
¹⁶ Evyosor was Eili’s great great grandson. See I Shmuel 14:3, 22:20.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:28:1)


**וְהַשְּׁמֻעָה בָּאָה עַד יוֹאָב.** שְׁמוּעַת מִצְוַת דָּוִד שֶׁצִּוָּה לְהָרְגוֹ, וְיָדַע שֶׁשְּׁלֹמֹה שׂוֹנְאוֹ.


**And the report reached Yoav.** The report of Dovid’s charge, that he had commanded to kill him, and he knew that Shlomo hated him.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁷ That is why Yoav feared for his life. Alternatively, he heard of Adoniyahu’s death and of Evyosor’s banishment.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Kings 2:28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:28:2)


**כִּי יוֹאָב נָטָה אַחֲרֵי אֲדֹנִיָּה וְאַחֲרֵי אַבְשָׁלוֹם לֹא נָטָה.** סוֹף דָּבָר, אֲבָל בִּקֵשׁ לִנְטוֹת, אֶלָּא נִתְיָרֵא שֶׁעֲדַיִן לַחְלוּחִית שֶׁל דָּוִד קַיֶּמֶת.


**For Yoav had sided with Adoniyah, but he had not sided with Avsholom.** At the end of the matter; however, he did seek to turn after him, but he was fearful that trace of Dovid[’s might] was still present.



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:30:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר לֹא.** שְׁתַּיִם לֹא אֲקַבֵּל עָלַי, קְלָלוֹת שֶׁקִּלְלַנִי דָּוִד אָבִיךָ בְּמִיתַת אַבְנֵר: יָחוּלוּ עַל רֹאשׁ יוֹאָב, יְקַבְּלֵם שְׁלֹמֹה עָלָיו, וְאַחַר כָּךְ אֵצֵא.


**And he said, “No.”** Two [punishments] I will not accept upon myself. The curses that your father Dovid cursed me at the death of Avner, as it is stated, “They shall fall upon the head of Yo’av.”¹⁸ let Shlomo accept them upon himself, and afterward I will go.

footnotes:
¹⁸ II Shmuel 3:29.



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:31:1)


**עֲשֵׂה כַּאֲשֶׁר דִּבֶּר.** אֲנִי מְקַבְּלָם עָלַי, לְכָךְ נִדְבְּקוּ כָּל אֵלֶּה בְּזֶרַע דָּוִד, עֻזִּיָּה הָיָה מְצֹרָע, אָסָא מַחֲזִיק בַּפֶּלֶךְ, יְהוֹיָקִים נָפַל בַּחֶרֶב, וַחֲסַר לֶחֶם צִדְקִיָּהוּ.


**Do as he said.** I accept them [i.e., the curses] upon myself. Therefore, all these became attached to Dovid’s offspring. Uziyah became [a] leper,¹⁹ Assa leaned on a staff,²⁰ Yehoyakim died by the sword,²¹ and Tzidkiyahu lacked bread.²²

footnotes:
¹⁹ II Divrei Hayomim 26:19.
²⁰ See II Shmuel 3:29 and I Melochim 15:23.
²¹ See Yirmiyahu 22:19.
²² See II Melochim 25:30.



### Verse 34

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:34:1)


**בְּבֵיתוֹ בַּמִּדְבָּר.** שֶׁהָיָה בֵּיתוֹ הֶפְקֵר כַּמִּדְבָּר, לַעֲנִיִּים.


**In his own house in the wilderness.** For his home was unrestricted as a wilderness, to [help] the needy.²³

footnotes:
²³ See Rashi in Maseches Sanhedrin 49a. Alternatively, Yoav had a house in the wilderness where his shepherds stayed when they were out with the sheep, or Yoav house was free of stolen property just like the wilderness.—Radak.



### Verse 36

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:36:1)


**וְלֹא תֵצֵא מִשָּׁם.** כְּדֵי שֶׁיְּהֵא מָצוּי לוֹ, וְלִמְצֹא לוֹ חֵטְא מָוֶת.


**You must never go out from there.** In order that he be accessible to him and that he might find him guilty of a capital sin.



### Verse 42

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:42:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:42:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:42:1)


**וָאָעִד בְּךָ.** לְשׁוֹן הַתְרָאָה.

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:43:1

**אֵת שְׁבֻעַת ה'.** אֲשֶׁר הִשְׁבַּעְתִּיךָ בַּה'.

### Verse 46

###### Rashi on I Kings 2:46:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:46:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/2:46:1)


**וְהַמַּמְלָכָה נָכוֹנָה וְגוֹ'.** כְּלוֹמַר, לֹא נֶעֱנַשׁ עַל כָּל אֵלֶּה.


**The kingdom was thus established, etc.** I.e., he was not punished for all these [actions].



## Chapter 3


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on I Kings 3:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:1:1)


**וַיִּתְחַתֵּן שְׁלֹמֹה.** וְכָל זְמַן שֶׁהָיָה שִׁמְעִי רַבּוֹ קַיָּם, לֹא נִתְחַתֵּן בּוֹ, מִכָּאן אָמְרוּ: לְעוֹלָם יָדוּר אָדָם בִּמְקוֹם רַבּוֹ.


**Shlomo became the son-in-law.** As long as Shim’i, his teacher, was alive, he did not enter into marriage [with Pharaoh’s daughter]. From here, [our Sages derived and] declared, that a person should always live in the vicinity of his teacher.¹²

footnotes:
¹ Based on the juxtaposition of Shimi’s death and Shlomo’s marriage to Pharaoh’s daughter. See Maseches Berachos 8a.
² In order to remain under his teacher’s guidance and influence.


###### Rashi on I Kings 3:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:1:2)


**עַד כַּלֹּתוֹ לִבְנוֹת וְגוֹ'.** וְאַחַר כָּךְ בָּנָה לָהּ בַּיִת.


**Until he finished building, etc.** And afterwards he built a house for her.³

footnotes:
³ Alternatively, after he finished building the Beis Hamikdosh, Shlomo moved Pharaoh’s daughter out of the City of Dovid which had become sanctified by the presence of the Ark.—Radak



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on I Kings 3:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:2:1)


**מְזַבְּחִים בַּבָּמוֹת.** לְשֵׁם שָׁמַיִם, כָּל הָרוֹצֶה בּוֹנֶה מִזְבֵּחַ בְּרֹאשׁ גַּגּוֹ אוֹ בַּחֲצֵרוֹ.


**Sacrificed on improvised altars.** For the sake of Heaven, each [person] who so desired would build an altar on top of his roof or in his yard.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ This was permitted during the period between the destruction of the Mishkan in Shilo and the construction of the Beis Hamikdosh. See Mishnayos Zevachim 14:7.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Kings 3:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:3:1)


**וַיֶּאֱהַב שְׁלֹמֹה לָלֶכֶת בְּחֻקּוֹת דָּוִד אָבִיו.** אַרְבַּע שָׁנִים, עַד שֶׁלֹּא הִתְחִיל לִבְנוֹת הַבַּיִת, אֲבָל מִשֶּׁהִתְחִיל לִבְנוֹת, וַיִּתְחַתֵּן שְׁלֹמֹה אֶת פַּרְעֹה, נִמְצֵאתָ אוֹמֵר: כִּי עַל אַפִּי וְעַל חֲמָתִי הָיְתָה לִּי הָעִיר הַזֹּאת, לְמִן הַיּוֹם אֲשֶׁר בָּנוּ אוֹתָהּ וְעַד הַיּוֹם הַזֶּה, כָּךְ שָׁנִינוּ בְּסֵדֶר עוֹלָם, לָמַדְנוּ שֶׁאֵין הַפָּרָשִׁיּוֹת כְּתוּבוֹת כַּסֵּדֶר.


**And Shlomo loved Adonoy, he conformed to the decrees of his father Dovid.** [During the first] four years, before beginning to build the Beis [Hamikdosh]. But once he began to build and Shlomo married Pharaoh’s daughter, consequently it states, “for as a provocation of anger and of fury has this city has been to me from the day that they built it until this day.”⁵ Thus we learned in Seder Olam. We learn that the passages are not written in chronological order.

footnotes:
⁵ Yirmiyahu 32:31.


###### Rashi on I Kings 3:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:3:2)


**בַּבָּמוֹת הוּא מְזַבֵּחַ.** בִּגְנוּתוֹ דִּבֵּר הַכָּתוּב, שֶׁשָּׁהָה אֶת בִּנְיַן הַבַּיִת אַרְבַּע שָׁנִים.


**Upon improvised altars he brought sacrifices.** The text discusses his dishonor, [this occurred] because he delayed the construction of the Beis [Hamikdosh] four years.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Alternatively, Shlomo desired to attain prophetic revelation by offering sacrifices.—Ralbag



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on I Kings 3:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:4:1)


**הִיא הַבָּמָה הַגְּדוֹלָה.** הוּא מִזְבַּח הַנְּחשֶׁת שֶׁעָשָׂה משֶׁה בַּמִּדְבָּר, וְהֻקְבְּעָה בְּשִׁילֹה, חָרְבָה שִׁילֹה בִּימֵי עֵלִי וּבָא לְנוֹב, חָרְבָה נוֹב בִּימֵי שָׁאוּל, וּבָאוּ לְגִבְעוֹן.


**For that was the great improvised altar.** That was the copper altar which Moshe made in the wilderness,⁷ and it was instituted in Shilo. Shilo was destroyed in Eili’s days, and then it came to Nov. Nov was destroyed in Shaul’s days, and they came to Givon.

footnotes:
⁷ I.e., it was “great” because of its holiness.


###### Rashi on I Kings 3:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:4:2)


**יַעֲלֶה שְׁלֹמֹה.** כְּמוֹ מַעֲלֶה שְׁלֹמֹה.


**Shlomo offered.** Did Shlomo offer.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Literally, יעלה means, “will offer.”


###### Rashi on I Kings 3:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:4:3)


**עַל הַמִּזְבֵּחַ הַהוּא.** בְּיוֹם אֶחָד.


**Upon that altar.** In one day.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Kings 3:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:6:1)


**וַתִּשְׁמָר לוֹ.** לְשׁוֹן מְאַמֵּת אֶת דְּבָרָיו, כְּמוֹ: וְשָׁמַר ה' אֱלֹהֶיךָ לְךָ וְגוֹ', וְאַף כָּאן וַתִּשְׁמָר לוֹ אֶת הַחֶסֶד, הֶאֱמַנְתָּ דְבָרֶיךָ לְקַיֵּם לוֹ אֶת הַחֶסֶד, אֲשֶׁר הִבְטַחְתּוֹ עַל יְדֵי נָתָן הַנָּבִיא.


**You preserved for him.** An expression of authenticating His words as in, “And Adonoy, your God will keep for you, etc.”⁹ Here too You have preserved for him the kindness, You have authenticated Your words to fulfill to him the kindness which You had promised him through Noson the prophet.

footnotes:
⁹ Devarim 7:12.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Kings 3:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:7:1)


**נַעַר קָטֹן.** בֶּן שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה שָׁנָה הָיָה, וְזֶה לְךָ הַחֶשְׁבּוֹן, וַיִּקְרָא שְׁמוֹ יְדִידְיָה, בּוֹ בַּפֶּרֶק עִנָּה אַמְנוֹן אֶת תָּמָר, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וַיְהִי אַחֲרֵי כֵן וּלְאַבְשָׁלוֹם אָחוֹת, מִקֵּץ שְׁנָתַיִם יָמִים וַיִּהְיוּ גֹזְזִים לְאַבְשָׁלוֹם וְהָרַג אֶת אַמְנוֹן, וְאַבְשָׁלוֹם בָּרַח וַיֵּלֶךְ גְּשׁוּרָה, וַיְהִי שָׁם שָׁלשׁ שָׁנִים, הֲרֵי חָמֵשׁ שָׁנִים, וְשָׁב אַבְשָׁלוֹם לִירוּשָׁלַיִם, וַיֵּשֶׁב בִּירוּשָׁלַיִם שְׁנָתַיִם, הֲרֵי שֶׁבַע לִשְׁלֹמֹה, וּמָרַד בְּאָבִיו וְנֶהֱרַג, אַחַר זֹאת: וַיְהִי רָעָב בִּימֵי דָוִד שָׁלשׁ שָׁנִים, הֲרֵי עֶשֶׂר, בְּאַחַת עֶשְׂרֵה מָנָה אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל, וַיָּשׁוּטוּ בְּכָל הָאָרֶץ תִּשְׁעָה חֳדָשִׁים, בִּשְׁנַת מוֹתוֹ תִּקֵּן מִשְׁמָרוֹת, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: בִּשְׁנַת הָאַרְבָּעִים לְמַלְכוּת דָּוִיד נִדְרָשׁוּ, הֲרֵי שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה לַמֶּלֶךְ שְׁלֹמֹה.


**A small lad.** He was twelve years old. And this is how [his age] is calculated: “And he called his name Yedidyah.”¹⁰ At the same time Amnon raped Tamar, as it is stated, “And it was after this, and Avsholom [the son of Dovid] had a sister.”¹¹ At the end of, “two years, and Avsholom had sheep shearers,”¹² and he killed Amnon. Avsholom fled and went to Geshur, and he remained there three years,¹³ so we have five years. Avsholom then returned to Yerusholayim, “and [Avsholom] lived in Yerusholayim two years,”¹⁴ so we have seven [years in the life] of Shlomo. And he [Avsholom] rebelled against his father and was killed. After this, [it states], “And there was famine in the days of Dovid [for] three years,”¹⁵ so we have ten [years]. In the eleventh [year] he counted the [Bnei] Yisroel, “and they scattered in all the land,”¹⁶ nine months. In the year of his death he instituted divisions as it is stated, “In the fortieth year of Dovid’s reign they were sought,”¹⁷ so we have twelve [years in the life] of King Shlomo.¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁰ II Shmuel 12:25.
¹¹ Ibid., 13:1.
¹² Ibid. v. 23.
¹³ Ibid. v. 38.
¹⁴ Ibid. 14:28.
¹⁵ Ibid. 21:1.
¹⁶ Ibid 24:8.
¹⁷ I Divrei Hayomim 26:31.
¹⁸ Other, including Abarbanel, suggest that Shlomo may have been around twenty years old at the time. The fact that he is referred to as a “lad ]=נער]” is not necessarily a reflection of his age, for in Bereishis 44:22, Binyomin is described as a “lad [=נער]”at the age of thirty.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Kings 3:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:9:1)


**אֶת עַמְּךָ הַכָּבֵד.** מִתּוֹךְ שֶׁהֵם רַבִּים, יֵשׁ לָהֶם עֲסָקִים רַבִּים, וּבָאִים לְדִין, וְאֵין לִי מָתוּן לְעַיֵּן בְּדִינָם. דָּבָר אַחֵר: כִּי מִי יוּכַל לִשְׁפֹּט אֶת עַמְּךָ הַכָּבֵד הַזֶּה, מִשְׁפָּטָם כָּבֵד מְאֹד, אִם יָבֹא דִין שְׁנֵי גוֹיִם לְפָנַי, וְאֶטֹּל מִזֶּה וְאֶתֵּן לָזֶה שֶׁלֹּא כַדִּין, אֵינִי נֶעֱנַשׁ, אֲבָל יִשְׂרָאֵל הֲרֵינִי נֶעֱנַשׁ עָלָיו עֹנֶשׁ נְפָשׁוֹת, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וְקָבַע אֶת קֹבְעֵיהֶם נָפֶשׁ.


**This great people of Yours.** Since they are many, they have many dealings, and they come to litigate and I have no time to deliberate over their arguments. Another interpretation of, “for who is able to judge this great people of Yours,” their legal decisions are very difficult [for me to make]. If a lawsuit comes before me between two non-Jews, and I take from one and give to the other unjustly, I will not be punished. But [if a lawsuit comes before me] between Jews [and I take from one and give to the other unjustly], I will be punished for it with capital punishment,¹⁹ as it is stated, “And He takes the lives of those that steal from them.”²⁰

footnotes:
¹⁹ That is why Shlomo refers to the Bnei Yisroel as a “weighty [=כבד] nation.”
²⁰ Mishlei 22:23.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on I Kings 3:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:14:1)


**וְאִם תֵּלֵךְ בִּדְרָכַי וְגוֹ' וְהַאֲרַכְתִּי אֶת יָמֶיךָ.** הָעשֶׁר וְהַכָּבוֹד שֶׁלֹּא הִתְנֵיתִי בְּתוֹרָתִי לָתֵת לַמֶּלֶךְ, אֶתֵּן לְךָ, בֵּין זַכַּאי בֵּין חַיָּב, אֲבָל אֹרֶךְ הַיָּמִים וּמַלְכוּת הַדּוֹרוֹת, כְּבָר הִתְנֵיתִי בְּתוֹרָתִי: לְבִלְתִּי סוּר מִן הַמִּצְוָה לְמַעַן יַאֲרִיךְ יָמִים עַל מַמְלַכְתּוֹ וְגוֹ', וְעַל אוֹתוֹ תְּנַאי לֹא אֶשְׁנֶה, וְאִם תֵּלֵךְ בִּדְרָכַי וְהַאֲרַכְתִּי וְגוֹ', וְכֵן בְּמַלְכוּת דּוֹרוֹתָיו אַחֲרָיו, אָמַר לוֹ: וְאַתָּה אִם תֵּלֵךְ לְפָנַי וְגוֹ' לֹא יִכָּרֵת לְךָ אִישׁ וְגוֹ', כָּךְ שְׁנוּיָה בְּסִפְרֵי. וְרַבִּי חֲנִינָא בֶּן גַּמְלִיאֵל אוֹמֵר: הֲרֵי הוּא אוֹמֵר גַּם עשֶׁר גַּם כָּבוֹד וְגוֹ'.


**And if you will go in My ways, etc., I will lengthen your days.** The wealth and the honor for which I did not impose any conditions in my Torah to give to the king, I will give you whether deserving or not. However, [to attain] longevity and [continuation of] the monarchy for [future] generations, I have already imposed conditions in my Torah, “that he shall not turn aside from the commandment [etc.,] so that he may prolong days over his kingdom, etc.”²¹ and that condition I will not alter. So, “if you will go in My ways...I will lengthen, etc.” and likewise concerning [the continuation of] the monarchy to his descendants, He said to him, “As for you, if you walk before Me, etc., no man [descendant] of yours will be cut off, etc.”²² Thus is it learned in Sifrei, Rabbi Chanina the son of Gamliel says, “Behold he has said, ‘both wealth and honor, etc.’”²³

footnotes:
²¹ Devarim 17:20.
²² Below, 9:4, 5.
²³ Above v. 13.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on I Kings 3:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:15:1)


**וַיִּקַץ שְׁלֹמֹה וְהִנֵּה חֲלוֹם.** וְהִנֵּה הֵבִין שֶׁחֲלוֹמוֹ אֱמֶת, שׁוֹמֵעַ עוֹף מְצַפְצֵף וּמֵבִין לְשׁוֹנוֹ, כֶּלֶב נוֹבֵחַ וּמֵבִין לְשׁוֹנוֹ.


**Shlomo woke up and behold it was a dream.** And behold he understood that his dream was true. He heard a bird chirping and understood its language, a dog barking and he understood its language.


###### Rashi on I Kings 3:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:15:2)


**וַיַּעַשׂ מִשְׁתֶּה.** מִשִּׂמְחַת לִבּוֹ, שֶׁהֵבִין שֶׁחֲלוֹמוֹ אֱמֶת.


**And [he] made a feast.** Out of his heart’s happiness [he celebrated] because he realized that his dream was true.²⁴

footnotes:
²⁴ Shlomo celebrated his newly acquired wisdom. Rabbi Elazar said that from here we derive the custom of making a festive meal [e.g., siyum] to honor the completion of reading the Torah or studying a Masechta. See Shir Hashirim Rabboh 1:9.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on I Kings 3:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:21:1)


**וָאֶתְבּוֹנֵן אֵלָיו.** נָתַתִּי לֵב לְהִסְתַּכֵּל בּוֹ.


**I scrutinized him.** I concentrated to scrutinize him.²⁵

footnotes:
²⁵ And therefore I am certain of not making a mistake.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on I Kings 3:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/3:27:1)


**הִיא אִמּוֹ.** בַּת קוֹל הוֹפִיעָה וְאָמְרָה: הִיא אִמּוֹ.


**She is his mother.** A Divine voice appeared and said, “She is his mother.”²⁶

footnotes:
²⁶ See Rashi in Maseches Makos 23b.



## Chapter 4


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on I Kings 4:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:1:1)


**מֶלֶךְ עַל כָּל יִשְׂרָאֵל.** מִשֶּׁרָאוּ בְּחָכְמָתוֹ, שָׂמְחוּ כֻּלָּם בְּמַלְכוּתוֹ.


**King over all of Yisroel.** When they observed his wisdom they all rejoiced in his kingship.¹

footnotes:
¹ And no one opposed him.—Radak



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Kings 4:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:3:1)


**סֹפְרִים.** לִכְתֹּב דִּבְרֵי הַיָּמִים.


**Scribes.** To write the [king’s] chronicles.


###### Rashi on I Kings 4:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:3:2)


**הַמַּזְכִּיר.** אֵיזֶה מִשְׁפָּט בָּא לְפָנָיו רִאשׁוֹן, שֶׁיִּפְסְקֶנּוּ רִאשׁוֹן, כָּךְ שָׁמַעְתִּי.


**The secretary.** [To record] which legal case came before him first that he might rule on it first.² Thus have I heard.

footnotes:
² I.e., he was a court administrator.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on I Kings 4:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:5:1)


**עַל הַנִּצָּבִים.** שְׁנֵים עָשָׂר נְצִיבִים הָיוּ, לְכַלְכֵּל אֶת הַמֶּלֶךְ וְאֶת בֵּיתוֹ.


**Over the officers.** He had twelve officers to provide sustenance for the king and his household.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Kings 4:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:6:1)


**עַל הַמַּס.** שֶׁהֶעֱלָה עַל יִשְׂרָאֵל, כְּמָה שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וַיַּעַל הַמֶּלֶךְ שְׁלֹמֹה מַס.


**The tax commissioner.** Which he [Shlomo] had raised on [Bnei] Yisroel, as it is stated, “And Shlomo haMelech raised a tax.”³

footnotes:
³ Below 5:27.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Kings 4:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:7:1)


**וְכִלְכְּלוּ אֶת הַמֶּלֶךְ.** כָּל סְעוּדָתוֹ, וּמַאֲכַל הַסּוּסִים, וְכָל צָרְכֵי הוֹצָאָה.


**And they provided sustenance for the king.** All his meals, and food of the horses and all expenditures for his necessities.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ See I Shmuel 8:11-18. There it describes that the king had a right to demand all these things. Shmuel had told the people the king’s rights before he anointed Shaul.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on I Kings 4:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:11:1)


**נָפַת דֹּאר.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) פַּלְכֵי דֹאר.


**Nofas-dor.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] the district [פלך] of Dor.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Kings 4:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:13:1)


**חֶבֶל אַרְגֹּב.** מְמֻנֶּה הָיָה עֲלֵיהֶם לִגְבּוֹת מַס הַמֶּלֶךְ, כִּי מִשְׁפַּט הַמֶּלֶךְ יֵשׁ לִגְבּוֹת יְצִיאוֹתָיו מִן הָעָם, כְּמָה שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בְּמִשְׁפַּט הַמְּלוּכָה בְּסֵפֶר שְׁמוּאֵל.


**The Argov region.** He [Gever’s son] was appointed over them to collect the king’s tax, because the rules pertaining to the monarchy allow for his expenditures to be collected from the people, as it is stated in the rules pertaining to the monarchy, in the book of Shmuel.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ See Ibid.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on I Kings 4:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/4:19:1)


**וּנְצִיב אֶחָד אֲשֶׁר בָּאָרֶץ.** רַב וּשְׁמוּאֵל פְּלִיגֵי, חַד אָמַר: אֶחָד מְמֻנֶּה עַל כֻּלָּם, הוּא עֲזַרְיָה בֶּן נָתָן הָאָמוּר לְמַעְלָה, וְחַד אָמַר: כְּנֶגֶד חֹדֶשׁ הָעִבּוּר.


**And one officer over the land.** Rav and Shmuel have differing opinions, one says, one was appointed over all of them, and this was Azaryoh[u] the son of Noson, who was mentioned previously,⁶ and one says he was [appointed] for the thirteenth month.⁷

footnotes:
⁶ Above v. 5.
⁷ I.e., the purpose of appointing a thirteenth officer was to take care of the king for the thirteenth month of a leap year. See Maseches Sanhedrin 12a.



## Chapter 5


### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:3:1)


**בְּרִאִים.** פְּטוּמִים עַל הָאֵבוּס.


**Fat.** Fattened in the stall.


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:3:2)


**רְעִי.** מִן הָאָחוּ.


**Pastured.** [I.e.,] on the grass.


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:3:3)


**וּבַרְבֻּרִים אֲבוּסִים.** יֵשׁ מֵרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אוֹמְרִים: תַּרְנְגוֹלִים פְּטוּמִין (וּכְעֵין זֶה כָּתַב רש"י בב"מ פו ב).


**And fattened fowl.** Among our Rabbis there are those who say [it refers to] roosters which were fattened.¹

footnotes:
¹ See Maseches Bava Metzia 86b.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:6:1)


**אַרְבָּעִים אֶלֶף אֻרְו‍ֹת סוּסִים.** וּבְדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים הוּא אוֹמֵר: אַרְבַּעַת אֲלָפִים, פֵּרְשׁוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ אַרְבָּעִים אֶלֶף אֻרְווֹת הָיוּ, וּבְכָל אַחַת אַרְבַּע אֶלֶף אִצְטַבְּלָאוֹת. אוֹ אַרְבַּעַת אֲלָפִים אֻרְווֹת, וּבְכָל אַחַת אַרְבָּעִים אֶלֶף אִצְטַבְּלָאוֹת.


**Forty thousand stables of horses.** In Divrei Hayomim it states, “four thousand [stables of horses].”² Our Rabbis explained there were forty thousand stables and in each there were four thousand partitions, or there were four thousand stables and in each there were forty thousand partitions.³

footnotes:
² II Divrei Hayomim 9:25.
³ See Maseches Sanhedrin 21b.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:7:1)


**לֹא יְעַדְּרוּ דָּבָר.** אֲפִלּוּ שֶׁל יְמוֹת הַחַמָּה בִּימוֹת הַגְּשָׁמִים.


**They omitted nothing.** Even [fruits and vegetables] of the summer season, in the winter season.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:8:1)


**וְהַשְּׂעֹרִים וְהַתֶּבֶן.** הָיוּ נוֹתְנִים אֵלּוּ הַנְּצִיבִים, אֶל הַמָּקוֹם אֲשֶׁר יִהְיֶה שָׁם הַמֶּלֶךְ.


**And the barley and the straw.** Were provided by these officers in the place where the king would be.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:10:1)


**מֵחָכְמַת כָּל בְּנֵי קֶדֶם.** מַה הִיא חָכְמַת בְּנֵי קֶדֶם, יוֹדְעִים בְּמַזָּלוֹת וְכוּ' כִּדְאִיתָא בִּפְסִיקְתָּא דְּפָרָה אֲדֻמָּה, וְכָל הַפָּרָשָׁה הַזֹּאת נִדְרֶשֶׁת שָׁם לְפִי מַשְׁמָעָהּ.


**Than the wisdom of all the Eastern people.** What was the wisdom of the children of the East? They were learned in the field of astronomy, etc. as it is [stated] in the Pesikta of Parah Adumah, and this entire chapter is expounded there according to its plain meaning.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:11:1)


**וַיֶּחְכַּם מִכָּל הָאָדָם.** כְּמַשְׁמָעוֹ.


**He was wiser than any man.** [To be understood] according to its plain meaning.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ Alternatively, “he gained wisdom from every man,” as the Mishmah in Avos 4:1 states, “Who is wise? He who learns from every man.”


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:11:2)


**אֵיתָן וְהֵימָן וְכַלְכֹּל וְדַרְדַּע.** אַחִין הָיוּ, בְּנֵי זֶרַח בֶּן יְהוּדָה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וּבְנֵי זֶרַח זִמְרִי וְאֵיתָן (הָיוּ לְוִיִּם מְשׁוֹרְרִים).


**Eison the Ezrachite and Heimon, Chalkol and Darda.** They were brothers, the sons of Zerach son of Yehudah, as it is stated, “And the children of Zerach were Zimri and Eison [and Heimon and Chalkol and Dora]”⁵ (who were *Leviyim* who played musical instruments).

footnotes:
⁵ I Divrei Hayomim 2:6.


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:11:3)


**בְּנֵי מָחוֹל.** הָיוּ יוֹדְעִים לְיַסֵּד מִזְמוֹרִים הָאֲמוּרִים בִּמְחוֹלוֹת שִׁיר, שֶׁהָיוּ הַלְוִיִּם אוֹמְרִים עַל דּוּכָנָם מִזְמוֹרִים שֶׁיִּסְּדוּ בְסֵפֶר תְּהִלִּים: מַשְׂכִּיל לְאֵיתָן הָאֶזְרָחִי; וּמַשְׂכִּיל לְהֵימָן הָאֶזְרָחִי. מִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה בִּפְסִיקְתָּא: מִכָּל הָאָדָם — זֶה אָדָם הָרִאשׁוֹן; אֵיתָן הָאֶזְרָחִי — זֶה אַבְרָהָם; הֵימָן — זֶה משֶׁה; וְכַלְכֹּל — זֶה יוֹסֵף; וְדַרְדַּע — זֶה דּוֹר הַמִּדְבָּר; מָחוֹל — שֶׁנִּמְחַל לָהֶם עַל מַעֲשֵׂה הָעֵגֶל.


**The sons of Mochol.** They knew how to compose psalms which are recited with the accompaniment of dance and song; the *Leviyim* recited on their platform psalms which were composed in the book of Tehillim, [e.g.,] “An instruction of Eison the Ezrachi,”⁶ [and,] “An instruction to Heimon the Ezrachi.”⁷ The Midrash Aggadah in the Pesikta [expounds]: “Than all men,” refers to Adam; “Eison Ha’ezrachi,” refers to Avrohom; “Heimon,” refers to Moshe; “Chalkol,” refers to Yoseif; “Darda,” refers to the generation of the wilderness; “Mochol,” who were forgiven for the act of the golden calf.

footnotes:
⁶ Tehillim 89:1.
⁷ Ibid. 88:1.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:12:1)


**שְׁלשֶׁת אֲלָפִים מָשָׁל.** שְׁלשֶׁת לִמּוּדֵי מְשָׁלוֹת, שָׁלשׁ פְּעָמִים כָּתוּב 'מִשְׁלֵי שְׁלֹמֹה' בְּסֵפֶר מִשְׁלֵי.


**Three thousand parables.** Three parabolical studies [=אלפים]. It is written three times, “the proverbs of Shlomo,” in the book of Mishlei.


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:12:2)


**וַיְהִי שִׁירוֹ חֲמִשָּׁה.** שְׁלשָׁה אֵלֶּה, וְשִׁיר הַשִּׁירִים, וְקֹהֶלֶת.


**And his songs numbered [one thousand and] five.** The above three, and Shir Hashirim and Koheles.


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:12:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:12:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:12:3)


**וָאָלֶף.** בְּכָל אֶחָד כְּדַי הוּא לִלְמֹד. וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה: שֶׁהָיָה אוֹמֵר בְּכָל פָּסוּק שְׁלשֶׁת אֲלָפִים מָשָׁל, וְעַל כָּל מָשָׁל, אֶלֶף וַחֲמִשָּׁה טְעָמִים.


**One thousand.** Each on its own is worthy of studying [=ואלף]. The Midrash Aggadah [states that Shlomo’s wisdom] enabled him to say three thousand allegories for each verse, and for each of the allegories [he was able to say] one thousand and five reasons.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ See Maseches Eruvin 21b.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:13:1)


**וַיְדַבֵּר עַל הָעֵצִים.** מָה רְפוּאַת כָּל אֶחָד, וְעֵץ פְּלוֹנִי יָפֶה לְבִנְיָן פְּלוֹנִי, וְלִטַּע בְּקַרְקַע פְּלוֹנִית וְכֵן עַל הַבְּהֵמָה, מָה רְפוּאָתָהּ, וְעִקַּר גִּדּוּלֶיהָ וּמַאֲכָלָהּ. וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה: מָה רָאָה מְצֹרָע לִטָּהֵר בְּגָבוֹהַּ שֶׁבַּגְּבוֹהִים, וּבְנָמוּךְ שֶׁבַּנְּמוּכִים. וְעַל הַבְּהֵמָה וְעַל הָעוֹף, מָה רָאָה זֶה לִהְיוֹת כָּשֵׁר בִּשְׁחִיטָה בְּסִימָן אֶחָד, וְזֶה בִּשְׁנֵי סִימָנִין, וְדָגִים וַחֲגָבִים בְּלֹא כְלוּם.


**He spoke of trees.** Which medical remedy is derived from each [tree], and that a particular tree is best suited for that type of building or to be planted in a particular field. And similarly regarding to animals, what is its cure and the vital elements needed for its development and its food. The Midrash Aggadah [expounds]: Why did God see [fit] that a leper becomes ritually clean with the tallest of the tall [trees]⁹ and the lowest of the low [trees]?¹⁰ “And of the animals and the fowl,” why did God see fit that this one¹¹ should become permissible through Shechitah, by severing one organ,¹² the esophagus. and this one,¹³ by [severing] both [organs],¹⁴ the esophagus. and fish and grasshoppers without anything [i.e., slaughtering].

footnotes:
⁹ I.e., cedar tree.
¹⁰ I.e., hyssop tree.
¹¹ Fowl.
¹² The trachea or
¹³ E.g., cattle, etc.
¹⁴ The trachea and



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:17:1)


**לֹא יָכֹל לִבְנוֹת בַּיִת וְגוֹ'.** לֹא הָיָה רַשַּׁאי מִפְּנֵי הַמִּלְחָמָה, וְהַכָּתוּב אוֹמֵר: וְהֵנִיחַ לָכֶם מִכָּל אֹיְבֵיכֶם מִסָּבִיב, וְאַחַר כָּךְ: וְהָיָה הַמָּקוֹם וְגוֹ' לְשִׁכְנוֹ תִדְרְשׁוּ וְגוֹ'.


**Was unable to build a house, etc.** He was not permitted because of the state of war and the Torah states, “And He will give you rest from all your enemies all around,”¹⁵ and afterwards, “It will be that the place, etc.¹⁶ Will you seek His Presence, etc.”¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁵ Devarim 12:10.
¹⁶ Ibid. v. 11.
¹⁷ Ibid. v. 5.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:20:1)


**כַּצִּדֹנִים.** שְׁכֵנִים לַיַּעַר לְצַד אֶחָד הָיוּ, וּבְמֶמְשַׁלְתּוֹ שֶׁל חִירָם.


**As the Tzedonim.** They¹⁸ were neighboring with the forest on one side, and under the kingdom of Chirom.

footnotes:
¹⁸ Tzidon and Tzur.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:23:1)


**אֲשִׂימֵם דֹּבְרוֹת.** 'רַפְסוֹדוֹת' קוֹרֵא לָהֶם בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים, וּבִלְשׁוֹן אַשְׁכְּנַז פליצי"ן, וּבְלַעַ"ז רי"ץ, קוֹשְׁרִין הָעֵצִים זֶה עִם זֶה, וּמְצִיפִין אוֹתָן עַל פְּנֵי הַמַּיִם, וּמוֹלִיכִין אוֹתָן כְּמוֹ שֶׁמּוֹלִיכִין הַסְּפִינוֹת.


**I will make them into rafts.** They are referred to as רפסודות in Divrei Hayomim.¹⁹ And in German, *flots*, and in O.F., *res*. They would tie logs together and float them on the water, and sail them [in the same manner] as ships are sailed.

footnotes:
¹⁹ II Divrei Hayomim 2:15.


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:23:2)


**עַד הַמָּקוֹם אֲשֶׁר תִּשְׁלַח אֵלַי.** אֲשֶׁר תּוֹדִיעֵנִי לַהֲבִיאָם שָׁם.


**To the designation that you designated to me.** That you will notify me to bring them there.


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:23:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:23:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:23:3)


**וְנִפַּצְתִּים שָׁם.** כְּשֶׁמַּתִּיר אוֹגְדָן וּמוֹצִיאָן אֶל הַיַּבָּשָׁה כָּל עֵץ וְעֵץ לְעַצְמוֹ, הִיא קְרוּיָה נִפּוּץ (כָּל דָּבָר שָׁלֵם שֶׁמְּחַלְּקִים אוֹתוֹ לִפְרָקִים, קָרוּי נִפּוּץ), דיפצי"ר בְּלַעַ"ז.


**We will detach them there.** As their binding is untied and they are brought unto dry land each log individually, is called ניפוץ. (Any whole object when it is taken apart, is called ניפוץ.) *Depced*, in O.F.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:25:1)


**מַכֹּלֶת לְבֵיתוֹ.** לְשׁוֹן מַאֲכָל.


**Food for his household.** אלפים is an expression of food [מאכל].


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:25:2)


**שֶׁמֶן כָּתִית.** שֶׁמֶן נָקִי, שֶׁאֵין זֵיתִים נִטְחָנִים בָּרֵיחַיִם, אֶלָּא כּוֹתֵשׁ בְּמַכְתֶּשֶׁת, וְאֵין מַעֲלֶה שְׁמָרִים כָּל כָּךְ כְּמוֹ הַטָּחוּן.


**Crushed oil.** Pure oil, because the olives were not ground in a mill but were crushed with a mortar, and this does not stir up as much dregs as ground [olives].



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:27:1)


**וַיְהִי הַמַּס.** עוֹלֶה לְשָׂכָר שֶׁל שְׁלשִׁים אֶלֶף אִישׁ.


**The tax consisted of.** Which amounted to the wages of thirty thousand men.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:28:1)


**חֲלִיפוֹת.** כְּמוֹ שֶׁמְּסַיֵּם וְאוֹמֵר: חֹדֶשׁ יִהְיוּ בַלְּבָנוֹן, כְּשֶׁהָיוּ עֲשֶׂרֶת אֲלָפִים אִישׁ בְּתִשְׁרֵי בַּלְּבָנוֹן, וְהָעֶשְׂרִים אֶלֶף אִישׁ בְּבֵיתוֹ, בְּמַרְחֶשְׁוָן מִתְחַלְּפִים עֲשֶׂרֶת הָאֲלָפִים הַשְּׁנִיִּים וְיוֹצְאִים לִלְבָנוֹן, וְאֵלּוּ חוֹזְרִים לְבֵיתָם, וּבְכִסְלֵו יוֹצְאִים עֲשֶׂרֶת אֲלָפִים הַשְּׁלִישִׁים וְיוֹצְאִים לִלְבָנוֹן, וְאֵלּוּ חוֹזְרִים לְבֵיתָם, וּבְטֵבֵת יוֹצְאִים הָרִאשׁוֹנִים וְחוֹזְרִים לִלְבָנוֹן, וְכֵן חוֹזְרִים חָלִילָה, נִמְצְאוּ כָּל עֲשֶׂרֶת אֲלָפִים שֶׁבָּהֶם, חֹדֶשׁ בַּלְּבָנוֹן, וּשְׁנֵי חֳדָשִׁים בְּבֵיתָם.


**In shifts.** As he concludes and says, “they will be a month in Levanon.” When the ten thousand men were in Levanon in Tishrei and the twenty thousand men were at home in Mar Cheshvan, the second ten thousand would alternate and go out to Levanon and these would return to their home[s]. And in Kislev the third ten thousand would go out to Levanon, and these [second ones] would return to their home[s]. And in Teiveis the first ones go out and return to Levanon, and thus they would continue the cycle, which resulted that each ten thousand among them spent one month in Levanon and two months at home.


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:28:2)


**עַל הַמַּס.** לִגְבּוֹתוֹ.


**In charge of the tax.** To collect it.



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:29:1)


**שִׁבְעִים אֶלֶף נֹשֵׂא סַבָּל.** לְהָבִיא הָאֲבָנִים מִן הָהָר לָעִיר, וּשְׁמוֹנִים אֶלֶף הַחוֹצְבִים אוֹתָם בָּהָר, הֲרֵי מֵאָה וַחֲמִשִּׁים אֶלֶף, וְכֻלָּם גֵּרִים גְּרוּרִים הָיוּ, שֶׁנִּתְגַּיְּרוּ מֵחֲמַת גְּדֻלַּת שְׁלֹמֹה וְשֻׁלְחָנוֹ, וְכֵן כָּתוּב בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים: וַיִּסְפֹּר שְׁלֹמֹה אֶת כָּל הַגֵּרִים וַיִּמְצָאֵם מֵאָה וַחֲמִשִּׁים אֶלֶף וַיַּעַשׂ מֵהֶם שִׁבְעִים אֶלֶף וְגוֹ'.


**Seventy thousand men who carried loads.** To bring the stones from the mountain to the city, and there were [an additional] eighty thousand who quarried them from the mountain, totaling one hundred fifty thousand [workers]. And they were all proselytes who were drawn, [i.e.,] they converted because of Shlomo’s greatness and his hospitality. And it is likewise written in Divrei Hayomim, “And Shlomo counted all the proselytes and they were found a hundred and fifty thousand. And he made seventy thousand, etc.”²⁰

footnotes:
²⁰ II Divrei Hayomim 2:17.



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:30:1)


**מִשָּׂרֵי הַנִּצָּבִים.** נוֹגְשִׂים וּנְצִיבִים עַל עוֹשֵׂי הַמְּלָאכָה.


**Chief officers.** The oppressors and overseers over those who did the work.


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:30:2)


**שְׁלשֶׁת אֲלָפִים וּשְׁלשׁ מֵאוֹת.** וּבְדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים הוּא אוֹמֵר: וְשֵׁשׁ מֵאוֹת, אוֹמֵר אֲנִי, שְׁלשֶׁת אֲלָפִים וּשְׁלשׁ מֵאוֹת הָיוּ מְמֻנִּים עַל מֵאָה וַחֲמִשִּׁים אֶלֶף, שֶׁכָּל אֶחָד מְמֻנֶּה עַל אַרְבָּעִים וַחֲמִשָּׁה וְיוֹתֵר, וְהַשְּׁלשׁ מֵאוֹת שֶׁנּוֹסָפִים בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים, הָיוּ מְמֻנִּים עַל כֻּלָּם, שֶׁכָּל אֶחָד מְמֻנֶּה עַל חֲמֵשׁ מֵאוֹת וְיוֹתֵר. וְתֵדַע, שֶׁהֲרֵי יֵשׁ עוֹד שְׁנֵי כְתוּבִים בְּדָבָר זֶה, וּמַכְחִישִׁין זֶה אֶת זֶה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בּוֹ בְּסֵפֶר זֶה: אֵלֶּה שָׂרֵי הַנִּצָּבִים אֲשֶׁר לִשְׁלֹמֹה חֲמִשִּׁים וַחֲמֵשׁ מֵאוֹת, וּבְדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים הוּא אוֹמֵר: מָאתַיִם וַחֲמִשִּׁים, הֲרֵי אַרְבַּע מִקְרָאוֹת מַכְחִישִׁין זֶה אֶת זֶה. אֲבָל יֵשׁ לָנוּ לְפָרֵשׁ, שֶׁבַּמִּקְרָאוֹת הָאַחֲרוֹנִים מְמֻנִּים עַל כֻּלָּן, וְלִשְׁלֹמֹה הָיוּ אֵלּוּ הַגֵּרִים לִהְיוֹת לְנוֹשֵׂא סַבָּל וְלַחֲצֹב אֲבָנִים בָּהָר, וְיִהְיוּ לוֹ שְׁאָר עוֹשֵׂי הַמְּלָאכָה לְעָרֵי הַמִּסְכְּנוֹת, שֶׁהֵם אֶזְרָחִיִּים, שֶׁאֵלּוּ הַכְּתוּבִים אֲמוּרִים שָׁם. וּבְסֵפֶר מְלָכִים מָנָה אֶת הַנְּצִיבִים הַגֵּרִים שְׁנֵי מִנְיָנִים, שָׂרֵי הָאַרְבָּעִים וְהַחֲמִשָּׁה לְבַדָּם, וְהַשְּׁלשׁ מֵאוֹת הַחֲשׁוּבִים צֵרֵף עִם מָאתַיִם וַחֲמִשִּׁים שֶׁהָיוּ מְמֻנִּים עַל עוֹשֵׂי עָרֵי הַמִּסְכְּנוֹת, וּמָנָה חֲמֵשׁ מֵאוֹת וַחֲמִשִּׁים שָׂרִים, וּבְדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים מָנָה כָּל נְצִיבֵי הַגֵּרִים לְעַצְמָם, הַגְּדוֹלִים עִם הַקְּטַנִּים שְׁלשֶׁת אֲלָפִים וְשֵׁשׁ מֵאוֹת, וְכֻלָּן גֵּרִים, וּנְצִיבֵי עָרֵי הַמִּסְכְּנוֹת שֶׁהָיוּ יִשְׂרָאֵל מָאתַיִם וַחֲמִשִּׁים, מָנָה לְעַצְמָן.


**Three thousand three hundred [officers].** And in II Divrei Hayomim it says, “[three thousand] and six hundred.”²¹ It is my opinion that three thousand three hundred men were in charge of one hundred fifty thousand, so that each one was in charge of slightly over forty-five, and the three hundred that were added in Divrei Hayomim were in charge of all of them, so that each was in charge of a little more than five hundred. And you should know that there are two additional passages regarding this matter, that contradict each other. For [further on] in this book it states, “These were the supervising officers that oversaw the work for Shlomo, [there were] five hundred and fifty [of them],”²² and in Divrei Hayomim it says, “two hundred and fifty.”²³ These are four verses contradicting each other. But we may explain that those that are in the latter verses²⁴ are referring to those who were in charge of all of them. And Shlomo had employed these proselytes to be bearers of burdens to hew stones in the mountain. He had the remaining workers for the store cities who were native born [Jews], because these passages are stated there.²⁵ However, in the book of Melochim he counted the proselyte officers in two separate totals. [First was the total of] the officers who were in charge of forty-five men each, separately, and he combined²⁶ the three hundred superior officers with the two hundred and fifty men who were in charge of the builders of the store cities and total together five hundred and fifty officers. And in II Divrei Hayomim he counted all the proselyte officers separately, the more important together with the less important, three thousand six hundred [men], all were proselytes. And the officers [in charge of the workers] in the store cities who totaled two hundred and fifty Jews, he counted separately.

footnotes:
²¹ Ibid.
²² I Melochim 9:23.
²³ II Divrei Hayomim 8:10.
²⁴ I Melochim 9:23 and II Divrei Hayomim 8:10.
²⁵ In II Divrei Hayomim the passages discuss the store cities.
²⁶ In I Melochim 9:23.


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:30:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:30:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:30:3)


**הָרֹדִים בָּעָם הָעֹשִׂים בַּמְּלָאכָה.** הַנּוֹגְשִׂים אֶת הָעָם עוֹשֵׂי הַמְּלָאכָה.


**Who controlled the people that did the work.** The taskmasters over the people who did the work.



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:31:1)


**וַיַּסִּעוּ.** מִן הָהָר, לְשׁוֹן עֲקִירָה.


**And they brought.** From the mountains; an expression of uprooting.


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:31:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:31:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:31:2)


**אֲבָנִים יְקָרוֹת.** כְּבֵדוֹת.


**Heavy stones.** Heavy.


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:31:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:31:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:31:3)


**אַבְנֵי גָזִית.** נִפְסָלוֹת וּמְסֻתָּתוֹת. וְאִם תֹּאמַר וַהֲרֵי כְּבָר נֶאֱמַר: וּמַקָּבוֹת וְהַגַּרְזֶן לֹא נִשְׁמַע בַּבַּיִת בְּהִבָּנוֹתוֹ, פֵּרוּשׁ שֶׁכְּלִי בַּרְזֶל לֹא נִשְׁמַע בַּבַּיִת בְּהִבָּנוֹתוֹ, אֶלָּא שֶׁהָיָה מְסַתֵּת מִבַּחוּץ, וּמַכְנִיס וּבוֹנֶה מִבִּפְנִים (סוטה מח ב).


**Hewn stone.** Carved and chiseled. And if you ask, it has already been stated, “Neither hammer nor axe [nor any iron tool] was heard in the Beis Hamikdosh, during its construction.”²⁷ The explanation is, that no iron was heard in the Beis [Hamikdosh], while it was being built, but they would chisel on the outside, and bring in and build [it] inside. Thus is this expounded in the Maseches Sotah.²⁸

footnotes:
²⁷ Below 6:7.
²⁸ See 48b.



### Verse 32

###### Rashi on I Kings 5:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:32:1)


**וַיִּפְסְלוּ.** בֶּהָרִים.


**[And they] carved.** In the mountains.


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:32:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:32:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:32:2)


**וְהַגִּבְלִים.** אֻמָּה שֶׁשְּׁמָהּ גְּבָל, וּבְקִיאִים לְסַתֵּת אֲבָנִים וְלִבְנוֹת, כְּמָה שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בְּצוֹר: זִקְנֵי גְבַל וַחֲכָמֶיהָ הָיוּ בָךְ מַחֲזִיקֵי בִּדְקֵךְ.


**And the Givlim.** A nation whose name was Geval, and they were skilled in chiseling stones and in building,²⁹ as it is stated in reference to Tzur, “The elders of Geval and its wise men, were within you, caulkers of your cracks.”³⁰

footnotes:
²⁹ Geval comes from the word גבול [=boundary]. The Givlim knew how to mark the boundaries of the stone before they cut it, and were thus able to cut stone to exact dimensions.
³⁰ Yechezkeil 27:9.


###### Rashi on I Kings 5:32:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:32:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/5:32:3)


**וַיָּכִינוּ.** לְשׁוֹן הַזְמָנָה.


**And they prepared.** An expression of designating.³¹

footnotes:
³¹ The stones and wood to their exact position.



## Chapter 6


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on I Kings 6:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:1:1)


**בְּחֹדֶשׁ זִו.** הוּא אִיָּר, דְּאִית בֵּהּ זִיוָא לְאִילָנָא, וְכֵן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: זִיו נִיצָנַיָּא.


**In the month of Ziv.** This is Iyar because the trees have blossoms. And [Targum] Yonoson translated this similarly, “the bloom of the buds.”


###### Rashi on I Kings 6:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:1:2)


**הוּא הַחֹדֶשׁ הַשֵּׁנִי.** לְמִנְיַן הֶחֳדָשִׁים, שֶׁהֲרֵי נִיסָן רֹאשׁ הַשָּׁנָה לֶחֳדָשִׁים.


**Which is the second month.** When numbering the months, because Nissan is the beginning of the year for [counting] months.


###### Rashi on I Kings 6:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:1:3)


**לִמְלֹךְ שְׁלֹמֹה.** מוּסַב עַל הַשָּׁנָה הָרְבִיעִית, רְבִיעִית לְמַלְכוּת שְׁלֹמֹה, וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים: וַיָּחֶל לִבְנוֹת בַּחֹדֶשׁ הַשֵּׁנִי בִּשְׁנַת אַרְבַּע לְמַלְכוּתוֹ.


**Of the reign of Shlomo.** Referring to “the fourth year.”¹ It was the fourth year of Shlomo’s reign. And similarly it is stated in Divrei Hayomim, “And he began to build in the second month [on the second day] in the fourth year of his reign.”²

footnotes:
¹ I.e., it does not mean “the second month of Shlomo’s reign,” rather it means the second month of the fourth year of Shlomo’s reign.
² II Divrei Hayomim 3:2.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on I Kings 6:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:2:1)


**שִׁשִּׁים אַמָּה.** הַהֵיכָל וְהַדְּבִיר יַחַד.


**Sixty *amohs*.** The Sanctuary and the Holy of Holies together.³ and the Holy of Holies was 20 *amos*.

footnotes:
³ The length of the Sanctuary was 40 amos



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Kings 6:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:3:1)


**וְהָאוּלָם עַל פְּנֵי.** לִפְנֵי הַהֵיכָל בִּכְנִיסָתוֹ.


**The entrance hall along the front.** In front of the entrance to the Sanctuary.


###### Rashi on I Kings 6:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:3:2)


**אָרְכּוֹ עַל פְּנֵי רֹחַב הַבָּיִת.** אֹרֶךְ הָאוּלָם לְרָחְבּוֹ שֶׁל הַהֵיכָל מִן הַצָּפוֹן לַדָּרוֹם. כָּל מִדָּה הַיְתֵרָה קְרוּיָה אֹרֶךְ, וְהַפְּחוּתָה קְרוּיָה רֹחַב, לְפִי שֶׁבַּהֵיכָל מִדַּת מִן הַמִּזְרָח לַמַּעֲרָב יְתֵרָה, קְרוּיָה אֹרֶךְ, וּבָאוּלָם שֶׁמִּדַּת מִצָּפוֹן לַדָּרוֹם יְתֵרָה בּוֹ, קְרוּיָה אֹרֶךְ מִן הַצָּפוֹן לַדָּרוֹם, וּמִן הַמִּזְרָח לַמַּעֲרָב קְרוּיָה בּוֹ רֹחַב.


**[Twenty *amohs*] long along the front of the width of the Beis Hamikdosh.** The length of the entrance hall was along the width of the Sanctuary, from north to south. All larger measurements are referred to as the length, and the lesser are referred to as the width. Since in the Sanctuary, the measurement from east to west was greater,⁴ versus 20 *amos* from north to south. it was called the length, but since the entrance hall’s measurement from north to south was greater,⁵ versus 10 *amos* from east to west. [that which is] called the length, [is the measurement] from north to south and [the measurement] from east to west was called the width.

footnotes:
⁴ It measured 40 amos
⁵ It measured 20 amos


###### Rashi on I Kings 6:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:3:3)


**עַל פְּנֵי הַבָּיִת.** לִפְנֵי הַבַּיִת, לְצַד מִזְרָח לְצַד חוּץ.


**Along the front of the house.** Was in front of the house on the eastern side, on the outside.⁶ mentioned in v. 2 above.

footnotes:
⁶ I.e., it was in addition to the 60 amos



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on I Kings 6:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:4:1)


**שְׁקֻפִים אֲטֻמִים.** רַבּוֹתֵינוּ פֵּרְשׁוּ: שְׁקוּפִים לְשׁוֹן רְאִיָּה וּפְתִיחָה וְהַשְׁקָפָה, פְּתוּחִים מִבַּחוּץ וַאֲטוּמִים מִבִּפְנִים, קְצָרִים מִבִּפְנִים, שֶׁלֹּא כְּדֶרֶךְ שְׁאָר חַלּוֹנוֹת אֲחֵרוֹת הָעֲשׂוּיוֹת לְמָאוֹר, לְהַרְאוֹת שֶׁאֵינוֹ צָרִיךְ לְאוֹרָה.


**That were open [wide] on the outside and closed [narrow] on the inside.** Our Rabbis explained [שקופים] as an expression of looking, an opening, an observation;⁷ open on the outside and closed on the inside, narrow on the inside, not in the usual manner of other windows⁸ which are made for illumination,⁹ to indicate that it was not in need of illumination.¹⁰

footnotes:
⁷ It was made of a glass-like substance.—Metzudas Dovid.
⁸ Alternatively, חלוני means “sockets” into which שקפים [=“horizontal beams”] were inserted. The sockets were open only on the inside and did not go all the way through the wall, i.e., they were אטומים [=sealed] and not visible from the outside of the building.
⁹ They are therefore constructed narrow on the outside and widening towards the inside to allow the light to spread out as it enters.
¹⁰ See Maseches Menachos 86b.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on I Kings 6:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:5:1)


**יָצִיעַ.** אפנטי"ץ בְּלַעַ"ז (יָצִיעַ), וּשְׁלשָׁה שֵׁמוֹת יֵשׁ לָהּ, יָצִיעַ, תָּא, צֶלַע כַּךְ מְפוֹרָשׁ בְּבָּבָא בַּתְרָא.


**A chamber.** *Apentec*, in O.F. It has three names, יציע' תא' צלע, thus is this explained in [Maseches] Bava Basra.¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ 61a.


###### Rashi on I Kings 6:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:5:2)


**אֶת קִירוֹת הַבַּיִת סָבִיב.** כְּלוֹמַר כְּנֶגֶד הַהֵיכָל וּבֵית קָדְשֵׁי הַקֳּדָשִׁים הוּא הַדְּבִיר, עָשָׂה צְלָעוֹת מִבַּחוּץ, לְדָרוֹם וּלְמַעֲרָב וּלְצָפוֹן, וְסוֹף הַמִּקְרָא הוּא פֵּרוּשׁ לְרֹאשׁוֹ, וַיִּבֶן עַל קִיר הַבַּיִת, סָמוּךְ לְקִיר הַבַּיִת, יָצִיעַ סָבִיב, כֵּיצַד עָשָׂה? אֶת קִירוֹת הַבַּיִת לַהֵיכָל וְלַדְּבִיר, עָשָׂה צְלָעוֹת.


**Around the walls of the Beis Hamikdosh circling all around.** I.e., against the Sanctuary and the Holy of Holies, which is the דביר, he made chambers on the outside at the south, west, and north. And the end of the verse explains its beginning. “He built against the wall of the Beis Hamikdosh, [meaning] near the wall of the Beis Hamikdosh, a chamber round about. How did he make it? “Against the walls of the Beis Hamikdosh, [i.e., against] the Sanctuary and the Holy of Holies, he made chambers.”


###### Rashi on I Kings 6:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:5:3)


**וַיַּעַשׂ.** כְּמוֹ עָשָׂה, וְכֵן: וַיְהִי לִי לִישׁוּעָה, כְּמוֹ הָיָה לִי לִישׁוּעָה.


**And he made.** He made. And similarly, “He was [ויהי] my salvation,”¹² means the same as “He was [היה] my salvation.”¹³

footnotes:
¹² Shemos 15:2.
¹³ The word ‘יהי’ is in the future tense but the ‘vav’ converts it [="ו" המהפך] to the past tense.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Kings 6:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/6:6:1)
