⁸ This type of gold can be spun like thread [חוט]. See Maseches Yoma 44b-45a.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on I Kings 10:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:18:1)


**זָהָב מוּפָז.** מַבְהִיק כְּמַרְגָּלִית.


**Glittering gold.** Sparkling like pearls.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on I Kings 10:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:19:1)


**שֵׁשׁ מַעֲלוֹת.** מַדְרֵגוֹת לַעֲלוֹת בּוֹ, וְרֹאשׁ הַכִּסֵּא הָיָה עָגֹל, מְקוֹם מוֹשַׁב הַמֶּלֶךְ.


**Six steps.** Steps by which to go up [to it], and the top of the throne, was circular, where the king sits.


###### Rashi on I Kings 10:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:19:2)


**מֵאַחֲרָיו.** תִּקּוּן הַכִּסֵּא הָיָה רָחָב, וְהַמַּעֲלוֹת הָיוּ לְצַד פָּנָיו, וּלְצַד רְחָבָה לְצַד אַחֲרָיו הָיָה הַמּוֹשָׁב גָּבוֹהַּ מִן הַכִּסֵּא מָקוֹם עָגֹל.


**From behind.** The throne was wide, and the steps were to the front and to the width, to the back was the seat, higher than the throne, was a round place.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ A circular dome protruded from above the throne above the king’s head.—Metzudas Dovid


###### Rashi on I Kings 10:19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:19:3)


**וְיָדֹת מִזֶּה וּמִזֶּה.** לִסְמֹךְ זְרוֹעוֹתָיו עֲלֵיהֶם, כְּמִין מַקְלוֹת זָהָב מִפֵּאָה לְפֵאָה, פְּשׁוּטִין לְיָמִין וְלִשְׂמֹאל.


**And there were arm-rests on each side.** On which to support his arms, like two gold rails from end to end, extending on the right and on the left.


###### Rashi on I Kings 10:19:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:19:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:19:4)


**וּשְׁנַיִם אֲרָיוֹת.** שֶׁל זָהָב, וְכָל צוּרַת הַכִּסֵּא מְפֹרֶשֶׁת בְּאַגָּדַת מְגִלַּת אֶסְתֵּר.


**There were two lions.** Of gold. The entire plan of the throne is described in the Aggadah of Megillas Esther.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ Esther Rabboh 1:12.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on I Kings 10:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:21:1)


**אֵין כֶּסֶף לֹא נֶחְשָׁב.** אֵין כֶּסֶף בְּאֶחָד מֵהֶם, כִּי לֹא נֶחְשָׁב.


**None of them were made of silver, as [silver] was not considered of value.** There was no silver in any of them because it was not valuable.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on I Kings 10:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:22:1)


**אֳנִי תַרְשִׁישׁ.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) סְפִינַת אַפְרִיקָא.


**Ships of Tarshish.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “ships of Africa.”


###### Rashi on I Kings 10:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:22:2)


**שֶׁנְהַבִּים וְקֹפִים וְתֻכִּיִּים.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) שֵׁן דְּפִיל וְקוֹפִין וְטַוָּסִין.


**Ivory, monkeys, and peacocks.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] ivory, monkeys and peacocks.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on I Kings 10:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:25:1)


**וְנֵשֶׁק.** כְּלֵי זַיִן.


**Weapons.** Weapons.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on I Kings 10:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:26:1)


**אֶלֶף וְאַרְבַּע מֵאוֹת רֶכֶב.** וּבְדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים כָּתוּב: אֶלֶף (וּשְׁבַע) [וְאַרְבַּע] מֵאוֹת. וְאוֹמֵר אֲנִי, אֶלֶף וְאַרְבַּע מֵאוֹת בְּעָרֵי הָרֶכֶב הָיוּ, וְהַשְּׁלשׁ מֵאוֹת עִם הַמֶּלֶךְ בִּירוּשָׁלַיִם, וְכָךְ פֵּרוּשׁוֹ בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים: וַיַּנִּיחֵם בְּעָרֵי הָרֶכֶב, וְעִם הַמֶּלֶךְ הָיָה עוֹד רֶכֶב אֶחָד בִּירוּשָׁלַיִם. וְכָאן כָּךְ פֵּרוּשׁוֹ: וַיַּנְחֵם בְּעָרֵי הָרֶכֶב, וְעִם הַמֶּלֶךְ הָיוּ מִקְצָתָן בִּירוּשָׁלַיִם.


**Fourteen hundred chariots.** But in Divrei Hayomim it states, “seventeen hundred.”¹¹ Therefore I say that fourteen hundred were in the chariot cities, and three hundred were with the king in Yerusholayim. And this is the explanation in Divrei Hayomim, “and he stationed them in the chariot cities, and with the king there were other chariots in Yerusholayim.” And while here, this is the explanation, and he stationed them in the chariot cities and with the king; and in Yerusholayim [were additional ones].

footnotes:
¹¹ II Divrei Hayomim 1:14. Rashi apparently had a different version of the text than ours. Our text in Divrei Hayomim reads, “fourteen hundred chariots.” Thus there is no discrepancy between the texts.


###### Rashi on I Kings 10:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:26:2)


**וַיַּנְחֵם.** וַיְנַהֲלֵם.


**He placed them.** And he led them.¹²

footnotes:
¹² Alternatively, he stationed them.—Metzudas Dovid.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on I Kings 10:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:27:1)


**כַּאֲבָנִים.** וּלְמַעְלָה הוּא אוֹמֵר 'לִמְאוּמָה'. רַבּוֹתֵינוּ פֵּרְשׁוּ: כָּאן קֹדֶם שֶׁנָּשָׂא שְׁלֹמֹה אֶת בַּת פַּרְעֹה, כָּאן לְאַחַר שֶׁנָּשָׂא שְׁלֹמֹה אֶת בַּת פַּרְעֹה.


**As stones.** But above it states, “of no value.”¹³ Our Rabbis explained that one refers to the time before Shlomo married Pharaoh’s daughter, and one refers to the time after Shlomo wedded Pharaoh’s daughter.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹³ Above v. 21.
¹⁴ See Maseches Sanhedrin 21b. After Shlomo married Pharaoh’s daughter and began to go astray, the nation became less prosperous and silver regained some value.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on I Kings 10:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:28:1)


**וּמוֹצָא הַסּוּסִים אֲשֶׁר לִשְׁלֹמֹה.** הָיָה מִמִּצְרַיִם.


**The source of the horses that Shlomo possessed.** Was from Egypt.


###### Rashi on I Kings 10:28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:28:2)


**וּמִקְוֵה.** אֲסֵפַת סוֹחֲרֵי הַסּוּסִים בְּמִצְרַיִם, סוֹחֲרֵי הַמֶּלֶךְ שְׁלֹמֹה הָיוּ קוֹנִין אוֹתָם בִּמְחִיר מִמִּצְרַיִם מִן הַמֶּלֶךְ, שֶׁלֹּא יוֹצִיא אָדָם סוּס מִשָּׁם, אֶלָּא עַל יָדָם, כָּךְ שָׁמַעְתִּי. וּלְכָךְ נָקוּד עַל 'וּמִקְוֵה' טַעַם זָקֵ"ף גָּדוֹל, לִלְמֹד שֶׁהַתֵּבָה עוֹמֶדֶת בְּעַצְמָהּ, וְאֵינָהּ דְּבוּקָה לְאַחֲרֶיהָ.


**A privileged cartel.** The gathering of horse dealers was in Egypt. Shlomo’s merchants bought [the rights] from the king of Egypt, because no person could export horses from there except through them. This is what I heard. Therefore, there is a “zakef gadol”¹⁵ on “ומקוה,” to denote that the word מקוה stands by itself and is not connected with what follows it.¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁵ I.e., a cantillation signifying a pause.
¹⁶ Alternatively, מקוה is linen thread [thread=תקוה, See Yehoshua 2:18], i.e. Egypt exported two items for Shlomo, horses and linen thread—Radak. Or מקוה [=מן קוה], i.e., they brought horses from a region called קוה.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on I Kings 10:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:29:1)


**וְסוּס בַּחֲמִשִּׁים וּמֵאָה.** לָמַדְנוּ שֶׁהַמֶּרְכָּבָה בְּאַרְבַּע סוּסִים, וְכֵן בַּדָּמִים הַלָּלוּ לְכָל מַלְכֵי הַחִתִּים וּלְמַלְכֵי אֲרָם.


**And a single horse for one hundred fifty.** We deduce that a chariot consisted of four horses.¹⁷ And for these prices [they sold] to all the kings of the Chitim and Aram.

footnotes:
¹⁷ If a single horse was exported for 150 silver pieces, and a chariot was exported for 600 silver pieces, obviously a chariot consisted of four horses.


###### Rashi on I Kings 10:29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/10:29:2)


**בְּיָדָם.** שֶׁל סוֹחֲרֵי שְׁלֹמֹה, יוֹצְאִים מִמִּצְרַיִם.


**Through them.** Through Shlomo’s merchants, they exported them from Egypt.



## Chapter 11


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on I Kings 11:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:1:1)


**וְאֶת בַּת פַּרְעֹה.** אַף הִיא מִן הַנָּכְרִיּוֹת, אֶלָּא שֶׁהָיְתָה חֲבִיבָה עָלָיו מִכֻּלָּם, מְנָאָהּ לְעַצְמָהּ, וְכֵן: וַיִּפָּקְדוּ מֵעַבְדֵי דָוִד תִּשְׁעָה עָשָׂר אִישׁ וַעֲשָׂהאֵל.


**And [especially] the daughter of Pharaoh.** She too was one of the foreign women, but because she was beloved by him more than all of them, she was counted separately.¹ And similarly, “And there were missing of Dovid’s servants nineteen men and Asahel.”²

footnotes:
¹ Alternatively, Pharaoh’s daughter is singled out because she was the cause for the beginning of Shlomo’s downfall.—Radak
² II Shmuel 2:30.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Kings 11:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:7:1)


**אָז יִבְנֶה שְׁלֹמֹה בָּמָה.** רַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ: מִתּוֹךְ שֶׁלֹּא מִחָה בְּנָשָׁיו, נִקְרֵאת עַל שְׁמוֹ.


**Then Shlomo built a *Bomoh* [altar].** Our Rabbis said, that because he did not protest against his wives, it is called by his name.³

footnotes:
³ Maseches Shabbos 56b. A Jew is responsible for the behavior of those subject to his influence. The Torah demands, “you shall reprove your fellow and do not bear a sin on his account” [Vayikra 19:17], implying that if you fail to reprove him, you will bear his sin.—Ralbag


###### Rashi on I Kings 11:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:7:2)


**בָּהָר אֲשֶׁר עַל פְּנֵי יְרוּשָׁלָיִם.** הַר הַזֵּיתִים.


**On the mountain opposite Yerusholayim.** The Mount of Olives.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on I Kings 11:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:11:1)


**אֲשֶׁר הָיְתָה זֹּאת עִמָּךְ.** אֲשֶׁר יָדַעְתָּ שֶׁאַתָּה עוֹבֵר עַל מִצְוָתִי.


**This had been with you.** [I.e.,] you knew that you were transgressing My commandments.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on I Kings 11:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:15:1)


**וַיְהִי בִּהְיוֹת דָּוִד אֶת אֱדוֹם.** אָז וַיִּבְרַח הֲדַד מִפְּנֵי דָוִד וְיוֹאָב.


**Dovid was in Edom.** Then Hadad fled from Dovid and Yoav.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ Below v. 17.


###### Rashi on I Kings 11:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:15:2)


**לְקַבֵּר אֶת הַחֲלָלִים.** שֶׁקָּבְרוּ לַהֲרוּגִים שֶׁל אֱדוֹם, הוּא שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר שָׁם בְּסֵפֶר שְׁמוּאֵל: וַיַּעַשׂ דָּוִד שֵׁם בְּשׁוּבוֹ מֵהַכּוֹתוֹ אֶת (אֱדוֹם) [אֲרָם], שֶׁהָיוּ הַכֹּל מְקַלְּסִין אוֹתוֹ, אִישׁ חָסִיד שֶׁקּוֹבֵר אֶת הֲרוּגָיו, וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר בְּמִלְחֲמוֹת גּוֹג וּמָגוֹג: וְקָבְרוּ כָּל עַם הָאָרֶץ וְהָיָה לָהֶם לְשֵׁם. וְיוֹנָתָן תִּרְגֵּם לְקַבֵּר אֶת הַחֲלָלִים: לְחַלָּצָא יַת קְטִילַיָּא, לָקַחַת אֶת חֲלִיצָתָם.


**To bury the dead.** For they buried the slain of Edom, this is what is stated in the Book of Shmuel, “And Dovid made [for himself] a name when he returned from slaying [those of] Edom,”⁵.” For clarification, see Tehillim 60:2. for all were praising him as a pious person for he buries his slain. And similarly it states concerning the wars of Gog and Magog, “And they will bury all the people of the land, and it will bring them fame.”⁶ However, [Targum] Yonoson rendered לקבר את החללים, “to strip the slain,” [i.e.,] to remove their clothing.

footnotes:
⁵ II Shmuel 8:13. Rashi apparently had a different version of the text than ours which reads, “...after returning from slaying [those of] Aram
⁶ Yechezkeil 39:13.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on I Kings 11:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:18:1)


**וְלֶחֶם אָמַר לוֹ.** אָמַר לִמְשָׁרְתָיו, כָּךְ וְכָךְ לֶחֶם תְּנוּ לוֹ לְיוֹם.


**And ordered that he be provided with food.** He said to his servants, “So much and so much bread should you give him daily.”



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on I Kings 11:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:19:1)


**הַגְּבִירָה.** הַמַּלְכָּה.


**The Queen.** The queen.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on I Kings 11:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:21:1)


**כִּי שָׁכַב דָּוִד.** דָּוִד שֶׁמֵּת מִיתַת עַצְמוֹ, נֶאֶמְרָה בוֹ שְׁכִיבָה, יוֹאָב שֶׁנֶּהֱרַג, נֶאֶמְרָה בוֹ מִיתָה. דָּבָר אַחֵר: דָּוִד שֶׁהִנִּיחַ בֵּן הָגוּן מְמַלֵּא מְקוֹמוֹ, נֶאֱמַר בּוֹ שְׁכִיבָה, יוֹאָב שֶׁלֹּא הִנִּיחַ בֵּן הָגוּן מְמַלֵּא מְקוֹמוֹ, לֹא נֶאֶמְרָה בוֹ שְׁכִיבָה, כִּי אִם מִיתָה.


**That Dovid slept.** For Dovid who died of natural causes, an expression of sleeping [שכיבה] is mentioned; but for Yoav who was killed, an expression of dying [מיתה] is mentioned.. Another explanation is that concerning Dovid who was survived by a meritorious son who took his place, [an expression of] sleeping is mentioned, but concerning Yoav who was not survived by a meritorious son who took his place, [an expression of] sleeping is not mentioned, rather, [an expression of] dying [is mentioned].⁷

footnotes:
⁷ Maseches Bava Basra 116a.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on I Kings 11:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:23:1)


**וַיָּקֶם אֱלֹהִים לוֹ שָׂטָן.** לִשְׁלֹמֹה, וְזֶה הוּא שֶׁאָמַר נָתַן הַנָּבִיא לְדָוִד: אֲשֶׁר בְּהַעֲוֹתוֹ וְהֹכַחְתִּיו בְּשֵׁבֶט אֲנָשִׁים.


**And God raised up a rival against him [Shlomo].** Against Shlomo. And this is what Noson the prophet said to Dovid, “so that when he goes astray, I will chasten him with the rod of men.”⁸

footnotes:
⁸ II Shmuel 7:14. God sent adversaries against Shlomo for the purpose of inducing him to repent.—Radak



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on I Kings 11:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:25:1)


**וְאֶת הָרָעָה אֲשֶׁר הֲדָד.** עִם הָרָעָה שֶׁעַל יְדֵי הֲדָד, הָיְתָה גַּם זוֹ שֶׁל רָזוֹן.


**In conjunction with the evil of Hadad.** Together with the evil that was perpetrated by Hadad, was also this evil of Rezon.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ For without Hadad, Rezon alone would be unable to antagonize Shlomo.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on I Kings 11:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:26:1)


**וַיָּרֶם יָד בַּמֶּלֶךְ.** הוֹכִיחוֹ בָּרַבִּים.


**And he raised his hand against the king.** He rebuked him in public.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ He was correct in rebuking him about building up the Millo, but he was wrong for doing it in public. See Maseches Sanhedrin 101b and Rashi there.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on I Kings 11:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:27:1)


**וְזֶה הַדָּבָר.** אֲשֶׁר הוֹכִיחוֹ עָלָיו.


**It was about this matter.** Concerning which he rebuked him.


###### Rashi on I Kings 11:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:27:2)


**שְׁלֹמֹה בָּנָה אֶת הַמִּלּוֹא.** וּבְבִנְיָן זֶה סָגַר אֶת פֶּרֶץ הָעִיר דָּוִד, שֶׁהָיָה הַפֶּרֶץ אֲחוֹרֵי הַמִּלּוֹא, אָמַר לוֹ: אָבִיךָ פָּרַץ פְּרָצוֹת בַּחוֹמָה לִכָּנֵס בּוֹ עוֹלֵי רְגָלִים, וְאַתָּה גָּדַרְתָּ אוֹתָהּ, לַעֲשׂוֹת אַנְגַּרְיָא לְבַת פַּרְעֹה, לְהוֹשִׁיב שָׁם עֲבָדֶיהָ וּמְשָׁרְתֶיהָ.


**Shlomo had built up the Millo.** And with this building, he closed up the breach of the City of Dovid which was behind the Millo.¹¹ He said to him, “Your father made breaches in the wall for the festival pilgrims to enter, and you closed it up, to create a labor force for Pharaoh’s daughter, to station there her menservants and maidservants.”¹²

footnotes:
¹¹ See above 9:24.
¹² Maseches Sanhedrin 101b and Rashi there.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on I Kings 11:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:28:1)


**וַיַּרְא שְׁלֹמֹה אֶת הַנַּעַר.** קֹדֶם לָכֵן.


**And Shlomo had seen that this young man.** Prior to this.


###### Rashi on I Kings 11:28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:28:2)


**כִּי עוֹשֶׂה מְלָאכָה הוּא.** מָהִיר בִּמְלַאכְתּוֹ וְזָרִיז.


**Did his work with diligence.** Quick in his work and diligent.


###### Rashi on I Kings 11:28:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:28:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:28:3)


**לְכָל סֵבֶל בֵּית יוֹסֵף.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) לְכָל מַסְקֵי מִסִּין דְּבֵית יוֹסֵף, מְנַשֶּׁה וְאֶפְרַיִם, לְפִי שֶׁמִּשֵּׁבֶט אֶפְרַיִם הָיָה.


**Over all the [tax] burdens of the House of Yosef.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “over all the taxpayers of the House of Yosef,” [i.e.,] Menashe and Ephrayim, because he was from the tribe of Ephrayim.



### Verse 36

###### Rashi on I Kings 11:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:36:1)


**נִיר.** מֶמְשָׁלָה, וְ'נִיר' לְשׁוֹן 'עֹל'.


**A lamp.** A kingdom. ניר is an expression of a yoke.¹³

footnotes:
¹³ The king’s subjects must obey their king, just as the ox must obey the person controlling his yoke. Alternatively, ניר means “a candle [=נר],” just as a candle illuminates its surroundings, so does a king influence his subjects.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 39

###### Rashi on I Kings 11:39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/11:39:1)


**אַךְ לֹא כָּל הַיָּמִים.** כִּי לִימוֹת הַמָּשִׁיחַ תָּשׁוּב הַמְּלוּכָה אֵלָיו. וּבְסֵדֶר עוֹלָם מָצָאתִי: וַאעַנֶּה אֶת זֶרַע דָּוִד לְמַעַן זֹאת, כְּנֶגֶד שְׁלשִׁים וָשֵׁשׁ שָׁנָה שֶׁנִּתְחַתֵּן שְׁלֹמֹה אֶת בַּת פַּרְעֹה, שֶׁנְּשָׂאָהּ בַּשָּׁנָה הָרְבִיעִית לְמָלְכוֹ, וּכְנֶגְדּוֹ נִגְזְרָה גְּזֵרָה עַל מַלְכוּת בֵּית דָּוִד לֵחָלֵק, וּרְאוּיָה הָיְתָה הַמְּלוּכָה לַחֲזֹר בִּימֵי אָסָא בִּשְׁנַת שֵׁשׁ עֶשְׂרֶה לְמָלְכוֹ, אֶלָּא שֶׁקִּלְקֵל לִשְׁלֹחַ שֹׁחַד לְמֶלֶךְ אֲרָם, וְלֹא סָמַךְ עַל הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא, וְהוּא שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים: בִּשְׁנַת שְׁלשִׁים וָשֵׁשׁ לְמַלְכוּת אָסָא, בָּנָה בַּעְשָׁא אֶת הָרָמָה, וְאִי אֶפְשָׁר, שֶׁהֲרֵי אָסָא קָבַר אֶת בַּעְשָׁא בִּשְׁנַת עֶשְׂרִים וָשֵׁשׁ לְמַלְכוּתוֹ, אֶלָּא שֵׁשׁ עֶשְׂרֵה שָׁנָה שֶׁלּוֹ, הוּא קוֹרֵא שְׁלשִׁים וָשֵׁשׁ, שֶׁהֵן סוֹף שְׁלשִׁים וָשֵׁשׁ לַחֲלֻקַת הַמַּלְכוּת, וְהִגִּיד הַכָּתוּב שֶׁבְּאוֹתָהּ שָׁנָה קִלְקֵל אָסָא, אַךְ לֹא כָּל הַיָּמִים, אֶלָּא שְׁלשִׁים וָשֵׁשׁ שָׁנָה.


**But not for all the days.** For in the days of the Mashiach, the kingdom will be restored to him. In Seder Olam I found: “I shall afflict Dovid’s descendants because of this,” corresponding to the thirty-six years that Shlomo was married to Pharaoh’s daughter. For he married her during the fourth year of his reign, and corresponding to this, the decree was promulgated on the kingdom of the House of Dovid to be divided. The kingdom should have been restored, in Asa’s time, in the sixteenth year of his reign. However, he sinned by sending a bribe to the king of Aram, and did not depend on the Holy One Blessed Is He. This is [the meaning] of what is stated in Divrei Hayomim, “In the thirty-sixth year of Asa’s reign, etc., Baasha built the high place.”¹⁴ This is impossible, because Asa buried Baasha in the twenty-seventh year of his reign. Rather, the sixteenth year of his reign, Scripture calls “the thirty-sixth,” for they are the end of thirty-six years since the division of the kingdom, and Scripture tells us that in that year Asa sinned, “but not for all the days,” only for thirty-six years.

footnotes:
¹⁴ II Divrei Hayomim 16:1.



## Chapter 12


### Verse 10

###### Rashi on I Kings 12:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:10:1)


**קָטָנִּי עָבָה.** אֶצְבַּע קְטַנָּה שֶׁלִּי.


**My little finger is thicker.** My little finger.¹

footnotes:
¹ the beginning of his reign, Rechovom was 41 years old [See below 14:21], but Shlomo was only 12 years old when he became king [See above 3:7 and Rashi there].—Ralbag



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on I Kings 12:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:11:1)


**עַקְרַבִּים.** אשגלנטי"ר בְּלַעַ"ז (עֵץ וֶרֶד–הַבָּר שֶׁיֵּשׁ לוֹ קוֹצִים), שֶׁעוֹקְצִין כְּעַקְרָב.


**Thorns.** *Aiguillon*, in O.F., That sting like scorpions.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on I Kings 12:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:16:1)


**רְאֵה בֵיתְךָ.** בֵּית הַמִּקְדָּשׁ שֶׁבָּנִיתָ, לְךָ לְבַדְּךָ יִהְיֶה.


**Look after your house.** The Beis Hamikdosh that you built, will be for you alone.²

footnotes:
² Alternatively, “see who is left with you,” and rule over them, for you will not reign over Yisroel as a whole.—Radak



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on I Kings 12:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:18:1)


**אֲדוֹרָם.** הוּא אֲדוֹנִירָם.


**Adoram.** He was Adoniram.³

footnotes:
³ Who is mentioned in 4:6 and 5:28 above. See II Shmuel 20:24. Rechovom sent Adoram to speak gently to the people and to calm their anger.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on I Kings 12:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:21:1)


**וְאֶת שֵׁבֶט בִּנְיָמִן.** הָעוֹמְדִים עַל הַגְּבוּל, שֶׁיְּהוּדָה וּבִנְיָמִין הָיָה גְּבוּלָם סָמוּךְ זֶה לָזֶה.


**And the tribe of Binyomin.** Who were situated on the border, because the boundaries of Yehudah and Binyomin were adjacent.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on I Kings 12:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:27:1)


**אִם יַעֲלֶה הָעָם הַזֶּה וְגוֹ'.** אֵין יְשִׁיבָה בָּעֲזָרָה אֶלָּא לְמַלְכֵי בֵּית דָּוִד בִּלְבַד, וְיִהְיֶה הוּא יוֹשֵׁב וַאֲנִי עוֹמֵד, וּבְמוֹצָאֵי שְׁבִיעִית בִּזְמַן הַקְהֵל, יִקְרָא אֶת פָּרָשַׁת הַמֶּלֶךְ, שֶׁהוּא רֹאשׁ בִּמְדִינָתוֹ, וַאֲנִי כִּשְׁאָר הָעָם, נִמְצֵאתִי בָּזוּי.


**If this people will go up, etc.** Sitting in the Temple court is not permitted only for kings of the house of Dovid, therefore, he will be sitting and I will be standing. Moreover, [during Succos] following the Sabbatical Year, at the time of the Assembly, he [is the one who] will read the section [in Devarim] relating to the king,⁴ for he is the king of his province, whereas I will be [standing] like the rest of the people, and I will, therefore, be degraded.⁵

footnotes:
⁴ See Mishnayos Sotah 7:8.
⁵ Maseches Sanhedrin 101b.



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on I Kings 12:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:30:1)


**עַד דָּן.** בְּסוֹף גְּבוּל הָאָרֶץ.


**Even as far as Dan.** At the end of the boundary of the land.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ They were so attached to idol worship that they made the long pilgrimage from one end of the land all the way to the altar in Dan in the northernmost part of Yisroel.



### Verse 33

###### Rashi on I Kings 12:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:33:1)


**בַּחֹדֶשׁ הַשְּׁמִינִי.** דָּרַשׁ לָהֶם הוּא חֹדֶשׁ הָאָסִיף, וּבוֹ רָאוּי הֶחָג לִהְיוֹת.


**Of the eighth month.** He expounded to them that this was the month of the harvest, and was, therefore, the proper time for the festival to be.


###### Rashi on I Kings 12:33:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:33:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/12:33:2)


**בָּדָא מִלִּבּוֹ.** לְשׁוֹן כָּזָב, קונטרובי"ר בְּלַעַ"ז:


**He thought up out of his own heart.** An expression of falsehood, *controver*, in O.F.



## Chapter 13

¹ See II Divrei Hayomim 9:29.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on I Kings 13:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:2:1)


**מִזְבֵּחַ מִזְבֵּחַ.** שֶׁבְּבֵית אֵל וְשֶׁבְּדָן.


**Altar, altar.** [The one] in Beis Eil and [the one] in Dan.²

footnotes:
² Alternatively, it is common to use repetition when summoning, i.e., “Avrohom, Avrohom [Bereishis 22:1],” etc.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Kings 13:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:2:2)


**וְעַצְמוֹת אָדָם.** שֶׁל יָרָבְעָם זֶה, אֶלָּא שֶׁחָלַק לוֹ כָּבוֹד.


**And the bones of human beings.** This refers to Yerovom’s [bones], but he [the prophet] treated him with respect.³

³ Indeed he was prophesying that Yerovom’s bones will be burnt on the same altar where he was standing now and offering sacrifices. However the Torah demands that a king be treated with respect. See Shemos 11:8 and Rashi there.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Kings 13:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:3:1)


**וְנָתַן בַּיּוֹם הַהוּא.** הַנָּבִיא מוֹפֵת לִדְבָרָיו בַּיּוֹם שֶׁנִּתְנַבֵּא, זֶה לָכֶם הָאוֹת, הַמִּזְבֵּחַ נִקְרַע הַיּוֹם מֵאֵלָיו, וְנִשְׁפַּךְ הַדֶּשֶׁן לָאָרֶץ.


**And he gave on that day.** The prophet [gave] a sign on the day that he prophesied, [saying,] “This is your sign, the altar will split by itself today, and the ashes will be spilled onto the ground.”



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on I Kings 13:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:4:1)


**וַתִּיבַשׁ יָדוֹ.** נָקַם הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא עַל כְּבוֹדוֹ שֶׁל צַדִּיק יוֹתֵר מִכְּבוֹדוֹ, עוֹמֵד וּמַקְטִיר לַעֲבוֹדַת כּוֹכָבִים לֹא יָבְשָׁה יָדוֹ, וּבִשְׁבִיל לַעַז הַצַּדִּיק יָבְשָׁה יָדוֹ.


**But his hand dried up [stiffened].** The Holy One Blessed Is He, avenged the honor of a righteous person more than His own honor. [When] he was standing and offering sacrifices to idols, his hand did not stiffen, yet, because of slandering the righteous person, his hand stiffened.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ I.e., his hand became paralyzed. Yerovom had stretched out his hand to signal his servants to seize the man of God.—Metzudas Dovid

###### Rashi on I Kings 13:6:1

**אֱלֹהֶיךָ.** וְלֹא אֱלֹהַי, עֲדַיִן עוֹמֵד בְּמִרְדּוֹ.


**Your God.** But not my God, because he was still in a state of rebellion.

**As it was originally.** Standing and burning sacrifices to idols.⁵

**וּסֳעָדָה.** אֱכֹל.

###### Rashi on I Kings 13:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:11:1)


**וְנָבִיא אֶחָד.** נְבִיא הַשֶּׁקֶר.

###### Rashi on I Kings 13:11:2

**יוֹשֵׁב בְּבֵית אֵל.** וְאֵינוֹ מִשָּׁם, אֶלָּא מִשּׁוֹמְרוֹן בָּא, וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר בְּפָרָשַׁת יֹאשִׁיָּהוּ: וַיְמַלְּטוּ עַצְמוֹתָיו אֶת עַצְמוֹת הַנָּבִיא אֲשֶׁר בָּא מִשּׁוֹמְרוֹן.


**Who lived in Beis Eil.** But he was not from there, but he came from Shomron, and so it is stated in the section dealing with Yoshiyahu, “his bones with the bones of the prophet who had come from Shomron.”⁷

⁷ II Melochim 23:18.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on I Kings 13:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:20:1)


**אֶל הַנָּבִיא.** נְבִיא הַשֶּׁקֶר.


**To the prophet.** The false prophet.


###### Rashi on I Kings 13:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:20:2)


**אֲשֶׁר הֱשִׁיבוֹ.** לָאִישׁ הָאֱלֹהִים, מִכָּאן אָמְרוּ: גְּדוֹלָה לְגִימָה שֶׁמַּשְׁרָה שְׁכִינָה עַל נְבִיאֵי הַבַּעַל.


**That caused him to return.** To the man of God. From here they [the Rabbis] deduced that [offering] refreshment is a great thing, for it causes the Divine Presence to rest on the prophets of the Baal.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ The simple act of hospitality can have great and far reaching consequences. See Maseches Sanhedrin 103b-104a.

###### Rashi on I Kings 13:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:21:1)


**וַיִּקְרָא.** הַנָּבִיא הַזָּקֵן, אֶל אִישׁ הָאֱלֹהִים.

###### Rashi on I Kings 13:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:23:1)


**לַנָּבִיא אֲשֶׁר הֱשִׁיבוֹ.** שֵׁם זֶה לִרְכֹּב עָלָיו אִישׁ הָאֱלֹהִים.


**For the prophet that he brought back.** Whom this one [had brought] there, for the man of God to ride upon.



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on I Kings 13:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:30:1)


**בְּקִבְרוֹ.** בְּבֵית קְבוּרָה שֶׁהָיָה לִנְבִיא הַשֶּׁקֶר הַזֶּה בָּעִיר.


**In his own grave.** In the graveyard belonging to the false prophet in the city.¹⁰

¹⁰ Alternatively, “in the grave that he had dug for himself.”—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 32

###### Rashi on I Kings 13:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:32:1)


**כִּי הָיֹה יִהְיֶה וְגוֹ'.** אוּלַי יְמַלְּטוּ עַצְמוֹתָיו אֶת עַצְמוֹתַי.

¹¹ This is indeed what happened. See below, II Melochim 23:18.



### Verse 33

###### Rashi on I Kings 13:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/13:33:1)


**אַחַר הַדָּבָר הַזֶּה.** שֶׁרָאָה אֶת הַמּוֹפֵת הַזֶּה, וְשָׁמַע אֶת דִּבְרֵי הַנָּבִיא, וְאַף עַל פִּי כֵן לֹא שָׁב. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ דָּרְשׁוּ: אַחַר שֶׁתְּפָסוֹ הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא בְּבִגְדּוֹ, וְאָמַר לוֹ: חֲזֹר בְּךָ, וַאֲנִי וְאַתָּה וּבֶן יִשַׁי נְטַיֵּל בְּגַן עֵדֶן.


**[Even] after all that transpired.** That he saw this wonder and heard the words of the prophet, and he, nevertheless, did not repent. And our Rabbis expounded, “After the Holy One Blessed Is He, grasped him by his garment and said to him, ‘Repent, and I and you and the son of Yishay will stroll in the Garden of Eden.’”¹²

footnotes:
¹² See Maseches Sanhedrin 102a. The conversation continued, “Yerovom said, ‘Who will walk in front?’ [and the reply was,] ‘The son of Yishy.’ [Yerovom responded,] ‘If so, I want no part of it.’”



## Chapter 14


### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Kings 14:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:3:1)


**וְנִקֻּדִים.** מִין קְלָיוֹת, מְיַבְּשִׁין בְּתַנּוּר וְעוֹשִׂין אוֹתָן קֶמַח לַעֲשׂוֹת מִמֶּנּוּ מַאֲכָל, שֶׁקּוֹרִין לוֹ שְׁתִיתָא בִּלְשׁוֹן גְּמָרָא.


**Biscuits.** A type of parched grain, dried in an oven, and they make flour from it to make it into a dish called שתיתא, in the language of the Gemara.¹

footnotes:
¹ It was a custom in those days to give a prophet a present before asking him a question. See I Shmuel 9:7,8.—Ralbag



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on I Kings 14:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:5:1)


**וְהִיא מִתְנַכֵּרָה.** כְּאִלּוּ אֵינָהּ זֹאת.


**She will be in disguise.** As though it was not she.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Kings 14:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:6:1)


**שָׁלוּחַ אֵלַיִךְ.** מֵאֵת הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא בִּשְׁלִיחוּת קָשָׁה.


**Sent to tell you.** From the Holy One Blessed Is He, with a harsh message.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on I Kings 14:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:10:1)


**מַשְׁתִּין בְּקִיר.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: יָדַע מַדָּע, מֵשִׁית בְּקִירוֹת לִבּוֹ.


**Male child.** [Targum] Yonoson rendered, “one who knows knowledge,” [i.e.,] he who places it into the walls of his heart.²³

footnotes:
² See I Shmuel 25:22.
³ Alternatively, משתין בקיר refers to a dog who urinates on walls, i.e., the prophet is prophesying that the destruction will be so severe, that so much as a dog will not be left when the house of Yerovom is destroyed.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Kings 14:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:10:2)


**עָצוּר וְעָזוּב.** עָצוּר שֶׁיֵּשׁ לוֹ מַחֲזִיק יָד, כְּמוֹ: זֶה יַעְצֹר בְּעַמִּי, וְעָזוּב, שֶׁאֵין לוֹ מַחֲזִיק בַּיָּד.


**Any that are restrained and that are abandoned.** עצור is one who has someone restraining him, as in, “This one will rule over My people.”⁴ And עזוב is one who has no one restraining him.

footnotes:
⁴ I Shmuel 9:17.


###### Rashi on I Kings 14:10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:10:3)


**כַּאֲשֶׁר יְבַעֵר.** הַמְבַעֵר אֶת הַמַּאֲכָל בְּפִיו, לַעֲשׂוֹת גָּלָל, כָּךְ אֲבָעֵר אַחֲרָיו עַד תֻּמּוֹ.


**As one removes.** As the one who digests food which [eventually] becomes dung,⁵ so will I expunge after him until he is totally consumed.⁶

footnotes:
⁵ Alternatively, גלל means “tooth,” which consumes food completely.—Radak
⁶ Alternatively, “I will completely remove the house of Yerovom, as one who sweeps out dung until it is thoroughly cleaned out.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Kings 14:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:13:1)


**דָּבָר טוֹב.** שֶׁבִּטֵּל מִשְׁמַרְתּוֹ, שֶׁהוֹשִׁיבוֹ אָבִיו שׁוֹמֵר שֶׁלֹּא יַעֲלֶה אִישׁ לָרֶגֶל, וּבִטֵּל מִשְׁמַרְתּוֹ וְעָלָה.


**Something good.** He abandoned his watch; for his father had stationed him as a sentry so that no person would go up [to Yerusholayim] on the pilgrimage festival. He abandoned his watch and went up.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ See Maseches Mo’ed Katan 28b.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on I Kings 14:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:14:1)


**אֲשֶׁר יַכְרִית אֶת בֵּית יָרָבְעָם זֶה הַיּוֹם וּמֶה גַּם עַתָּה.** הַנּוֹלָדִים לוֹ הַיּוֹם, וַאֲשֶׁר עֲתִידִין לְהִוָּלֵד לוֹ מֵעַתָּה, כָּךְ תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן.


**Who will cut off the house of Yerovom this day and also those that will be born.** Those who are already born to him this day, and those who are destined to be born to him from now on, so did [Targum] Yonoson render.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on I Kings 14:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:21:1)


**הָעִיר אֲשֶׁר בָּחַר ה'.** וְאַף עַל פִּי כֵן:


**The city that Adonoy had chosen.** Yet, even so,



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on I Kings 14:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:22:1)


**וַיַּעַשׂ יְהוּדָה הָרָע.** וְלֹא נָתְנוּ זֶה עַל לִבָּם.


**And Yehudah did what was evil.** And they did not pay attention to this.


###### Rashi on I Kings 14:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:22:2)


**וַיְקַנְאוּ.** לְשׁוֹן קְנִיטָה.


**And they angered Him.** An expression of provocation.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on I Kings 14:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:23:1)


**גַּם הֵמָּה.** כִּשְׁאָר הַשְּׁבָטִים.


**They too.** Like the other tribes.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on I Kings 14:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:24:1)


**קָדֵשׁ.** נִאוּף.


**Prostitution.** Prostitution.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on I Kings 14:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:25:1)


**שִׁישַׁק.** מָצִינוּ בְּמִדְרַשׁ (שִׁיר הַשִּׁירִים) הוּא פַּרְעֹה נְכֹה, וְנִקְרָא שִׁישַׁק, עַל שֶׁהָיָה שׁוֹקֵק וּמִתְאַוֶּה כָּל יָמָיו לְכִסֵּא הַשֵּׁן שֶׁהָיָה לִשְׁלֹמֹה חֲתָנוֹ, וְעַכְשָׁיו עָלָה וּלְקָחוֹ.


**Shishak.** We find in Midrash Shir Hashirim that this was Pharaoh Neco. He was called Shishak because all his life he longed for [=שוקק] the ivory throne that belonged to Shlomo his son-in-law, and now, he went up and took it.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on I Kings 14:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:26:1)


**וְאֶת הַכֹּל לָקַח.** אֶת הַכִּסֵּא, הֶחָבִיב מִן הַכֹּל.


**He took everything.** The throne, that was dearer to him than everything.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on I Kings 14:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/14:28:1)


**יִשָּׂאוּם הָרָצִים.** לְפָנָיו לְכָבוֹד, וְאַחַר כָּךְ יְשִׁיבוּם אֶל תָּא שֶׁהָרָצִים עוֹמְדִים שָׁם תָּמִיד.


**The runners would carry them.** Before him, in [his] honor,⁸ and afterwards, they would return them⁹ to the chamber where the runners were always stationed.

footnotes:
⁸ Alternatively, it may have been done as protection against a rebellion.—Radak
⁹ I.e., the copper shields.



## Chapter 15


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on I Kings 15:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:1:1)


**וּבִשְׁנַת שְׁמוֹנֶה עֶשְׂרֵה לַמֶּלֶךְ יָרָבְעָם.** שָׁנָה אַחַת אַחַר מוֹת רְחַבְעָם, וּבִשְׁנַת עֶשְׂרִים לְיָרָבְעָם מָלַךְ אָסָא, שָׁנָה הַשְּׁלִישִׁית שֶׁל נָדָב (אֲבִיָּם) נִמְנֵית לַמֶּלֶךְ אָסָא שָׁנָה, וְלוֹ נַמֵּי נִמְנֵית.


**In the eighteenth year [of the reign] of King Yerovom.** One year after Rechovom’s death, in the twentieth year of Yerovom, Asa reigned. The third year of Nodov (Aviyam) is counted for King Asa as a year, and for him it is also counted.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Kings 15:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:12:1)


**וַיַּעֲבֵר הַקְּדֵשִׁים.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: וּפַלֵּי נָפְקַת בְּרָא.


**And he rid [the land] of prostitutes.** [Targum] Yonoson rendered, “and he abolished the prostitutes.”



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Kings 15:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:13:1)


**וַיְסִרֶה מִגְּבִירָה.** מִהְיוֹת גְּבִירָה.


**He removed from her position of authority.** From being queen.


###### Rashi on I Kings 15:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:13:2)


**אֲשֶׁר עָשְׂתָה מִפְלֶצֶת.** אָמְרוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ מַפְלִיא לֵיצָנוּתָא, כְּמִין זַכְרוּת עָשְׂתָה לָּהּ, וְהִיא הָיְתָה נִבְעֶלֶת לוֹ בְּכָל יוֹם.


**For she had made a fearful image.** Our Rabbis stated [that מפלצת is a combination of] מפליא ליצונתא [=extreme lasciviousness]. She made for it a phallic symbol,¹ and she would copulate with it every day.²

footnotes:
¹ Alternatively, an image that casts fear and dread unto its worshipers.—Radak
² Maseches Avodah Zarah 44a.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on I Kings 15:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:14:1)


**וְהַבָּמוֹת לֹא סָרוּ.** בָּמוֹת הַיָּחִיד, שֶׁהֻרְגְּלוּ לִקָּרֵב עֲלֵיהֶן לַשָּׁמַיִם מִשֶּׁחָרְבָה שִׁילֹה עַד שֶׁנִּבְנָה הַבַּיִת שֶׁהָיוּ הַבָּמוֹת מֻתָּרוֹת, לֹא סָרוּ עַתָּה, וְאַף עַל פִּי שֶׁנֶּאֶסְרוּ מִשֶּׁנִּבְנָה הַבַּיִת, וְהָיוּ עֲנוּשִׁים עָלֶיהָ כָּרֵת.


**[However] the [private] altars he did not remove.** The private altars upon which they had become accustomed to sacrifice to Heaven since Shiloh had been destroyed until the Beis [Hamikdosh] was built, when private altars were permissible, were not removed now, even though they were forbidden once the Beis [Hamikdosh] was built, and they were punished by premature death because of it.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on I Kings 15:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:22:1)


**אֵין נָקִי.** אֲפִלּוּ חָתָן מֵחֶדְרוֹ, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בּוֹ: נָקִי יִהְיֶה לְבֵיתוֹ שָׁנָה אֶחָת.


**There will be no exceptions.** Even a bridegroom from his chamber,³ concerning whom it is stated, “he shall be free for his home one year.”⁴

footnotes:
³ Not even Torah scholars were exempt and Asa was punished for this. See Maseches Sotah 10a.
⁴ Devarim 24:5.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on I Kings 15:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:25:1)


**שְׁנָתַיִם.** הַשְּׁנִיָּה וְהַשְּׁלִישִׁית לְאָסָא.


**Two years.** The second and the third [year] of Asa.



### Verse 33

###### Rashi on I Kings 15:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/15:33:1)


**בִּשְׁנַת שָׁלשׁ לְאָסָא מֶלֶךְ יְהוּדָה מָלַךְ בַּעְשָׁא.** שָׁנָה הַשְּׁלִישִׁית לְאָסָא, נִמְנֵית לְנָדָב וּלְבַּעְשָׁא.


**It was during the third year of Asa king of Yeudah, Bashah son of Achiyoh became king.** The third year of Asa is counted for Nodov and Bashah.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ The in year in which Basha killed Nodov was during the third year of Asa’s [above v. 28] reign, and it is counted for both kings.



## Chapter 16


### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Kings 16:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:7:1)


**וְגַם בְּיַד יֵהוּא בֶן חֲנָנִי הַנָּבִיא דְּבַר ה' הָיָה אֶל בַּעְשָׁא.** כְּלוֹמַר הֲרֵי שֶׁמָּלַךְ אֵלָה בֶּן בַּעְשָׁא, וְאַף גַּם זֹאת דְּבַר ה' הָיָה בְּיַד יֵהוּא, שֶׁלֹּא תִּתְקַיֵּם מַלְכוּתוֹ.


**Also, in the hand of Yeihu the son of Chanoni the prophet, there was a word of Adonoy concerning Bashah.** I.e., behold Eilah son of Bashah reigned, nevertheless, the word of Adonoy through Yeihu was that his kingdom would not last.¹

footnotes:
¹ This verse is a continuation of Yeihu’s prophecy contained in v.1-4 above, therefore it begins with “Also.”—Metzudas Dovid


###### Rashi on I Kings 16:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:7:2)


**וְעַל אֲשֶׁר הִכָּה אוֹתוֹ.** הוֹאִיל וְגַם הוּא הָלַךְ בְּחַטֹּאתָיו, נִמְצָא שֶׁלֹּא הָיָה לוֹ לְהָרְגוֹ, לְכָךְ נֶעֱנַשׁ עַל הֲרִיגָתוֹ, וְכֵן מָצִינוּ: וּפָקַדְתִּי אֶת דְּמֵי יִזְרְעֶאל עַל בֵּית יֵהוּא, הוֹאִיל וְלֹא סָרוּ מִדַּרְכֵי אַחְאָב, נֶעֶנְשׁוּ עַל הֲרִיגָתָם.


**And because he had killed him [Nodov].** Since he too, committed the same sins, consequently he should not have killed him, therefore he was punished for his murder. And similarly we find, “And I will avenge the blood of Yizre’el upon the house of Yeihu,”² because they did not turn away from Achov’s ways, they were punished for his murder.

footnotes:
² Hoshe’a 1:4.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Kings 16:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:9:1)


**שַׂר מַחֲצִית הָרָכֶב.** הוּא וְאַחֵר הָיוּ שָׂרִים עַל הָרֶכֶב.


**He was the officer of half the chariots.** He and another were captains over the chariots.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on I Kings 16:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:11:1)


**וְגֹאֲלָיו וְרֵעֵהוּ.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) וּקְרִיבוֹהִי וְרִחֲמוֹהִי.


**Nor any of his relatives or friends.** [Targum Yonoson rendered, “neither] his kinfolk nor his friends.”³

footnotes:
³ Alternatively, “his friend,” singular, meaning his confidant.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on I Kings 16:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:22:1)


**וַיָּמָת תִּבְנִי.** כְּשֶׁנָּשָׂא אָסָא בִּתּוֹ שֶׁל עָמְרִי לִיהוֹשָׁפָט בְּנוֹ, הָרְגוּ אֶת תִּבְנִי, כְּשֶׁרָאוּ חֲשִׁיבוּתוֹ שֶׁל עָמְרִי, בְּסֵדֶר עוֹלָם.


**And Tivni died.** When Asa took Omri’s daughter in marriage for Yehoshofot his son, they killed Tivni,⁴ when they saw Omri’s prominence; according to Seder Olam.

footnotes:
⁴ Alternatively, he died a natural death and thereby Omri ruled undisputedly.—Radak See v. 23 below and Rashi there.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on I Kings 16:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:23:1)


**בִּשְׁנַת שְׁלשִׁים וְאַחַת שָׁנָה לְאָסָא וְגוֹ' מָלַךְ עָמְרִי.** מַלְכוּת שְׁלֵמָה, וַחֲמֵשׁ שָׁנִים הָיְתָה מַחֲלֻקְתּוֹ שֶׁל תִּבְנִי, מֵעֶשְׂרִים וְשֶׁבַע לְאָסָא, עַד שְׁלשִׁים וְאַחַת.


**In the thirty-first year of the reign of Asa, etc., Omri reigned.** An undisputed reign. His conflict with Tivni lasted five years, from the twenty-seventh year of Asa[’s reign] until the thirty-first [year].


###### Rashi on I Kings 16:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:23:2)


**שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה שָׁנָה.** סַךְ הַכֹּל, חָמֵשׁ בַּמַּחֲלֹקֶת וְשֶׁבַע בְּמַלְכוּת שְׁלֵמָה, וְאִי אֶפְשָׁר לוֹמַר שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה בְּמַלְכוּת שְׁלֵמָה, שֶׁהֲרֵי אַחְאָב בְּנוֹ מָלַךְ בִּשְׁנַת שְׁלשִׁים וּשְׁמוֹנֶה לְאָסָא.


**For twelve years.** Was the total [of his reign], five [years] were in conflict and seven [years] his reign was undisputed. It is impossible to say that his reign was undisputed for twelve years, because Achov his son reigned in the thirty-eighth year of Asa[’s reign].


###### Rashi on I Kings 16:23:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:23:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:23:3)


**בְּתִרְצָה מָלַךְ שֵׁשׁ שָׁנִים.** עַד שֶׁלֹּא בָּנָה אֶת שׁוֹמְרוֹן.


**In Tirtzoh he reigned six years.** Before he had built Shomron.



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on I Kings 16:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:31:1)


**וַיַּעֲבֹד אֶת הַבַּעַל.** מוֹסִיף עַל עֲגָלִים שֶׁל יָרָבְעָם.


**And worshiped the [idol] Baal.** In addition to Yerovom’s calves.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ The Gemara in Maseches Sanhedrin 102b states that Achov’s minor sins were like Yerovom’s major ones. If so, why are Yerovom’s sins emphasized here? Because he opened the way to sin for the Bnei Yisroel.



### Verse 33

###### Rashi on I Kings 16:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:33:1)


**אֲשֵׁרָה.** אִילָן הַנֶּעֱבָד.


**An *Asheirah*.** A tree that is worshiped.⁶.—Radak

footnotes:
⁶ In addition to the Baal, he also worshiped the Asheirah



### Verse 34

###### Rashi on I Kings 16:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:34:1)


**חִיאֵל בֵּית הָאֱלִי.** חִיאֵל אֲשֶׁר מִבֵּית אֵל, כְּמוֹ: יִשַׁי בֵּית הַלַּחְמִי, וְלֹא נֶאֱמַר הַבֵּית לַחְמִי; וְכֵן יְהוֹשֻׁעַ בֵּית הַשִּׁמְשִׁי, בְּסֵפֶר שְׁמוּאֵל, וְלֹא הַבֵּית שִׁמְשִׁי; וְכֵן בְּעָפְרַת אֲבִי הָעֶזְרִי וְלֹא נֶאֱמַר הָאֲבִי עֶזְרִי. וְיֵשׁ פּוֹתְרִין: בֵּית הָאֱלִי, עַל שֶׁקִּבֵּל עָלָיו קִלְלוֹת יְהוֹשֻׁעַ: אָרוּר הָאִישׁ אֲשֶׁר יָקוּם וּבָנָה וְגוֹ'. אָמְרוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ חִיאֵל מִשֶּׁל (בִּנְיָמִין) וִירִיחוֹ מִשֶּׁל יְהוֹשָׁפָט (בִּנְיָמִין) וּמִפְּנֵי מָה תְלָאוֹ בְּאַחְאָב, שֶׁתּוֹלִין אֶת הַקְּלָלָה בִּמְקֻלְקָל (סְפָרִים אֲחֵרִים: בִּמְקֻלָּל).


**Chieil of Beis Eil.** [I.e.,] Chieil who was from Beis Eil, similar to, “Yishay of Beis Lechem [בית הלחמי],”⁷ and it does not state הבית לחמי, and similarly, “Yehoshua of Beis Shemesh [בית השמשי],”⁸ in the Book of Shmuel, and [it does] not [state] הבית שמשי, and similarly, “In Ofrah [that belonged to Yoash] of the Avi Ezri [אבי העזרי],”⁹ and it does not state האבי עזרי. Some interpret בית האלי [is from the word אלה or curse], since he accepted upon himself Yehoshua’s curses, “Cursed [before Adonoy] be the man who rises up and rebuilds, etc.”¹⁰ Our Rabbis stated that Chieil was of [the tribe of] Binyomin, and Yericho belonged to Yehoshofot¹¹ [King of Yehudah]. Why does [Scripture] relate him to Achov [King of Yisroel], because a curse is related to a corrupt person.¹² (Other texts: To a cursed person.)

footnotes:
⁷ I Shmuel 16:1.
⁸ I Shmuel 6:14.
⁹ Shoftim 6:11.
¹⁰ Yehoshua 6:26. Also, see Maseches Sanhedrin 113a.
¹¹ Who was of the tribe of Binyomin.
¹² Talmud Yerushalmi Maseches Sanhedrin 10:2. Alternatively, even though Achov witnessed Divine retribution visited upon Chieil, Achov nonetheless did not repent.—Metzudas Dovid.


###### Rashi on I Kings 16:34:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:34:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/16:34:2)


**בַּאֲבִירָם בְּכֹרוֹ יִסְּדָהּ.** כְּשֶׁיִּסְדָהּ, מֵת בְּנוֹ בְּכוֹרוֹ, וְקָבַר וְהָלַךְ וְקָבַר כָּל בָּנָיו, עַד שֶׁמֵּת הָאַחֲרוֹן בְּהַצָּבַת דְּלָתוֹת, וְכֵן הוּא קְלָלוֹת שֶׁל יְהוֹשֻׁעַ: בִּבְכוֹרוֹ יְיַסְּדֶנָּה וּבִצְעִירוֹ יַצִּיב דְּלָתֶיהָ.


**With Avirom his first born he laid the foundation.** When he laid its foundation, his firstborn son died, and he buried [him] and continued to bury all his sons until the last one died when he was installing its doors. And, these were Yehoshua’s curses, “With [the loss of] his firstborn shall he lay its foundation, and with [the loss of] his youngest child he will set up its gates.”¹³

footnotes:
¹³ Yehoshua 6:26.



## Chapter 17


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on I Kings 17:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:1:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר אֵלִיָּהוּ הַתִּשְׁבִּי.** מֵאֶרֶץ (סְפָרִים אֲחֵרִים: שֵׁם הָעִיר) שֶׁשְּׁמָהּ תּוֹשָׁב.


**Eliyahu haTishbi said.** From a land (Other texts: The name of the city) named Toshav.


###### Rashi on I Kings 17:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:1:2)


**חַי ה'.** לָמָּה סְמָכוֹ כָּאן, אֶלָּא שֶׁהָלְכוּ אֵלִיָּהוּ וְאַחְאָב לְנַחֵם אֶת חִיאֵל בְּאֶבְלוֹ, אָמַר אַחְאָב לְאֵלִיָּהוּ: אֶפְשָׁר שֶׁקִּלְלַת הַתַּלְמִיד נִתְקַיְּמָה, וְקִלְלַת משֶׁה רַבֵּנוּ לֹא נִתְקַיְּמָה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וְסַרְתֶּם וַעֲבַדְתֶּם אֱלֹהִים אֲחֵרִים וְהִשְׁתַּחֲוִיתֶם לָהֶם, וְחָרָה אַף ה' בָּכֶם וְעָצַר אֶת הַשָּׁמָיִם, וַהֲרֵי כָּל יִשְׂרָאֵל עוֹבְדִין עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה, וְאֵין גְּשָׁמִים נֶעֱצָרִים, מִיָּד וַיֹּאמֶר אֵלִיָּהוּ וְגוֹ'.


**As Adonoy lives.** Why was this placed here? For Eliyahu and Achov went to console Chieil in his mourning. Achov said to Eliyahu, “Is it possible that the student’s curse was fulfilled while Moshe our teacher’s curse was not fulfilled, as it is stated, “And you turn away and serve other gods, and bow down to them, Adonoy’s wrath will be aroused against you, and He will restrain the skies [and there will be no rain].”¹ Now, all Yisroel are worshiping idols, yet, the rains are not being withheld. Immediately, Eliyahu said, etc.² ³

footnotes:
¹ Devarim 11:17,18.
² Maseches Sanhedrin 113a.
³ Eliyahu intended that by withholding the rain and dew, some or all of the people would repent from their idol worship.—Radak



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Kings 17:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:3:1)


**וְנִסְתַּרְתָּ.** מִפְּנֵי אַחְאָב וְאִיזֶבֶל.


**And hide.** From Achov and Ezevel.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ No one should know where you are.—Radak



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Kings 17:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:7:1)


**וַיִּבַשׁ הַנַּחַל.** כְּדֵי שֶׁיֵּדַע צֹרֶךְ הַגְּשָׁמִים וְיַטְרִיחַ לְגָּלוּת, שֶׁהָיָה קָשֶׁה בְּעֵינֵי הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא שֶׁיִּשְׂרָאֵל שְׁרוּיִין בְּרָעָב.


**The brook dried up.** In order that he realize the need for rain and trouble himself to go into exile, for it was displeasing to the Holy One Blessed Is He, that Yisroel should be living in hunger.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ See Maseches Sanhedrin 113a and Rashi there.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Kings 17:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:9:1)


**צָרְפָתָה.** שֵׁם הָעִיר.


**To Tzorphas.** [Tzorphas was] the name of the city.


###### Rashi on I Kings 17:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:9:2)


**צִוִּיתִי שָׁם.** בְּפָמַלְיָא שֶׁלִּי, שֶׁתְּכַלְכֶּלְךָ שָׁם אִשָּׁה אַלְמָנָה.


**I have commanded there.** In My retinue, that a widow will feed you there.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on I Kings 17:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:10:1)


**קְחִי נָא לִי מְעַט מַיִם.** הוּא לֹא הָיָה מַכִּיר בָּאַלְמָנָה שֶׁאָמַר לוֹ הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא, וְלָמַד מֵאֱלִיעֶזֶר עֶבֶד אַבְרָהָם, וְאָמַר: אוֹתָהּ שֶׁתִּתֵּן לִי מַיִם לִשְׁתּוֹת, הִיא הָאַלְמָנָה.


**Please bring me a little water.** He was unable to identify the widow⁶ about whom the Holy One Blessed Is He, had told him. But he learned from Eliezer, the servant of Avrohom, and he said, “The one who gives me water to drink, she is the widow.”

footnotes:
⁶ According to the Midrash, this widow was the mother of Yonah the son of Amitai, the prophet.—Radak



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Kings 17:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:12:1)


**מָעוֹג.** כְּמוֹ עוּגָה.


**Cake.** [מעוג means] the same as עוגה [=cake].


###### Rashi on I Kings 17:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:12:2)


**מְלֹא כַף.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: מְלֵי פִּיסַת יְדָא.


**A handful.** [Targum] Yonoson rendered, “the palm of a handful.”


###### Rashi on I Kings 17:12:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:12:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:12:3)


**בַצַּפָּחַת.** ביטויל"א בְּלַעַ"ז (בַּקְבּוּקוֹן), כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בְּשָׁאוּל: צַפַּחַת הַמַּיִם אֲשֶׁר מֵרַאֲשׁוֹתָיו.


**In a bottle.** *Bouteille*, in O.F., as it is stated regarding Shaul, “the flask of water that was at [Shaul’s] head.”⁷

footnotes:
⁷ I Shmuel 26:12.


###### Rashi on I Kings 17:12:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:12:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:12:4)


**וָמָתְנוּ.** מִשָּׁם וּלְהַלָּן נָמוּת בָּרָעָב.


**We will die.** From then on,⁸ we will die of hunger.

footnotes:
⁸ I.e., because after we eat it there is nothing else left for us to eat so ultimately we will die of hunger.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Kings 17:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:13:1)


**עֻגָה קְטַנָּה בָרִאשֹׁנָה.** מִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה יֵשׁ, (לָמָּה בָּרִאשׁוֹנָה) בְּוַתֹּאמֶר לֵאָה בָּגָד.


**A small cake first.** There is a Midrash Aggdah in Bereishis Rabboh⁹ (why he wanted it first) on [the verse], “And Leah said, ‘Unexpected success has come.’”¹⁰

footnotes:
⁹ The Midrash cited by Rashi is not found in our texts of Midrash Rabboh on this verse.
¹⁰ Bereishis 30:11.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on I Kings 17:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:17:1)


**חָלָה בֶּן הָאִשָּׁה.** כְּדֵי שֶׁיִּצְטָרֵךְ לְמַפְתֵּחַ שֶׁל תְחִיַּת הַמֵּתִים, כִּדְאִיתָא בְשִׁלְהֵי אַגַּדְתָּא דְּחֵלֶק.


**Illness struck the son of the woman.** So that he should need the Key of Resurrection of the Dead, as stated at the end of the Aggadah of [the chapter entitled] “Cheilek.”¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ Maseches Sanhedrin 113a.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on I Kings 17:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:18:1)


**לְהַזְכִּיר אֶת עֲוֹנִי.** עַד שֶׁלֹּא בָּאתָ אֵלַי, הָיוּ שׁוֹקְלִין מַעֲשַׂי וּמַעֲשֵׂה עִירִי, וְהָיִיתִי רְאוּיָה לְנֵס, מִשֶּׁבָּאתָ לְכָאן, לֹא נֶחְשַׁבְתִּי לִכְלוּם, וְאֵין צִדְקָתִי נִכֶּרֶת (סְפָרִים אֲחֵרִים: נִזְכֶּרֶת), וְכֵן בְּלוֹט הוּא אוֹמֵר: וְאָנֹכִי לֹא אוּכַל לְהִמָּלֵט הָהָרָה, אֵצֶל אַבְרָהָם, שֶׁלֹּא תְּהֵא זְכוּתִי נִזְכֶּרֶת אֶצְלוֹ.


**To cause my sins to be remembered.** Before you came, they would weigh my deeds and the deeds of my city, and I was worthy of a miracle. But since you came here, I am not reckoned with as [being worthy of] anything and my righteousness is not noticeable.¹² ¹³ (Other texts: Remembered) And similarly, concerning Lot it states, “I cannot escape to the mountain,”¹⁴ [i.e., referring] to Avrohom, for my merit will not be remembered next to his.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹² Before you came I was no worse than my neighbors, but compared to you I am a sinner.—Ralbag
¹³ Alternatively, she said that her sins were being remembered because she had not properly served Eliyahu, and that is why her son died.—Radak
¹⁴ Bereishis 19:19.
¹⁵ Bereishis Rabboh 50:11.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on I Kings 17:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/17:21:1)


**וַיִּתְמֹדֵד.** מִלְּשׁוֹן מִדָּה, נִשְׁתַּטַּח עָלָיו.


**He spread himself out.** [ויתמודד is] an expression of מדה, [i.e.,] he spread himself upon him.¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁶ Eliyahu prostrated himself in the same manner as Elisha did, in the case of Shunamis’ son. He placed his eyes on the youth’s eyes, and his mouth on the youth’s mouth, in II Melochim 4:34.—Radak

**וַיְהִי בְּהַכְרִית אִיזֶבֶל.** לְהַגִּיד בָּא אֵיךְ הָיָה עוֹבַדְיָהוּ יָרֵא אֶת ה'.


**And when Ezevel killed.** [Scripture] comes to tell [us] how Ovadyahu feared God.


###### Rashi on I Kings 18:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:4:2)

**Fifty men in a cave.** And fifty men in another cave.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:5:1)


**וְלוֹא נַכְרִית מֵהַבְּהֵמָה.** לֹא תִכָּרֵת כָּל בְּהֶמְתֵּנוּ מִמֶּנּוּ.


**And we will not be bereft of [our] animals.** All our animals will not be cut off from us.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:9:1

**לַהֲמִיתֵנִי.** כְּמוֹ שֶׁמְּפָרֵשׁ וְאוֹמֵר: וְרוּחַ ה' יִשָּׂאֲךָ עַל אֲשֶׁר לֹא אֵדַע.


**To kill me.** As it [continues and] explains and states, “a wind from Adonoy will carry you away to where I do not know.”¹

**That they did not find you.** For the nation was unable to find you.²

**שְׁתֵּי הַסְּעִפִּים.** שְׁתֵּי הַמַּחֲשָׁבוֹת, שֶׁאֵינְכֶם מְבִינִים לְהַכְרִיעַ מִי הָאֱלֹהִים.


**Two concepts.** Two thoughts, because you do not know how to decide who is God.

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:21:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:21:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:21:3)


**וְלֹא עָנוּ הָעָם אוֹתוֹ דָּבָר.** שֶׁלֹּא הָיוּ יוֹדְעִין לְהַבְחִין.


**And the people did not answer him a word.** Because they did not know how to distinguish [between the two].³

### Verse 26

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:26:1)


**אֲשֶׁר נָתַן לָהֶם.** שֶׁהָיָה נִשְׁמַט מֵהֶם, שֶׁלֹּא יִקָּרֵב לַעֲבוֹדָה זָרָה, וּבָרַח לוֹ תַּחַת כְּנָפָיו שֶׁל אֵלִיָּהוּ, אָמַר לוֹ: לֵךְ, בִּשְׁנֵיכֶם יִתְקַדֵּשׁ הַמָּקוֹם.


**That he gave them.** For it slipped away from them so that it would not be offered to the idol, and it ran under Eliyahu’s cloak.⁴ He said to the ox, “Go, for through the both of you the Omnipresent will be sanctified.”⁵

footnotes:
⁴ I.e., they were undecided.
⁵ And ultimately it was Eliyahu who “gave” the ox to the prophets of the Baal. Alternatively, Achov supplied the oxen, and they chose the one they wanted. Achov then “gave” them the ox they chose.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Kings 18:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:26:2)


**וַיִּפְסְחוּ עַל הַמִּזְבֵּחַ אֲשֶׁר עָשָׂה.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: וְאִשְׁתַּטּוּ, הָיוּ מְרַקְּדִין כְּמִשְׁפָּטָן עַל מִזְבְּחוֹתָם.


**And they jumped on the altar that he had made.** [Targum] Yonoson rendered, “and they raved [ואשתטו], [i.e.,] they were dancing according to their custom, on their altar.


###### Rashi on I Kings 18:26:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:26:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:26:3)


**וַיִּפְסְחוּ.** כְּמוֹ וַיִּפְסְעוּ.


**And they jumped.** [ויפסחו means] the same as ויפסעו⁶ [=they stepped].

⁶ Bamidbar Rabboh 23:9.

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:27:1)


**כִּי אֱלֹהִים הוּא.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) אֲרֵי אַתּוּן אָמְרִין דַּחֲלָא הוּא.


**For he is a god.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “for you say he is one to be feared.”


###### Rashi on I Kings 18:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:27:2)


**כִּי שִׂיחַ וְכִי שִׂיג לוֹ.** שֶׁמָּא יֵשׁ לוֹ דָּבָר עִם יוֹעֲצָיו עַכְשָׁיו, אוֹ מַשִּׂיג וְרוֹדֵף הוּא עַכְשָׁיו בַּמִּלְחָמָה.


**Perhaps he is conversing or pursuing.** Maybe he is conferring with his advisors now, or he is overtaking and pursuing [his enemies] in war.


###### Rashi on I Kings 18:27:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:27:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:27:3)


**וְכִי דֶרֶךְ לוֹ.** לְבֵית הַכִּסֵּא.


**Or is traveling.** To the bathroom.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:28:1

**וַיִּתְגֹּדְדוּ.** לְשׁוֹן שְׂרִיטָה בִּכְלִי, וְכֵן הָיָה נִימוּסֵיהֶן.


**And they gashed themselves.** An expression of tearing the flesh with an instrument, for such was their custom.

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:29:1)


**וַיִּתְנַבְּאוּ.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) וְאִשְׁתַּטִּיאוּ.


**They continued to prophesy.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “and they raved.”



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:30:1)


**וַיְרַפֵּא אֶת מִזְבַּח ה' הֶהָרוּס.** בָּנָה מִזְבֵּחַ, וְהִזְכִּיר לְיִשְׂרָאֵל לִהְיוֹת מִזְבַּח ה' עוֹלֶה עַל לִבָּם וְשָׁגוּר בְּפִיהֶם, שֶׁהָיָה הָרוּס וְנִפְגָּר וּבָטֵל שְׁמוֹ וְהַזְכָּרָתוֹ מִפִּי כָּל עֲשֶׂרֶת הַשְּׁבָטִים, כָּךְ שָׁמַעְתִּי פְּשׁוּטוֹ. וּמִדְרָשׁוֹ שָׁמַעְתִּי, שֶׁשָּׁאוּל בָּנָה מִזְבֵּחַ בְּהַר הַכַּרְמֶל, וְזֶהוּ שֶׁאָמַר: בָּא שָׁאוּל הַכַּרְמֶלָה וְהִנֵּה מַצִּיב לוֹ יָד, וּלְהַלָּן הוּא אוֹמֵר: וַיַּצֵּב שָׁם מִזְבֵּחַ, וּמַלְכֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל הָרְסוּ כָּל הַמִּזְבְּחוֹת וְהַבָּמוֹת שֶׁבְּאַרְצָם שֶׁנַּעֲשָׂה לְשֵׁם שָׁמַיִם, וּבָנָה אֵלִיָּהוּ מִזְבֵּחַ זֶה שֶׁל שָׁאוּל, שֶׁהָיָה הָרוּס.


**And he repaired the altar of Adonoy that had been broken down.** He built an altar⁷ and reminded Yisroel that God’s altar should enter their thoughts and should be oft mentioned, for it was torn down and destroyed, and its name and its mention had ceased from the mouth of all the ten tribes. Thus I heard is its plain interpretation.⁸ And its Midrashic interpretation I heard, is that Shaul built an altar on Mount Carmel, and that is what it states, “Shaul came to Carmel, and is setting up a place for himself,”⁹ and there it states, “and he erected an altar there.”¹⁰ But the kings of Yisroel tore down all the altars and high places in their land that were made in the Name of Heaven, and Eliyahu built this altar of Shaul that had been torn down.

footnotes:
⁷ A ‘ח’ and an ‘ע’ are both guttural letters and are therefore interchangeable.
⁸ Although once the Beis Hamikdosh was built private altars were not permitted, however, extraordinary circumstances allow for the suspension of certain laws in cases of dire necessity, such as this. See Maseches Sanhedrin 89b and Tosafos there. Also see footnote on v. 36 below.
⁹ Alternatively, as a result of the miracle on Mount Carmel, many people repented and went to Yerusholayim to offer sacrifices in the Beis Hamikdosh. Thus he reactivated [וירפא] the altar which had been dormant [ההרוס].—Radak
¹⁰ I Shmuel 15:12.



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:31:1)


**אֲשֶׁר הָיָה דְּבַר ה' אֵלָיו לֵאמֹר יִשְׂרָאֵל יִהְיֶה שְׁמֶךָ.** לָמָּה נֶאֱמַר כָּאן, לְפִי שֶׁבְּיוֹם שֶׁנִּגְלְתָה שְׁכִינָה עַל יַעֲקֹב בְּבֵית אֵל וּקְרָאוֹ יִשְׂרָאֵל, בּוֹ בַּיּוֹם אָמַר לוֹ: גּוֹי וּקְהַל גּוֹיִם יִהְיֶה מִמֶּךָּ, עֲתִידִין בָּנֶיךָ לְהִקָּהֵל כִּשְׁאָר עַמִּים, וְלִבְנוֹת מִזְבֵּחַ לְהַקְטִיר עָלָיו בִּשְׁעַת אִסּוּר הַבָּמוֹת, וַאֲנִי מִתְרַצֶּה בַדָּבָר, בִּבְרֵאשִׁית רַבָּה.


**To whom the word of Adonoy had come, saying, “Yisroel will be your name.”** Why is this stated here? Because on the day that the Divine Presence was revealed to Yaakov in Beis Eil and named him Yisroel, on that day He said to him, “A nation and a community of nations will come from you,”¹¹ i.e., your children are destined to congregate like other nations and build an altar upon which to burn sacrifices during the period of the prohibition [of offering sacrifices on] the high places. Nevertheless, I will consent to it. [Thus is stated] in Bereishis Rabboh.¹²

footnotes:
¹¹ Bereishis 33:20.
¹² Bereishis 35:11.



### Verse 32

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:32:1)


**תְּעָלָה.** פושאד"ו בְּלַעַ"ז (חֲפִירָה).


**A ditch.** *Fosse*, in O.F.


###### Rashi on I Kings 18:32:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:32:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:32:2)


**כְּבֵית סָאתַיִם.** רָחְבּוֹ בֵּית סָאתַיִם, מֵאָה עַל חֲמִשִּׁים, כַּחֲצַר הַמִּשְׁכָּן.


**Which would contain two *se’ah*.** Its width [i.e., capacity] was as great as to hold two *se’ah*,¹³ [i.e.,] one hundred *amohs* by fifty *amohs*,¹⁴ like [the area of] the Tabernacle’s courtyard.¹⁵ by 50 *amohs*.—Radak

footnotes:
¹³ 82:5.
¹⁴ An area that accommodates the sowing of two se’ah of seed.
¹⁵ Alternatively, an area whose dimensions are 50 amohs



### Verse 34

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:34:1)


**כַּדִּים מַיִם.** לְהַגְדִּיל אֶת הַנֵּס.


**Pitchers with water.** To make the miracle even greater.


###### Rashi on I Kings 18:34:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:34:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:34:2)


**וְיִצְקוּ.** אֱלִישָׁע (יָצַק) עַל יָדָיו, וְנַעֲשׂוּ אֶצְבְּעוֹתָיו כְּמַעְיָנוֹת וְנִתְמַלְּאָה הַתְּעָלָה, הוּא שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: פֹּה אֱלִישָׁע בֶּן שָׁפָט אֲשֶׁר יָצַק מַיִם עַל יְדֵי אֵלִיָּהוּ, וְנַעֲשָׂה נֵס בַּמַּיִם עַל יָדָיו, רָאוּי הוּא שֶׁיֵּעָשֶׂה הַנֵּס בַּמַּיִם עַל יָדָיו.


**And pour them.** Elisha (poured) on his hands, and his fingers became like springs,¹⁶ and the ditch became full. This is the meaning of what is stated, “Here is Elisha the son of Shofot who poured water on Eliyahu’s hands,”¹⁷ and a miracle took place with the water through him, [i.e.,] he is worthy that a miracle should take place with water through him.¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁶ See Maseches Eruvin 23b.
¹⁷ Rashi explains from where they got the water to fill the entire ditch.
¹⁸ II Melochim 3:11.



### Verse 36

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:36:1)


**וּבִדְבָרְךָ עָשִׂיתִי.** שֶׁהִקְרַבְתִּי בַּבָּמָה בִּשְׁעַת אִסּוּר הַבָּמוֹת.


**And at Your word have I done.** That I sacrificed on a private altar during the period of the prohibition of [offering sacrifices on] private altars.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁹ This verse implies that God commanded Eliyahu to offer the sacrifice on Mount Carmel even though private altars were not permitted once the Beis Hamikdosh had been built. See footnote on v. 30 above and Tosafos in Maseches Sanhedrin 89b.



### Verse 37

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:37:1)


**עֲנֵנִי ה' עֲנֵנִי.** עֲנֵנִי בָּאֵשׁ, עֲנֵנִי בַּמָּטָר, כָּךְ תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ: עֲנֵנִי בָּאֵשׁ (וּבַמַּיִם), עֲנֵנִי שֶׁלֹּא יֹאמְרוּ מַעֲשֵׂה כְשָׁפִים הֵם.


**Answer me, Adonoy, answer me.** Answer me with fire, answer me with rain.²⁰ So did [Targum] Yonoson render this. But our Rabbis said, “Answer me with fire, (and water) answer me so that they do not say that this deed was accomplished by sorcery.”²¹ ²²

footnotes:
²⁰ Rashi is explaining why he said “ענני” twi ce.
²¹ Maseches Berachos 9b.
²² Alternatively, it is not unusual for one who prays to repeat his request.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Kings 18:37:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:37:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:37:2)


**וְיֵדְעוּ וְגוֹ' כִּי אַתָּה ה'** וַאֲנִי עַבְדֶּךָ. וּכְשֶׁתִּשְׁלָחֵנִי לְבַשְּׂרָם לֶעָתִיד לָבֹא, יַאֲמִינוּ בִּי אִם הַיּוֹם תַּעֲנֵנִי.


**[And this people] will know that You are Adonoy.** And I am Your servant, and when You will send me to bring them tidings [of the redemption] that is forthcoming, they will believe me, if You will answer me today.


###### Rashi on I Kings 18:37:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:37:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:37:3)


**וְאַתָּה הֲסִבֹּתָ אֶת לִבָּם.** נָתַתָּ לָהֶם מָקוֹם לָסוּר מֵאַחֲרֶיךָ, וּבְיָדְךָ הָיָה לְהָכִין לְבָבָם אֵלֶיךָ. וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה: אִם לֹא תַּעֲנֵנִי, אַף אֲנִי אֶהְיֶה כּוֹפֵר וְאוֹמֵר, אַתָּה הֲסִבּוֹתָ אֶת לִבָּם, וְכֵן אָמַר משֶׁה: אִם כְּמוֹת כָּל הָאָדָם יְמוּתוּן אֵלֶּה, אַף אֲנִי כּוֹפֵר וְאוֹמֵר, לֹא ה' שְׁלָחַנִי לְדַבֵּר אֶת הַתּוֹרָה וְהַמִּצְוֹת, וְכָךְ אָמַר מִיכַיְהוּ: אִם שׁוֹב תָּשׁוּב בְּשָׁלוֹם, לֹא דִּבֵּר ה' בִּי.


**And You have turned their hearts away.** You gave them an opportunity to turn away from following You, although You could have directed their hearts toward You.²³ And according to Midrash Aggadah, if You do not answer me, I will deny and say that You turned their hearts. And so did Moshe say, “If as all men die will these men meet their death,”²⁴ I, too, will deny and say, “then Adonoy has not sent me,”²⁵ to speak [the words of] the Torah and the commandments, and so did Michoyhu say, “If you really return in peace, Adonoy did not speak to me.”²⁶

footnotes:
²³ See Maseches Berachos 31b. Alternatively, “You misled them by occasionally allowing their idolatrous prayers to be effective.”—Ralbag Or, Eliyahu is referring to the future and is saying, “When You answer my prayer and send down fire, You will have turned their hearts away from the Baal and back to You.”—Radak in the name of Rav Sa’adyah Gaon
²⁴ Bamidbar 16:29.
²⁵ Ibid.
²⁶ Below 22:28.



### Verse 39

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:39:1)


**ה' הוּא הָאֱלֹהִים.** וְלֹא הַבַּעַל הָאֱלֹהִים.


**Adonoy He is God.** And the Baal is not God.²⁷

footnotes:
²⁷ They repeated the declaration to reinforce their belief.—Metzudas Dovid.



### Verse 42

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:42:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:42:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:42:1)


**וַיִּגְהַר אַרְצָה.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) וּגְחִין לְאַרְעָא, לְהִתְפַּלֵּל עַל הַגְּשָׁמִים.


**He bowed to the ground.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “and he crouched on the ground,” to pray for rain.²⁸

footnotes:
²⁸ He put his face between his knees with the intention of remaining in this cramped position until a sign of rain appeared.—Radak



### Verse 43

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:43:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:43:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:43:1)


**דֶּרֶךְ יָם.** אִם תִּרְאֶה עָנָן עוֹלֶה מִן הַיָּם.


**To the sea.** If you see a cloud rising from the sea.²⁹

footnotes:
²⁹ The Mediterranean Sea is near Mount Carmel. Alternatively, דרך ים means westward.—Targum



### Verse 44

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:44:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:44:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:44:1)


**אֱסֹר וָרֵד.** אֱסֹר מַרְכְּבוֹתֶיךָ וְרֵד מַהֵר הַכַּרְמֶל לָשׁוּב לְבֵיתְךָ, פֶּן יִדְחַק אוֹתְךָ הַגֶּשֶׁם בַּדֶּרֶךְ.


**Harness your [horses to the carriage] and go down.** Harness your [horses to] your chariots and go down quickly from Mount Carmel, to return to your house, otherwise the rain will press you on the way.



### Verse 45

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:45:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:45:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:45:1)


**עַד כֹּה וְעַד כֹּה.** בְּתוֹךְ שֶׁהָלַךְ הַשָּׁלִיחַ וּבְתוֹךְ שֶׁאָסַר הַמֶּרְכָּבָה, וְהַשָּׁמַיִם הִתְקַדְּרוּ.


**In the meantime.** While the messenger went, and while he harnessed [he horses to] the chariot, the sky grew dark.



### Verse 46

###### Rashi on I Kings 18:46:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:46:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:46:1)


**וְיַד ה'.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) רוּחַ גְּבוּרָה מִן קֳדָם ה', נִתְלַבֵּשׁ כֹּחַ לָרוּץ לִפְנֵי הַמֶּרְכָּבָה בְּרַגְלָיו, שֶׁלֹּא יֵלֵךְ אַחְאָב יְחִידִי, שֶׁחָלַק כָּבוֹד לַמַּלְכוּת.


**And the hand [the spirit] of Adonoy.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “a spirit of strength from before Adonoy,” [i.e.,] he became endowed with strength to run on foot before the chariot, so that Achov would not go alone, for he treated royalty with respect.³⁰

footnotes:
³⁰ See above 13:2 and Rashi and footnote there.


###### Rashi on I Kings 18:46:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:46:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/18:46:2)


**וַיְשַׁנֵּס מָתְנָיו.** כְּמוֹ וַיֶּאֱזֹר, וְאֵין לוֹ דִמְיוֹן, וְאַף הוּא לֹא בָּא אֶלָּא לְלַמֵּד לְשׁוֹן זֵרוּז, נִזְדָּרֵז כְּגִבּוֹר.


**And he girded his loins.** [וישנס means] the same as ויאזור [=and he girded], but there is no similar word [in Scripture], and it came only to teach [us] an expression of acting quickly, [i.e.,] he was as quick as a mighty man.



## Chapter 19


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on I Kings 19:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:2:1)


**כֹּה יַעֲשׂוּן אֱלֹהִים.** כְּמוֹ שֶׁעָשִׂיתָ לִנְבִיאֵי הַבַּעַל.


**So may the gods do.** As you have done to the prophets of the Baal.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on I Kings 19:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:4:1)


**רֹתֶם.** ייניבר"א בְּלַעַ"ז (עַרְעָר קוֹצָנִי).


**Juniper.** *Genevrier*, in O.F.


###### Rashi on I Kings 19:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:4:2)


**וַיֹּאמֶר רַב.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) סַגִּי לִי אַרְכָּא, עַד אֵימָתַי אֲנָא מַטְרַף כְּדֵין.


**And he said, “It is enough.”** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “My long life is enough,¹ until when will I be confused like this?”

footnotes:
¹ According to the opinion that Eliyahu is Pinchos, Aharon’s grandson, he was over 500 years old at this time.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Kings 19:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:6:1)


**רְצָפִים.** פֶּחָמִים, וְכֵן: וּבְיָדוֹ רִצְפָּה.


**Hot coals.** Hot coals, and similarly, “and in his hand was a hot coal [רצפה].”²

footnotes:
² Yeshayahu 6:6.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Kings 19:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:9:1)


**אֶל הַמְּעָרָה.** הִיא נִקְרַת צוּר שֶׁעָמַד בָּהּ משֶׁה.


**To the cave.** That is the cleft of the rock where Moshe stood.³⁴

footnotes:
³ Shemos 33:22. The cave that Eliyahu entered is “the cleft of the rock” in which Moshe stood while God’s Divine Presence passed.
