### Verse 10

###### Rashi on I Kings 19:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:10:1)


**אֶת מִזְבְּחֹתֶיךָ הָרָסוּ.** בָּמוֹת יָחִיד הַנַּעֲשׂוֹת לְשֵׁם שָׁמַיִם, שֶׁהֲרֵי מִזְבַּח בֵּית הַבְּחִירָה בִּירוּשָׁלַיִם הָיָה.


**They have demolished Your Altars.** [I.e.,] the private altars that were built in the Name of Heaven,⁵ for the altar of the Beis [Hamikdosh] was in Yerusholayim.⁶

footnotes:
⁵ See above 18:30 and Rashi there.
⁶ Eliyahu intimated that God should take vengeance against the Bnei Yisroel.—Ralbag



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on I Kings 19:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:11:1)


**וְרוּחַ גְּדוֹלָה.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) מַשִּׁרְיַת מַלְאֲכֵי רוּחָא.


**And a great wind.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “a camp of angels of the wind.”


###### Rashi on I Kings 19:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:11:2)


**בְּרַעַשׁ.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) מַשִּׁרְיַת מַלְאֲכֵי זִיעָא.


**In the earthquake.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “a camp of angels of earthquake.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Kings 19:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:12:1)


**אֵשׁ.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) מַשִּׁרְיַת מַלְאֲכֵי אֶשָּׁתָא.


**Fire.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “a camp of angels of fire.”


###### Rashi on I Kings 19:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:12:2)


**קוֹל דְּמָמָה דַקָּה.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) קָל דִּמְשַׁבְּחִין בַּחֲשַׁאי, אֲבָל בִּנְבִיאֵי אֻמּוֹת הָעוֹלָם אוֹמֵר: דְּמָמָה וְקוֹל אֶשְׁמַע, דְּמָמָה הָיְתָה לְשַׁבֵּחַ. וַאֲנִי שָׁמַעְתִּי קוֹל הַבָּא מִתּוֹךְ הַדְּמָמָה, דטינטישמנ"ט בְּלַעַ"ז (הֵד), וְאֵין שׁוֹמְעִין הַקּוֹל מַמָּשׁ.


**A calm quiet.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “a voice of those praising quietly,” but regarding the prophets of the [other] nations, it states, “Silence and a voice I heard,”⁷ there was silence to the praising. But I heard, it is a voice coming out of the silence, *rentesment*, in O.F., but the voice itself is not heard.

footnotes:
⁷ Iyov 4:16.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Kings 19:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:13:1)


**וַיָּלֶט פָּנָיו.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) וְכָרִיךְ אַפּוֹהִי, וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר: לוּטָה בַשִּׂמְלָה.


**He wrapped his face.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “and he wrapped his face,”⁸ and so it states, “wrapped up [לוטה] in a cloth.”⁹

footnotes:
⁸ In the same manner that Moshe covered his face at the “burning bush” in Shemos 3:6.
⁹ I Shmuel 21:10.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on I Kings 19:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:16:1)


**תִּמְשַׁח לְנָבִיא תַּחְתֶּיךָ.** אִי אֶפְשִׁי בִּנְבִיאוּתְךָ, מֵאַחַר שֶׁאַתָּה מְלַמֵּד קָטֵּיגוֹרְיָא עַל בָּנַי.


**You should anoint as prophet in your place.** [God said,] “I do not want your prophecy, because you plead for the prosecution of My children.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ Alternatively, “you should appoint another prophet in your place because you had asked to die, and for God to take your soul,” in v. 4 above.—Radak



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on I Kings 19:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:17:1)


**יָמִית אֱלִישָׁע.** לֹא מָצִינוּ שֶׁהֵמִית אֶלָּא אַרְבָּעִים וּשְׁנַיִם יְלָדִים עַל יְדֵי הַדֻּבִּים בִּירִיחוֹ.


**Elisha will kill.** We do not find that Elisha killed anyone but the forty-two children through the bears in Yericho.¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ See II Melochim 2:24. Alternatively, the hunger in Shomron was initiated by Elisha’s curse, as implied in II Melochim 6:31.—Ralbag



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on I Kings 19:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:20:1)


**לֵךְ שׁוּב.** מֵאַחֲרַי.


**Go back.** From following me.¹²

footnotes:
¹² Eliyahu was testing Elisha’s resolve.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Kings 19:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:20:2)


**כִּי מֶה עָשִׂיתִי לָךְ.** שֶׁתָּבוֹא אַחֲרַי.


**For what have I done to you.** That you should follow me.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on I Kings 19:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:21:1)


**בִּשְּׁלָם הַבָּשָׂר.** בִּשֵּׁל לָהֶם אֶת הַבָּשָׂר, כָּךְ פֵּרְשׁוּ דוֹנַ"שׁ וּמְנַחֵם, וְכֵן תִּרְגְּמוֹ יוֹנָתָן בִּשְׁתֵּי תֵבוֹת (בָּשִׁיל לְהוֹן). וַאֲנִי אוֹמֵר, אֵין צָרִיךְ לְחַלְּקוֹ לִשְׁתֵּי תֵבוֹת, וּמַהוּ בִּשְּׁלָם הַבָּשָׂר, בִּשְּׁלָם לִשְׁנֵי הַשְּׁוָרִים אֶת הַבָּשָׂר שֶׁלָּהֶם, שֶׁהַצֶּמֶד אֵין פָּחוֹת מִשְּׁנַיִם.


**He cooked the meat for them.** He cooked for them the meat, so do Dunash and Menachem explain, and so did [Targum] Yonoson render [בשלם] as two words [בשיל להון]. But I say, that it is unnecessary to divide it into two words, and what is the meaning of “בשלם הבשר”? He cooked the two oxen, their flesh, for צמד is a minimum of two.


###### Rashi on I Kings 19:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/19:21:2)


**וַיִּתֵּן לָעָם.** מֵרֹב שִׂמְחָה עָשָׂה מִשְׁתֶּה.


**And he gave it the people.** He made a feast out of great joy.



## Chapter 20


### Verse 5

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:5:1)


**כִּי שָׁלַחְתִּי אֵלֶיךָ וְגוֹ' לִי תִתֵּן.** יָדַעְתִּי כִּי תִתְּנֵם לִי, אֲבָל אַחֶרֶת אֲנִי גּוֹזֵר עָלֶיךָ.


**When I have sent to you, etc., you must give [them] to me.** I know that you will give them to me, but I am decreeing upon you something else.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:6:1)


**כָּל מַחְמַד עֵינֶיךָ יָשִׂימוּ בְיָדָם וְלָקְחוּ.** וַהֲלֹא כָּל הָרִאשׁוֹנִים דִּבְרֵי חֶמְדָּה הֵם, אֶלָּא מַהוּ מַחְמַד עֵינֶיךָ, חֶמְדָּה מִתּוֹךְ חֶמְדָּה, זֶה סֵפֶר תּוֹרָה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בּוֹ: הַנֶּחֱמָדִים מִזָּהָב וּמִפַּז רַב, אָמַר אַחְאָב בְּלִבּוֹ, דָּבָר גָּדוֹל זֶה מְבַקֵּשׁ, אֵין זֶה שֶׁלִּי לְבַדִּי, שֶׁל זִקְנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל הִיא, לְפִיכָךְ: וַיִּקְרָא לְכָל זִקְנֵי הָאָרֶץ, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהָיוּ עוֹבְדִין עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה, הָיוּ מְכַבְּדִין אֶת הַתּוֹרָה.


**Everything you cherish they will put in their hands and take.** Were not the first [things] cherished things? What, then, is the meaning of “[everything] you cherish”? [This means] a treasure within a treasure, referring to the Torah Scroll, about which it is stated, “more desirable than gold, even more than quantities of fine gold.”¹ Achov said to himself, “He demands a great thing, but this is not mine alone; it belongs to the elders of Yisroel.”² Therefore, he “called all the elders of the land,”³ for even though they worshiped idols, they honored the Torah.⁴ with which the Torah is written.

footnotes:
¹ Tehillim 19:11.
² He therefore could not give away the Torah without consulting the elders.
³ Below v. 7.
⁴ See Maseches Sanhedrin 102b. Despite all his sins, Achov believed in the Torah and refused to give it up. He was ready to go to war if necessary, to defend it. God therefore rewarded him with a reign of 22 years corresponding to the 22 letters of the Alef Beis



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:10:1)


**אִם יִשְׂפֹּק עֲפַר שֹׁמְרוֹן לִשְׁעָלִים וְגוֹ'.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) לְמֵיסַב בִּשְׁעוֹלֵי פַרְסַת רַגְלֵי עַמָּא דְעִמִּי, עָפָר שֶׁנִּדְבַּק בְּפַרְסַת רַגְלַיִם.


**If the dust of Shomron will be sufficient for the soles of the feet, etc.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “to take with the footsteps of all the people who are with me,” the earth that sticks to the sole of the feet.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ Alternatively, שעלים means fistfuls, and Ben-Hadad said, that if each of his soldiers was to take a fistful of the dirt of Shomron, there would not be enough dirt for them, for his army was so large.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:10:2)


**לִשְׁעָלִים.** לְשׁוֹן מִדְרַךְ כַּף רָגֶל, וְכֵן: בְּמִשְׁעוֹל הַכְּרָמִים; וְכֵן: מִי מָדַד בְּשָׁעֳלוֹ מַיִם, שֶׁדָּרַךְ בְּיַם סוּף, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר דָּרַכְתָּ בַּיָּם סוּסֶיךָ.


**For the soles of the feet.** An expression of, “treading with the sole of the foot,” and similarly, “in a narrow path [משעול] of the vineyards,”⁶ and similarly, “who measured the water with his step [בשעלו],”⁷ for He trod in the Red Sea, as it is stated, “You tramped them in the sea with Your horses.”⁸

footnotes:
⁶ Bamidbar 22:24.
⁷ Yeshayahu 40:12.
⁸ Chavakuk 3:15.


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:10:3)


**אִם יִשְׂפֹּק.** אִם יֵשׁ דַּי.


**Will be sufficient.** If there is enough.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:11:1)


**דַּבְּרוּ אַל יִתְהַלֵּל חֹגֵר כִּמְפַתֵּחַ.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) אֱמַרוּ לֵהּ, לָא יִשְׁתַּבַּח דְּמִזְדְּרֵז וְנָחִית בִּקְרָבָא, כִּגְבָר דְּנַצַּח וְסָלִיק מִנַּהּ. אַל יִתְהַלֵּל הַחוֹגֵר חַרְבּוֹ לָרֶדֶת לַמִּלְחָמָה, שֶׁאֵינוֹ יוֹדֵעַ אִם יְנַצַּח אִם לָאו.


**“Tell him, ‘He who puts on the sword shall not brag like the one who takes it off.’”** “Say to him, ‘One should not boast, he who arms himself and goes down to war like a man who has been victorious and is coming up from it.’” One should not boast he who puts on his sword to wage to war, for he does not know whether or not he will be victorious.


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:11:2)


**כִּמְפַתֵּחַ.** חֲגוֹרַת חַרְבּוֹ, שֶׁעָלָה כְּבָר מִן הַמִּלְחָמָה וְנִצַּח, כָּךְ אֵין לַאֲדוֹנֵיכֶם לְהִתְהַלֵּל בִּדְבַר הֶעָתִיד.


**Like the one who takes it off.** His sword binding, [after] he has come up from the war victorious. Similarly, your master should not boast about a future event.

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:12:1)


**שִׂימוּ.** כְּלֵי הַמָּצוֹר עַל הָעִיר.

**נָבִיא אֶחָד.** הוּא מִיכַיְהוּ בֶּן יִמְלָה.

### Verse 14

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:14:1)


**בְּנַעֲרֵי שָׂרֵי הַמְּדִינוֹת.** הֵם בְּנֵי הַתַּעֲרוֹבוֹת, שֶׁהָיוּ כָּל הַשָּׂרִים שֶׁל שְׁאָר הָאֻמּוֹת נוֹתְנִים בְּנֵיהֶם תַּחַת יָדוֹ עֲרֵבִים, שֶׁלֹּא יִמְרְדוּ בוֹ.


**By the youths of the officers of the provinces.** They are the hostages, for all the governors of the other nations would give their children into his custody [as a guarantee] that they will not rebel against him.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ Also, this may have served as a demonstration of loyalty to the king. Alternatively, they were the children of Achov’s high ranking officers who were raised in the royal court.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:14:2)


**מִי יֶאְסֹר הַמִּלְחָמָה.** יַעֲרֹךְ לְצַוּוֹת אוֹתָהּ.


**Who will lead the battle?** Who will array [the battle], to command it.¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ Being an idolater, Achov felt that he was unworthy to have a miracle performed through him and therefore he looked for another leader.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:14:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:14:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:14:3)


**אָתָּה.** צֵא אַתָּה תְּחִלָּה וְכֹה תְמִיתֶנּוּ (וְעַתָּה תָּרִיעוּ).


**You.** You go forth first and so you shall kill him. (And now you shall shout: Omitted in some texts)



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:15:1)


**שִׁבְעַת אֲלָפִים.** אוֹמֵר אֲנִי, הֵם שֶׁאָמַר עֲלֵיהֶם: כָּל הַבִּרְכַּיִם אֲשֶׁר לֹא כָרְעוּ לַבַּעַל שִׁבְעַת אֲלָפִים.


**Seven thousand.** I say that they are the ones about whom it is stated, “All those whose knees did not bend to the Baal,” were seven thousand.¹²

¹² Above 19:18.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:16:1)


**בַּסֻּכּוֹת.** שֶׁעָשׂוּ לָהֶם מִפְּנֵי הַחַמָּה.


**In the shelters.** That they had made for themselves [as protection] from the sun.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:22:1)


**לִתְשׁוּבַת הַשָּׁנָה.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) לִזְמַן סוֹפָא דְּשַׁתָּא.


**At the end of the year.** [Targum Yonoson rendered], “at the time of the year’s end.”¹³

### Verse 24

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:24:1)


**וְשִׂים פָּחוֹת תַּחְתֵּיהֶם.** מִתּוֹךְ שֶׁלֹּא יְהוּ מִמִּשְׁפָּחָה רָמָה, יִתְּנוּ לֵב לְהִתְחַזֵּק, כְּדֵי לִמְצֹא חֵן.


**And replace them with generals.** Because they are not of a high [society] family, they will put in an extra effort in order to find favor [in your eyes].



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:25:1)


**אִם לֹא נֶחֱזַק מֵהֶם.** כְּלוֹמַר, וְתִרְאֶה אִם לֹא נֶחֱזַק מֵהֶם.


**And he will be more powerful than them.** I.e., and you will see if we will not overpower them.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:27:1)


**וְכָלְכְּלוּ.** פַּרְנָסַת צֵדָה לַמִּלְחָמָה הִצְטַיְּדוּ, הֵם כְּלֵי זַיִן וְכָל צָרְכֵי הַמִּלְחָמָה.


**And provisioned.** They armed themselves with supplies for the war; i.e., with weapons and all necessities of war.


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:27:2)


**וְכָלְכְּלוּ.** נִתְכַּלְכְּלוּ.


**And provisioned.** They were provisioned.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹⁴ Alternatively, וכלכלו means and they all [=כל] were accounted for and none were missing.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:27:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:27:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:27:3)


**כִּשְׁנֵי חֲשִׂפֵי עִזִּים.** קְבוּצֵי עִזִּים, וְאֵין לוֹ דִּמְיוֹן. וּמְנַחֵם חִבְּרוֹ בִּלְשׁוֹן גִּלּוּי, כְּמוֹ: מַחְשׂוֹף הַלָּבָן: חֶשְׂפִּי שׁוֹבֶל, וּלְשׁוֹן גִּלּוּי נוֹפֵל עַל עֶדְרֵי עִזִּים, כְּמוֹ: שֶׁגָּלְשׁוּ מֵהַר גִּלְעָד, כְּשֶׁיּוֹרְדִין מִן הָהָר, נִתְגַּלֶּה הָהָר.


**Like two flocks of goats.** [חשפי means] “flocks of goats,”¹⁵ but there is no similar word [in Scripture]. However, Menachem classifies [חשפי] as an expression of revelation, as in, “by uncovering [מחשוף] the white,”¹⁶ [and as in,] “bare [חשפי] a leg.”¹⁷ An expression of “revelation” can be applied to flocks of goats, as in, “who bared [שגלשו] from Mount Gilad,”¹⁸ [i.e.,] when they come down from the mountain, the mountain is bared.

footnotes:
¹⁵ I.e., they are small in number in comparison with Arom’s army which “filled the land.”. The two flocks consisted of one group of youths of the officers of the provinces and another group of 7000 men.—Metzudas Dovid
¹⁶ Bereishis 30:37.
¹⁷ Yeshayahu 47:2.
¹⁸ Shir Hashirim 4:1.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:28:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר אֶל מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל וַיֹּאמֶר וְגוֹ'.** אָמַר רַבִּי יוֹחָנָן: כָּל מָקוֹם שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר וַיֹּאמֶר וַיֹּאמֶר, צָרִיךְ לִדְרשׁ. אֲמִירָה רִאשׁוֹנָה, אָמַר לוֹ וְנָתַתִּי אֶת כָּל הֶהָמוֹן הַזֶּה בְּיָדֶךָ, אֲמִירָה שְׁנִיָּה, אִם יִפֹּל בֶּן הֲדַד בְּיָדְךָ אַל תַחֲמֹל עָלָיו, לְפִיכָךְ כְּשֶׁחָמַל עָלָיו אָמַר לוֹ: יַעַן שִׁלַּחְתָּ אֶת אִישׁ חֶרְמִי מִיָּד וְגוֹ'.


**And he spoke to the king of Yisroel, and he said, etc.** Rabbi Yochonon said that every place where it is stated, “And he said....and he said,” must be expounded. The first saying refers to what he said to him, “I will give this entire great multitude into your hand,” the second saying [refers to], “if Ben-Hadad falls into your hands, show him no clemency.” Therefore, when he took pity on him, he said to him, “Because you released the man whom I condemned from your hand, etc.”¹⁹ ²⁰

footnotes:
¹⁹ Below v. 42.
²⁰ Talmud Yerushalmi Sanhedrin 11:5.



### Verse 33

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:33:1)


**יְנַחֲשׁוּ וַיְמַהֲרוּ וַיַּחְלְטוּ הֲמִמֶּנּוּ.** יְנַחֲשׁוּ לְשׁוֹן מְנַחֵשׁ, אָמְרוּ כֵּיוָן שֶׁנָּפְלוּ דִּבְרֵי שָׁלוֹם בְּפִיו אֲפִלּוּ שֶׁלֹּא בְּמִתְכַּוֵּן, סִימָן טוֹב הוּא זֶה, וַיְמַהֲרוּ לְהַחֲזִיק הַדָּבָר.


**The [men] took it for a [good] omen, and they hurried to confirm it from him.** “ינחשו” is an expression of “divining.” They said that since words of peace fell from his mouth,²¹ even unintentionally, it is a good sign, and they hastened to confirm the matter.

footnotes:
²¹ Ben-Hadad’s men originally referred to their master as “your servant,” when they addressed Achov, but once they heard Achov refer to Ben-Hadad as “my brother,” they change and called him “your brother,” too.


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:33:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:33:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:33:2)


**וַיַּחְלְטוּ.** לְשׁוֹן: צְמִיתוּת, כָּרְתוּ הַדָּבָר מִפִּיו שֶׁלֹּא יַחֲזֹר בּוֹ, וְהַהֵ"א שֶׁל הֲמִמֶּנּוּ נוֹטֶה בְּמָסֹרֶת לִשְׁתֵּי הַתֵּבוֹת, כְּאִלּוּ נִכְתַּב וַיַּחְלְטוּהָ מִמֶּנּוּ, אִם מִמֶּנּוּ יֵצֵא הַדָּבָר בְּכַוָּנַת הַלֵּב.


**To confirm.** [ויחלטו is] an expression of “confirmation.” They cut the word from his mouth so that he should not retract. According to the Masorah, the ‘ה’ of “הממנו” leans to both words,²² as though it were written, ויחלטוה ממנו [=and they caught it from him], whether the word emanated from him intentionally.

footnotes:
²² The preceding word [ויחלטו] and the succeeding word [הממנו].



### Verse 34

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:34:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר אֵלָיו.** בֶּן הֲדַד, הֶעָרִים אֲשֶׁר לָקַח אָבִי מֵאֵת אָבִיךָ עָמְרִי, אָשִׁיב לְךָ.


**He [Ben-Hadad] said.** Ben-Hadad [said], “The cities that my father took from your father Omri, I will return to you.”


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:34:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:34:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:34:2)


**וְחוּצוֹת תָּשִׂים לְךָ בְדַמֶּשֶׂק.** וּתְהִי מוֹשֵׁל בָּהּ, וְהִיא רֹאשׁ לְמַלְכוּתִי.


**And you may establish markets in Damascus.** And you rule in it, although it is the capital of my kingdom.²³

footnotes:
²³ And you can collect taxes from the markets in Damascus.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:34:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:34:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:34:3)


**כַּאֲשֶׁר שָׂם אָבִי בְּשֹׁמְרוֹן.** עִירְךָ עַל כָּרְחֲךָ, וְכָל זֶה הָיָה אוֹמֵר מִיִּרְאָה, אָמַר לוֹ אַחְאָב, וַאֲנִי בַּבְּרִית הַזֹּאת אֲשַׁלְּחֶךָּ.


**As my father established in Shomron.** Your city, against your will. He was saying all this out of fear. Achov said to him, “And with this treaty I will release you.”



### Verse 35

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:35:1)


**הַכֵּנִי נָא.** סִימָן זֶה רַע עַל אַחְאָב, שֶׁאָמַר לוֹ הַנָּבִיא עַל פִּי הַגְּבוּרָה לְהַכּוֹת אֶת בֶּן הֲדַד וְלֹא הִכָּהוּ. וְאַף זֶה שֶׁאָמַר לוֹ הַנָּבִיא בִּדְבַר ה' הַכֵּנִי, וְלֹא הִכָּהוּ, כְּשֵׁם שֶׁנִּפְרַע מִזֶּה, יִפָּרַע מֵאַחְאָב.


**Hit me now.** This sign was bad for Achov, for the prophet said to him by the word of the Almighty to kill Ben-Hadad, and he did not kill him.²⁴ Also, this one to whom the prophet said by the word of God, “Hit me,” and he did not hit him, just as He punished him, He would punish Achov.

footnotes:
²⁴ Achov chose to be gracious to Ben-Hadad even though he had been commanded to destroy Arom. However, dealing mercifully with evildoers is evil in itself because the evildoers will eventually cause others to suffer.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:35:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:35:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:35:2)


**בִּדְבַר ה'.** הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא אָמַר שֶׁתַּכֶּה אוֹתִי.


**By the word of Adonoy.** The Holy One Blessed Is He, said that you should hit me.



### Verse 37

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:37:1)


**וּפָצֹע.** מַכַּת חַבּוּרָה.


**And wounding him.** A wounding blow.



### Verse 38

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:38:1)


**וַיִּתְחַפֵּשׂ בָּאֲפֵר.** שִׁנָּה אֶת מַעְפָּרְתּוֹ שֶׁלֹּא יַכִּירוּהוּ, כָּל שִׁנּוּי בְּגָדִים הוּא לְשׁוֹן חִפּוּשׂ.


**And disguised himself with a kerchief.** He changed his hooded cloak so that they would not recognize him. Any change of clothes is an expression of disguise.


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:38:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:38:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:38:2)


**בָּאֲפֵר.** תִּרְגְּמוֹ יוֹנָתָן: בְּמַעֲפָרָא, הוּא סוּדָר.


**With a kerchief.** [Targum] Yonoson rendered this במעפרא, “a hooded cloak.”



### Verse 39

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:39:1)


**וְהִנֵּה אִישׁ סָר.** מִן הַדֶּרֶךְ אֵלַי.


**And behold a man turned aside.** From the road toward me.


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:39:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:39:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:39:2)


**וַיָּבֵא אֵלַי אִישׁ.** שָׁבוּי מִן הַמִּלְחָמָה.


**And brought a man to me.** A prisoner of war.


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:39:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:39:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:39:3)


**אִם הִפָּקֵד יִפָּקֵד.** אִם יִבְרַח, לְשׁוֹן: לֹא נִפְקַד מִמֶּנּוּ אִישׁ, לְשׁוֹן חִסָּרוֹן.


**If he shall be missing.** If he escapes, an expression [similar to], “and none of us is missing [נפקד],” an expression of lacking.²⁵

footnotes:
²⁵ Bamidbar 31:49.



### Verse 40

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:40:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:40:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:40:1)


**וְהוּא אֵינֶנּוּ.** בָּרַח לוֹ.


**And disappeared.** He ran away.


###### Rashi on I Kings 20:40:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:40:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:40:2)


**אַתָּה חָרַצְתָּ.** אוֹ נֶפֶשׁ, אוֹ כִּכַּר כֶּסֶף.


**You have pronounced.** Either [your] life or a talent of silver.



### Verse 42

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:42:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:42:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:42:1)


**אִישׁ חֶרְמִי.** אִישׁ מִלְחַמְתִּי, לְשׁוֹן חֵירוּם, וּלְשׁוֹן חֵירוּם נוֹפֵל בְּמִלְחָמָה, וְכֵן: עַל אֱדוֹם תֵּרֵד וְעַל עַם חֶרְמִי. וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה כַּמָּה מְצוּדוֹת וַחֲרָמִים פָּרַשְׂתִּי לְךָ עַד שֶׁנָּפַל בְּיָדְךָ.


**The man whom I condemned.** איש חרמי means “the man of My war,” an expression of strife. An expression of strife [חרם] can be applied to war, and similarly, “on Edom it will descend and on the nation with whom I war [חרמי].²⁶ The Midrash Aggadah [states], “Many traps and nets [חרמים] have I spread for you until he fell into your hand.”²⁷

footnotes:
²⁶ Yeshayahu 34:5.
²⁷ Alternatively, איש חרמי means “the man whom I condemned.”—Radak



### Verse 43

###### Rashi on I Kings 20:43:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:43:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/20:43:1)


**סָר וְזָעֵף.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) נְסִיס וְדַוָּי, רוּחוֹ סָרָה מִמֶּנּוּ.


**Dejected and angry.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “aggrieved and sad.” His spirit turned [סר] away from him.



## Chapter 21


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on I Kings 21:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:1:1)


**וַיְהִי אַחַר הַדְּבָרִים הָאֵלֶּה.** עוֹד אַחֶרֶת לְקָרֵב מִיתָתוֹ.


**After these things [this occurred].** Yet another [incident] to bring his death near.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on I Kings 21:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:2:1)


**מְחִיר זֶה.** הַכֶּרֶם הַזֶּה.


**The worth of this one.** This vineyard.¹

footnotes:
¹ Achov did not attempt to take this vineyard by force because he was afraid of a popular rebellion if he were to act unjustly.—Radak



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on I Kings 21:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:8:1)


**אֶל הַזְּקֵנִים.** זִקְנֵי אַשְׁמַאי, סָבֵי דְבַהֲתָא.


**To the elders.** Wicked elders, elders of shame.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Kings 21:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:9:1)


**קִרְאוּ צוֹם.** וּבְיוֹם צוֹם דַּרְכָּם לִבְדֹּק בַּעֲבֵרוֹת שֶׁבְּיָדָם.


**Proclaim a day of fasting.** On a fast day it was their custom to examine their sins.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Kings 21:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:13:1)


**בֵּרַךְ נָבוֹת.** חִלּוּף שֶׁל קְלָלָה, וְתַרְגּוּם: גָּדִיף נָבוֹת קֳדָם ה' וּמַלְכָּא לָט.


**Navos cursed.** A euphemism for curse. And Targum [Yonoson rendered], Navos blasphemed against God and cursed the king.²

footnotes:
² Thus making him liable to death for either offense. The reason for mentioning both offenses is to arouse the people’s anger against Navos and thereby he would be executed quickly. See Maseches Sanhedrin 48b.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on I Kings 21:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:15:1)


**קוּם רֵשׁ.** יֵשׁ מֵחֲכָמִים אוֹמְרִים: הֲרוּגֵי מַלְכוּת נִכְסֵיהֶם לַמֶּלֶךְ, וְיֵשׁ מֵהֶן אוֹמְרִים: בֶּן אֲחִי אָבִיו הָיָה, וְאוֹתוֹ וְאֶת בָּנָיו הָרַג, וְרָאוּי לְיָרְשׁוֹ.


**Get up and take possession.** Some of our Sages say that the property of those executed by the king [belong] to the king. And some of them say³ that he was the son of his father’s brother. He killed him and his children and was therefore the legal heir to inherit his property.⁴

footnotes:
³ Some are of the opinion that even those executed by the king do not lose their right of having their property inherited by their closest of kin. Therefore they suggest that Achov was the son of, etc.
⁴ See Maseches Sanhedrin 48b.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on I Kings 21:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:20:1)


**הִתְמַכֶּרְךָ.** מָכַרְתָּ אֶת עַצְמְךָ לְהַכְעִיס אֶת בּוֹרַאֲךָ. הִתְמַכֶּרְךָ כְּמוֹ: וְהִתְמַכַּרְתֶּם שָׁם לְאוֹיְבֶיךָ.


**You have sold yourself.** You sold yourself to anger your Creator,⁵ התמכרת [=you have sold yourself], as in, “and there you will attempt to sell yourselves [והתמכרתם] to your enemies.”⁶

footnotes:
⁵ You made yourself like a stranger. Achov went so far as to erase God’s Name from the Torah and he replaced it with Baal’s name throughout.—Radak
⁶ Devarim 28:68.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on I Kings 21:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:23:1)


**בְּחֵל יִזְרְעֶאל.** בְּבִקְעַת יִזְרְעֶאל, בְּחֵל, תַּרְגּוּמוֹ שֶׁל גַּיְא.


**In the valley of Yizreil.** In the valley of Yizreil. “בחיל” is the Aramaic translation of “valley.”



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on I Kings 21:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:25:1)


**רַק לֹא הָיָה כְאַחְאָב.** הַכָּתוּב מֵעִיד עָלָיו, שֶׁלֹּא הָיָה בַּמְּלָכִים כְּמוֹתוֹ, שֶׁיָּרָבְעָם וְכָל אֲשֶׁר אַחֲרָיו, עָבְדוּ אֶת הָעֲגָלִים מִיִּרְאָה, אִם יַעֲלֶה הָעָם יְרוּשָׁלַיִם, תָּשׁוּב הַמַּמְלָכָה לְבֵית דָּוִד, וְזֶה הוֹסִיף הַבַּעַל וְהָאֲשֵׁרָה לְהַכְעִיס.


**But there was none likes of Achov.** Scripture testifies about him that there was none among the kings like him,⁷ for Yerovom and all those after him worshiped the calves out of fear, that if the people would go up to Yerusholayim, the kingdom would revert to the House of Dovid, but this one added the Baal and the Asheirah to anger [God].

footnotes:
⁷ Achov surpassed all his predecessors in doing evil.


###### Rashi on I Kings 21:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:25:2)


**הִתְמַכֵּר.** נִמְכַּר לַעֲבוֹדָה זָרָה. רָאִיתִי בִּירוּשַׁלְמִי חִיאֵל בֵּית הָאֱלִי שׁוֹשְׁבִינוֹ הָיָה, וּבְכָל יוֹם שָׁם דָּמָיו וְנָתַן לַעֲבוֹדָה זָרָה.


**Sold himself.** He was sold to idolatry. I saw in [Talmud] Yerushalmi that Chieil from the house of Eili was his advisor, and every day he would estimate his value and give it to the pagan deities.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Talmud Yerushalmi Maseches Sanhedrin 10:2.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on I Kings 21:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:27:1)


**וַיְהַלֵּךְ אַט.** יָחֵף.


**And walked barefoot.** [He walked] barefoot.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ Alternatively, Achov walked slowly [לאט], like a mourner or like one who is under a ban.—Ralbag



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on I Kings 21:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/21:29:1)


**לֹא אָבִיא הָרָעָה.** רָעַת בֵּיתוֹ לֹא אָבִיא בְּיָמָיו, אֲבָל גְּזֵרַת יָלֹקּוּ הַכְּלָבִים אֶת דָּמוֹ, אִי אֶפְשָׁר לָבֹא בִּימֵי בְּנוֹ, אֶלָּא עָלָיו.


**I will not bring the evil.** The disaster of this house I will not bring in his days, but the decree that the dogs will lick his blood is not possible to come in the days of his son, but upon him.



## Chapter 22


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:2:1)


**בַּשָּׁנָה הַשְּׁלִישִׁית.** שֶׁשָּׁלַח אֶת בֶּן הֲדַד.


**In the third year.** Since he had released Ben-Hadad.¹

footnotes:
¹ See above 20:34.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:3:1)


**וַאֲנַחְנוּ מַחְשִׁים.** מִתְעַצְּלִים, סיאמ"ו פיגר"י בְּלַעַ"ז (לְהַחֲרִישׁ).


**We have been quiet.** We are lazy.² *Siamo figre*, in O.F.

footnotes:
² Alternatively, “we have been quiet [=מחשים]” as in, “a time to be quiet [לחשות],” in Koheles 3:7—Metzudas Tzion



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:7:1)


**הַאֵין פֹּה נָבִיא לַה' עוֹד.** מְקֻבְּלַנִי אֵין שְׁנֵי נְבִיאִים מִתְנַבְּאִים בְּלָשׁוֹן אֶחָד, וְאֵלֶּה כֻּלָּם לָשׁוֹן אֶחָד אוֹמְרִים.


**Is there no other prophet of Adonoy?** I have a tradition that no two prophets prophesy with the same expression, and these are all saying the same expression.³⁴

footnotes:
³ See Maseches Sanhedrin 89a.
⁴ Yehoshofot did not accuse them directly of not being God’s prophets out of respect for Achov.—Radak



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:8:1)


**כִּי אִם רָע.** לְפִי שֶׁאָמַר: יַעַן שִׁלַּחְתָּ אֶת אִישׁ חֶרְמִי וְגוֹ'.


**But only evil.** For he said to me, “Because you released the man whom I condemned, etc.”⁵

footnotes:
⁵ Above 20:42.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:15:1)


**עֲלֵה וְהַצְלַח.** לֹא אָמַר לוֹ בְּשֵׁם ה'.


**Go up and be successful.** He did not say [it] to him in God’s Name.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ He was merely wishing him good luck, or giving him a blessing that he succeed.—Metzudas Dovid


###### Rashi on I Kings 22:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:15:2)


**וְנָתַן ה'.** הַלְוַאי יִתֵּן בְּיַד הַמֶּלֶךְ.


**And may Adonoy give.** If only He deliver it into the king’s hand.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:17:1)


**לֹא אֲדֹנִים לָאֵלֶּה.** אַתָּה לְבַדְּךָ תָּמוּת בַּמִּלְחָמָה, כְּעִנְיָן שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: לֹא תִּלָּחֲמוּ אֶת קָטֹן וְאֶת גָּדוֹל כִּי אִם אֶת מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל לְבַדּוֹ. וְאַף עַל פִּי שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וְעַמְּךָ תַּחַת עַמּוֹ, טִפָּה שֶׁיָּצְאָה מֵאוֹתוֹ צַדִּיק מִיכָיְהוּ, שֶׁהִכָּהוּ חֲבֵרוֹ הַכֵּה וּפָצוֹעַ, הָיְתָה כַּפָּרָה עַל כָּל יִשְׂרָאֵל. כָּךְ מְפֹרָשׁ בָּאַגָּדָה.


**These have no masters.** You alone will die in the war, as the matter is stated below, “Do not battle the small or the great, but only with the king of Yisroel himself.”⁷ Even though it was stated, “and your people instead of his people,”⁸ the drop of blood that came out of the righteous man, Michoyhu, whose friend struck him, striking and wounding him, was an atonement for all Yisroel. So it is explained in the [Midrash] Aggadah.⁹

footnotes:
⁷ Below v. 31.
⁸ Above 20:42.
⁹ Talmud Yerushalmi Maseches Sanhedrin 11:5.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:19:1)


**מִימִינוֹ וּמִשְּׂמֹאלוֹ.** וְכִי יֵשׁ שְׂמֹאל לְמַעְלָה, וַהֲלֹא נֶאֱמַר: יְמִין ה' רוֹמֵמָה יְמִין ה' עוֹשָׂה חָיִל: יְמִינְךָ ה' נֶאְדָּרִי בַכֹּחַ יְמִינְךָ ה' תִּרְעַץ אוֹיֵב, אֶלָּא אֵלּוּ מַיְמִינִים וְאֵלּוּ מַשְׂמְאִילִים, מַיְמִינִים מְלַמְּדִים זְכוּת, מַשְׂמְאִילִים מְלַמְּדִים חוֹבָה.


**To His right and to His left.** Is there “left” in Heaven? Is it not stated, “The right hand of Adonoy’s is exalted, the right hand of Adonoy performs deeds of valor,”¹⁰ [and,] “Your right hand, Adonoy, is adorned with power; Your right hand, Adonoy, crushes the enemy”¹¹? But rather, these are on the right and these are on the left. Those on the right present the case for the defense and those on the left present the case for the prosecution.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Tehillim 118:16.
¹¹ Shemos 15:6.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:21:1)


**וַיֵּצֵא הָרוּחַ.** רוּחוֹ שֶׁל נָבוֹת.


**And a spirit came forth.** The spirit of Navos.¹²

footnotes:
¹² Maseches Sanhedrin 89a. See also Maseches Shabbos 149b.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:24:1)


**אֵי זֶה עָבַר רוּחַ ה'.** אֵי זֶה הַדֶּרֶךְ עָבַר, וַהֲרֵי זֶה מִקְרָא קָצָר, בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים כְּתִיב: אֵי זֶה הַדֶּרֶךְ עָבַר וְגוֹ'.


**When did the spirit of Adonoy pass?** [I.e.,] which way did it pass? This is an abbreviated verse.¹³ In Divrei Hayomim it is written, “In what way did [Adonoy’s spirit] pass.”¹⁴

footnotes:
¹³ I.e., the word דרך [=way] is missing.
¹⁴ II Divrei Hayomim 18:23.



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:30:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל אֶל יְהוֹשָׁפָט הִתְחַפֵּשׂ וָבֹא בַמִּלְחָמָה.** לְהִתְחַפֵּשׂ וְלָבֹא, אֲנִי אֶתְחַפֵּשׂ, אֲשַׁנֶּה בִגְדֵי מַלְכוּתִי שֶׁלֹּא יַכִּירוּנִי, שֶׁאִם יַכִּירוּנִי יָסוּרוּ אֵלַי לְהִלָּחֵם.


**The king of Yisroel said to Yehoshofot, I will disguise myself and go into battle.** To disguise himself and go [into battle]. I will disguise myself, [i.e.,] I will change my royal garments, so that they will not recognize me, for if they recognize me, they will turn toward me to attack.


###### Rashi on I Kings 22:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:30:2)


**וְאַתָּה לְבַשׁ בְּגָדֶיךָ.** שֶׁעָלֶיךָ, שֶׁלֹּא מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל אָתָּה, אֶלָּא מֶלֶךְ יְהוּדָה, וְעִמְּךָ אֵין לָהֶם תְּגָר.


**But you wear your own [royal] garments.** That you are wearing, for you are not the king of Yisroel, but the king of Yehudah, and they have no conflict with you.



### Verse 32

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:32:1)


**וַיִּזְעַק יְהוֹשָׁפָט.** לְהַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא.


**And Yehoshofot cried out.** To the Holy One Blessed Is He.



### Verse 34

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:34:1)


**וְאִישׁ מָשַׁךְ.** זֶה נַעֲמָן.


**A man drew.** This was Naaman.¹⁵

¹⁵ Who would later be cured from leprosy by Elisha. See II Melochim 5:1-15.


###### Rashi on I Kings 22:34:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:34:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:34:2)


**לְתֻמּוֹ.** לֹא יָדַע שֶׁהוּא מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל.


**Innocently [without aiming].** He was not aware that he was the king of Yisroel.¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁶ Alternatively, לתמו means “to complete it,” i.e., the prophecies of Michoyhu and Eliyahu were completed with the death of Achov. See Maseches Sanhedrin 39b.


###### Rashi on I Kings 22:34:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:34:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:34:3)


**בֵּין הַדְּבָקִים וּבֵין הַשִּׁרְיָן.** יֵשׁ שִׁרְיוֹנוֹת שֶׁהֵם עֲשׂוּיִין קְלִפִּים קְלִפִּים, כְּעִנְיָן שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וְשִׁרְיוֹן קַשְׂקַשִּׂים, וְהַקְּלִפִּים נוֹפְלִים עַל נִקְבֵי הַשִּׁרְיוֹן וְסוֹתְמִין אוֹתָן, וְהֵם קְרוּיִין דְּבָקִים.


**Between the plates of armor.** Some coats of mail are made of layers of scales, in the manner it is stated, “and a coat of mail,”¹⁷ and the scales fall on the holes of the coat and close them, and they are called “דבקים” [=attached].

footnotes:
¹⁷ I Shmuel 17:5.



### Verse 35

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:35:1)


**מַעֳמָד בַּמֶּרְכָּבָה.** מִתְחַזֵּק וְעוֹמֵד, כְּדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יַכִּירוּ יִשְׂרָאֵל בְּמַכָּתוֹ וְיִבְרְחוּ, וּתְחִלַּת נְפִילָה נִיסָה.


**Stood up in the chariot.** He exerted himself and stood so that the [Bnei] Yisroel would not become aware of his wound and flee, for flight is the beginning of defeat.



### Verse 38

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:38:1)


**וַיָּלֹקּוּ הַכְּלָבִים אֶת דָּמוֹ.** וְאוֹמֵר אֲנִי, שֶׁשָּׁם נֶהֱרַג נָבוֹת, שֶׁהֲרֵי אָמַר הַנָּבִיא בַּמָּקוֹם אֲשֶׁר לָקְקוּ הַכְּלָבִים אֶת דַּם נָבוֹת וְגוֹ'.


**And the dogs licked his blood.** I say that Novos was killed there, for the prophet said, “In the place where the dogs licked the blood of Navos, etc.”¹⁸¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁸ Above 21:19.
¹⁹ But how is this possible, Navos was killed in Yizreel while Achov was killed in Shomron? Radak suggests that Yizreel was near Shomron and the water of the pool in Shomron flowed to Yizreel where dogs licked Achov’s blood which was mixed in the water.


###### Rashi on I Kings 22:38:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:38:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:38:2)


**וְהַזֹּנוֹת רָחָצוּ.** וּכְלֵי הַזַּיִן רָחֲצּוּ שָׁם מִן הַדָּם, כָּךְ תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ אַחְאָב מְצֻנָּן הָיָה, וְעָשְׂתָה לּוֹ אִיזֶבֶל דְּמוּת שְׁתֵּי זוֹנוֹת בְּמֶרְכַּבְתּוֹ.


**And they washed the weapons [there].** And they washed off blood from the weapons there, [Targum] Yonoson rendered it in this manner. But our Rabbis said, “Achov was a cold person, so Ezevel made for him the image of two prostitutes in his chariot.²⁰ ²¹

footnotes:
²⁰ Maseches Sanhedrin 39b and Rashi there.
²¹ Alternatively, “the prostitutes bathed there,” in the pool of Shomron, which was now mixed with Achov’s blood.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 39

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:39:1)


**וּבֵית הַשֵּׁן.** שֵׁן דְּפִיל.


**The ivory palace.** [Made of] elephant tooth [i.e., ivory].



### Verse 45

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:45:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:45:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:45:1)


**וַיַּשְׁלֵם יְהוֹשָׁפָט עִם מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל.** לְפִי שֶׁמִּלְחָמָה הָיְתָה בֵּין אָסָא אָבִיו וּבֵין בַּעְשָׁא מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל, הֻזְקַק לוֹמַר כֵּן.


**Yehoshofot made peace with the king of Yisroel.** Since there was war between Asa his father and Basha the king of Yisroel, it was necessary to say this.



### Verse 47

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:47:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:47:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:47:1)


**וְיֶתֶר הַקָּדֵשׁ.** נוֹהֲגִין נִאוּף בְּפַרְהֶסְיָא.


**The remainder of the prostitution.** Who acted lasciviously in public.



### Verse 48

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:48:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:48:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:48:1)


**וּמֶלֶךְ אֵין בֶּאֱדוֹם.** בִּימֵי דָוִד, הַנָּצִיב הוּא הַמֶּלֶךְ, וְכָאן נִכְתַּב, לְפִי שֶׁבִּימֵי יְהוֹרָם בְּנוֹ שֶׁקִּלְקֵל, בְּיָמָיו פָּשַׁע אֱדוֹם וַיַּמְלִיכוּ לָהֶם מֶלֶךְ, אֲבָל בִּימֵי יְהוֹשָׁפָט עֲדַיִן הָיוּ כְּפוּפִין לוֹ.


**There was no king in Edom.** [Beginning with] the time of Dovid, the governor²² was the king. This was written here because in the days of Yehorom his son who sinned, in his days Edom rebelled and crowned a king for themselves, but in the time of Yehoshofot they were still subordinate to him.

footnotes:
²² I.e., the governor that was appointed by the king of Yehudah.—Radak



### Verse 49

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:49:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:49:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:49:1)


**כִּי נִשְׁבְּרוּ אֳנִיּוֹת.** לְפִי שֶׁנִּתְחַבֵּר עִם אֲחַזְיָהוּ בָּזֹאת, לְכָךְ נִשְׁבְּרוּ. וְכֵן אָמַר לוֹ הַנָּבִיא בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים: בְּהִתְחַבֶּרְךָ עִם אֲחַזְיָהוּ פָּרַץ ה' אֶת מַעֲשֶׂיךָ.


**For they [the ships] were broken.** Since he joined Achazyahu in this [undertaking], therefore, they were broken. And so did the prophet say to him in Divrei Hayomim, “Because you have allied yourself with a Achazyahu, Adonoy has wrecked your undertakings.”²³

footnotes:
²³ II Divrei Hayomim 20:37.



### Verse 52

###### Rashi on I Kings 22:52:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:52:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Kings/r/22:52:1)


**בִּשְׁנַת שְׁבַע עֶשְׂרֵה לִיהוֹשָׁפָט.** יֵשׁ מַתְמִיהִים עַל הַמִּקְרָא הַזֶּה, לְפִי שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בְּאַחְאָב שֶׁמָּלַךְ עֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁתַּיִם, וּבִיהוֹשָׁפָט נֶאֱמַר שֶׁעָמַד בִּשְׁנַת אַרְבַּע לְאַחְאָב, נִמְצָא שֶׁכָּלוּ יְמֵי אַחְאָב בִּשְׁנַת תְּשַׁע עֶשְׂרֵה לִיהוֹשָׁפָט, וְכָאן הוּא אוֹמֵר שֶׁמָּלַךְ אֲחַזְיָה בִּשְׁנַת שְׁבַע עֶשְׂרֵה לִיהוֹשָׁפָט. אַךְ בָּדַקְתִּי וּמָצָאתִי בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים לְמַלְכֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל שֶׁאֵינוֹ מְדַקְדֵּק בְּמִנְיַן שְׁנוֹתֵיהֶם, וְאִם עָמַד בְּסוֹף שָׁנָה, מוֹנֶה אוֹתָהּ לוֹ בִּתְחִלָּתוֹ, וְאֵינוֹ מוֹנֶה אוֹתָהּ לוֹ בְּמָקוֹם אַחֵר, לְכָךְ אֲנִי אוֹמֵר, בִּשְׁנַת אַרְבַּע לְאַחְאָב שֶׁאָמַר בַּעֲמִידָתוֹ שֶׁל יְהוֹשָׁפָט, לֹא נִמְנֵית עִמּוֹ שָׁנָה רִאשׁוֹנָה שֶׁל אַחְאָב, וְכֵן אַתָּה מוֹצֵא בְּרֻבָּם, וְגַם שָׁנָה שֶׁעָמַד יְהוֹשָׁפָט, לֹא נִמְנֵית בִּשְׁנַת שְׁבַע עֶשְׂרֵה הַלָּלוּ.


**In the seventeenth year of Yehoshofot.** Some wonder about this verse, because it is stated concerning Achov that he reigned twenty-two [years],²⁴ and concerning Yehoshofot it is stated, that he became king “in the fourth year of Achov,”²⁵ thus resulting that Achov’s [rule] ended in the nineteenth year of Yehoshofot[’s rule], but here it states that Achazyahu became king in the seventeenth year of Yehoshofot[’s rule]. However, I searched and found in Divrei Hayomim concerning the kings of Yisroel, that [Scripture] is not exact in the number of their years, and if one became king at the end of a year, he counts it at the beginning, but he does not count it elsewhere. Therefore I say that, “in the fourth year of Achov,” that is mentioned in reference to when Yehoshofot became king, does not count with it Achov’s first year [of kingship]. And so you find in the majority [of cases]. Also, the year in which Yehoshofot became king is not included in these seventeen years.

footnotes:
²⁴ Above 16:29.
²⁵ Above v. 41.
